# CRM overhaul — interface registry

Status: **proposed, design only — pending owner review.** No code is changed by this document.
Companion to the programme contract `2026-10-07-crm-overhaul-programme-design.md` (referred to below as
"the programme"; its sections are cited as P§n, its owner decisions as D-n, its facts as P§3 F1–F11).

Base: `main` @ `caa7f112`; every path:line below was re-read at working-tree `HEAD` `3bc7f6ca`, where the CRM
paths are identical. Alembic heads today: deal `e94d7a2c5f18`, customer `e230c025337e`, lead `91dd3ca663fd`
(re-verified from the `revision`/`down_revision` pairs in each `migrations/versions`).

**Reconciled with the four sub-project specs.** The registry amendments that SP1 (§9.1), SP2 (§16), SP3 (§16) and
SP4 (§16) proposed are folded into the rows below, and §J.2 lists each one with what it changed or why it was
rejected. Those four sections are superseded by this file; the handle map from their proposed handles to the final
ones is in §J.2, and every row handle below is final.

## 0. How to read and use this registry

**What it is.** The four sub-project specs and plans (SP1 foundations and cleanup, SP2 platform backbone, SP3 core
revamp, SP4 suite connection) are written in parallel by different authors. This file is the **single binding source
of names** for every interface that crosses a sub-project boundary: platform-core API, schema, HTTP routes, events,
permissions and roles, settings and flags, shared UI, `tools/` commands and documents.

**Binding rule.** A name, path, code, column, event type, flag or file name that appears here is the only one a spec or
plan may use for that interface. A spec that needs a different name amends this file in the same review; it does not
rename locally. A name used inside one sub-project only and not listed here is that sub-project's to choose.

**Row columns.** Every row carries: name, kind, **owner** (the SP/release that creates or changes it),
**consumers** (the SP/releases that read or call it), **status** and the **key shape**. Owner and consumer cells read
`SPn/Rx.y`; where the same SP owns and consumes, only the release is repeated.

**Handles.** A row handle is a letter and a number (`A1`, `B7`, `C45`). Event rows in §D are **`EV1`-`EV55`**, never
`D<n>`: the programme's owner decisions are written `D-1` to `D-24`, the ones this registry and the specs raised afterwards
are `D-25` to `D-34` (all in §K), and `EV16` must not be read as `D-16`. Settings rows in §F are `F1`-`F20`, so the
programme's facts are always cited as `P§3 F1`-`F11`, never bare. Wiring rows in §F.1 are `W1`-`W52`. Release numbers are
`R<sub-project>.<n>`; a suffix names one deploy of a release where its owning spec splits it (`R1.2a`, `R3.7b`, `R4.3-S`,
`R4.5-C`, `R4.6-A`), and a row owned by `Rx.y` with no suffix belongs to every deploy of it. A handle is never reused or
renumbered once a plan can cite it: a row that is withdrawn stays, with status `withdrawn`. Handles that a spec
proposed with a prefix (`SP3-B62`) or from a 100-series placeholder (`SP3-C100`) have final numbers; §J.2 maps them.

**Status words.**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| exists | present at HEAD; the key shape cites `path:line` |
| changed | present at HEAD and altered by the owning release; the key shape says how, additive unless stated |
| new | not present at HEAD |

**Provenance words.** `(pending D-n)`: the programme §8 decision is open; the shape is the programme's recommended
default and moves only when the owner answers. Every row that cites a D-n is such a default, and §K lists D-1 to D-24 and the ten that this registry and the specs raised (D-25 to D-34). `(registered default)`: discovery fixed no name or shape, so this
registry chose one; the owning SP may change it only by amending this file. `inference`: not observed in code.
Skeptic corrections in the discovery digests override mapper claims; where a digest and the code disagreed, the code
at HEAD won and the row says so.

**Citations.** Line numbers in `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/auth.py` will shift when `fix/ci-failures`
(which edits that file) merges; build against symbols, not lines. Every other citation is stable at `3bc7f6ca`, except that a path a row marks `new` (a module, test, tool or document that a release creates) does not exist at HEAD and is named so the plan can create it.
For brevity a citation that starts with a service name (`customer/app/...`, `deal/...`, `lead/...`, `account/...`,
`finance/...`, `operations/...`, `notification/...`, `support/...`, `project/...`, `projects/...`, `leave/...`,
`approval/...`, `scheduler/...`, `app-gateway/...`) is under `services/`; `product-*/...` and `opsgrids-*/...` are under
`frontend/`; `auth.py`, `events.py`, `app.py` and `gateway.py` are under `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/`;
`compose.prod.yml`, `remote.sh`, `RUNBOOK.md` and `memory-budget.json` are under `infrastructure/edge/`; bare doc names
(`architecture.md`, `features.md`, `plan.md`, `ecosystem-gaps.md`) are under `docs/`. Every one resolves at HEAD.

**Amendments.** Where this registry resolves a conflict between the release lists, the programme and the digests, it
is recorded in §J and returned to the orchestrator. §J.2 lists the amendments of the four sub-project specs, each as
folded or rejected. Where two specs touched the same row, the sub-project that owns the interface decided it (its
owner cell), and §J says so. Questions a name cannot settle are in §L.

## 1. The naming grammar this registry follows

Derived from the code, not invented. A new name that breaks one of these rows is a registry bug.

| Surface | Rule | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Permission code | `crm.<module>.<resource>.<action>`; module is the owning service's slice (`book` = customer, `pipeline` = deal, `lead` = lead); resource and action are lower snake (`read_sensitive`, `purchase_order`); a code has exactly one owning service | `services/{customer,deal,lead}/app/catalogue.py` (`PermissionSpec.code`: customer/deal `:46-47`, lead `:55-56`); exporter refuses a duplicate code `tools/export_permissions.py:163` |
| Role template | UPPER_SNAKE `CRM_*`; same-code templates declared by several services are merged by unioning grants (wider scope wins); `name`, `is_default`, `cross_product` must agree across services or the exporter exits | `tools/export_permissions.py:116-137, 140-188` |
| Event type | `<product>.<module>.<resource>.<past-tense verb>`, or, where the fact is a state reached rather than an act, the repo's existing state-phrase form (here `due`, `stale`, `overdue`); CRM lead events are three-part (`crm.lead.created`); platform events use their own product (`core.*`, `platform.*`); scheduler targets end `_requested` | `libs/contracts/events/` (77 directories); `services/lead/app/services.py:71-77`; `services/deal/app/services.py:43-47`; `services/operations/app/fulfilment/services.py:93`; state phrases `operations.inventory.item.low_stock` (`services/operations/app/alerts/services.py:34`) and `commerce.subscription.payment_retry_due` (`services/payment/app/consumers.py:84`) |
| Event schema | `libs/contracts/events/<type>/v1.schema.json`, `$id opsgrids://contracts/<type>/v1`, `additionalProperties: false`; additive within v1; payload and schema change in one commit, proven by `platform_core.events.validate_event_schema(event_type, version, payload, *, contracts_root)` | `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/events.py:203-224`; `libs/contracts/tests/test_event_schemas.py:38-107` |
| Public route | under `/api/v1` (mounted by `create_app`); kebab-case static segments; the word after a colon is always a verb or verb phrase (`/leads/{lead_id}:convert`, `/contacts/{contact_id}/phone:reveal`, `/contacts:lookup-channels`), never a noun: a noun is a static path (`GET /deals/summary`, `GET /deals/follow-ups/summary`); static paths declared before `/{id}` paths | `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/app.py:363`; `services/lead/app/api/routes.py:239,273`; `services/customer/app/api/routes.py:132-134` |
| Internal route | mounted at the root, `/internal/v1/organizations/{organization_id}/...`, `include_in_schema=False`, service token addressed to the callee, per-route caller allow-list (`_require_caller`) and org-claim check (`_require_org_claim`); notification's claim-only `messaging-summary` is the one deliberate exception, and customer's own `messaging-target` and `contacts:resolve` are the two legacy routes that have neither check until C45 and C46 harden them | `services/customer/app/api/internal.py:69-121, 138-241`; `services/notification/app/api/c1_internal.py:36-49` |
| List query | `page` (>=1), `size` (1-200), `q`, `status`, `owner_id`, `company_id`; response `Page[T]` = `items,total,page,size,pages` | `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/pagination.py:23-52`; `services/customer/app/api/routes.py:142-153` |
| Table and column | snake_case plural tables; every business table is `TenantBase` (`id`, `organization_id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `created_by`, `updated_by`, `deleted_at`); money `BIGINT *_minor` + ISO currency; index names `ix_<table>_<cols>`, `uq_<table>_<cols>`; every index leads with `organization_id`; `ENABLE` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, policy `tenant_isolation` on `<schema>.current_org()` | `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/models.py:17-23,87`; `services/deal/migrations/versions/e94d7a2c5f18_enable_row_level_security.py:41-86` |
| Settings table | `<domain>_settings`, one row per organization, lazily created (`leave_settings`, `fulfilment_settings`); CRM's are `deal.pipeline_settings` (matches `crm.pipeline.settings.write`) and `lead.lead_settings` | `services/leave/app/models.py:110`; `services/operations/app/models.py:1800` |
| Rate stored as basis points | `*_bps INTEGER` (1 bps = 0.01 %, so `1800` is 18 %); never a float. Finance receives `tax_rate_pct = bps / 100` as `NUMERIC(5,2)`, which is exact at two decimals; a rate is not money, so ADR-0000 §8's ban on `Numeric` amounts is not involved. The suffix is `_bps` (not `_bp`) because billing already uses it | `services/billing/app/domain.py:201-205` (`rate_bps`), `services/billing/migrations/versions/fe5ef84271ed_initial_billing_schema.py:131`; finance side `services/finance/app/models.py:186` (`tax_rate_pct`, `Numeric(5, 2)`) |
| Campaign link | `marketing_campaign_id` (a plain nullable UUID into `marketing`). `source_ref` is **not** used by any CRM table: it is the approval engine's request key (`(organization_id, request_type, source_ref)`) and finance's portal reference | `services/approval/app/models.py:263`; `services/attendance/app/consumers.py:155`; `services/finance/app/models.py:578-589` |
| Party-link column (outside CRM) | `crm_company_id`, `crm_contact_id` (plain nullable indexed UUIDs, no FK) beside a display snapshot; never `customer_id` | P§4.2 |
| Party-link field (events) | `crm_company_id`, `crm_contact_id`, `owner_membership_id`, ids only, on every enriched event including CRM's own | P§4.4 |
| Inside a CRM schema | existing `company_id`, `contact_id`, `deal_id`, `owner_id` columns keep their names (frozen); new CRM tables reuse them | `services/deal/app/models.py:66,68,123-124` |
| Setting and env | snake_case attribute on `Settings(ServiceSettings)` is the env var upper-cased, no prefix, `case_sensitive=False`; compose-wide defaults live in the `x-service-env` anchor and unknown variables are ignored by services without the field | `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/config.py:48-72`; `infrastructure/edge/compose.prod.yml:57-94` |
| Queue | `<service>.events` (and `<queue>.dlq`); one shared DLX | `services/customer/app/consumers.py:27`; `services/deal/app/consumers.py:15` |
| Tool | `tools/<verb>_<noun>.py`; owner-run production tools default to a read-only dry run and write only under `--apply`, with `--confirm-count`, `--i-took-a-backup`, `--i-know-this-is-the-live-database` gates (the purge tools' gating, not the reconcile tools' opt-in `--dry-run`, which writes when omitted) | `tools/purge_test_orgs.py:1892-1910`; `tools/reconcile_membership_links.py:453-457` |

## A. platform-core API

### A.1 The membership subject (SP2/R2.2, flipped by SP2/R2.7)

Exactly as it should read against `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/auth.py`. The resolver registry is write-only
today (`_resolvers` at `auth.py:297` is written at `auth.py:300-301` and read nowhere), and `apply` and
`assert_can_touch` hard-wire `principal.subject_ref` (`auth.py:337-350, 372-376`), which is only ever an HR employee
id. The `mem` claim, `X-Membership-Id` header and `Principal.membership_id` already exist, so no token or gateway
change is needed.

| # | Name | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Key shape and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | `OwnerSubject` | `enum.StrEnum` in `platform_core.auth` (in `auth.__all__`; re-exported at `platform_core` root beside `Scope`) | SP2/R2.2 | SP2/R2.7 (CRM); outside this programme: ITSM NEW-8, projects C14 | new | `EMPLOYEE_LINK = "employee_link"` (today's behaviour, the default everywhere) and `MEMBERSHIP = "membership"`. No symbol like it exists on any ref (discovery `git log --all -S`) |
| A2 | `register_scope_resolver(product, factory, *, owner_subject=OwnerSubject.EMPLOYEE_LINK)` | function | SP2/R2.2 | SP2/R2.7 | changed | Today `(product: str, factory: Callable[[], ScopeResolver]) -> None` (`auth.py:300`). The new third parameter is keyword-only with the default above, so the 16 existing `scope.register()` call sites are byte-for-byte unchanged. The `ScopeResolver` Protocol (`auth.py:284-294`: `owner_column`, `team_column`, `location_column`) is **not** changed: a new required member breaks strict mypy in 16 services. The call runs at import in each service's routes module and must stay idempotent: re-registering a product with the **same** subject is a no-op, and re-registering it with a **different** subject raises `ValueError` (a silent flip of the scope subject is a scope defect; tests use A4 to reset). `register_scope_resolver` is in `platform_core.auth.__all__` but is **not** exported at the `platform_core` root (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/__init__.py` imports `Scope`, `ScopeContext` and the others by name, not this one), so every caller keeps `from platform_core.auth import register_scope_resolver` (`services/customer/app/scope.py:26`); only `OwnerSubject` joins the root (A1) |
| A3 | `owner_subject_for(permission)` | function `(permission: str) -> OwnerSubject` | SP2/R2.2 | `require()` (SP2/R2.2) | new | Product = text before the first `.` of the permission. No entry returns `EMPLOYEE_LINK`. A prefix miss is safe by construction: notification registers `notification` while 34 of its 39 `require(...)` call sites (`services/notification/app/api/*.py`) name an `engage.*` code (25 distinct codes in `libs/contracts/permissions/engage.json`, beside 5 `notification.*` codes in `notification.json`), so a prefix lookup misses them; `core` is shared by account (membership-keyed) and approval (employee-keyed) and has no resolver. Neither opts in under this programme |
| A4 | `reset_scope_resolvers()` | test hook | SP2/R2.2 | tests everywhere | new | Beside `reset_jwks_cache` (`auth.py:132`); empties `_resolvers` so import order and test pollution cannot decide enforcement |
| A5 | `ScopeContext.owner_subject` | pydantic field, default `OwnerSubject.EMPLOYEE_LINK` | SP2/R2.2 | SP2/R2.7 | changed | `require()` snapshots `owner_subject_for(permission)` when it builds the context (`auth.py:414`, `return ScopeContext(principal=..., scope=..., permission=...)`). Hand-built and synthetic contexts (`services/customer/app/services.py:141` `service_ctx`, `:164` `lead_conversion_ctx`, `services/deal/app/services.py` `lead_conversion_ctx`) take the default; they are ORG scope, which short-circuits before any subject is read |
| A6 | `ScopeContext.own_ref` | read-only property `-> uuid.UUID \| None` | SP2/R2.2 | SP2/R2.7, SP3/R3.8 | new | `principal.membership_id` when `owner_subject is MEMBERSHIP`, else `principal.subject_ref`. The one accessor for "who am I" in OWN and TEAM comparisons, **and** the replacement for every raw CRM read of `ctx.subject_ref` (six code sites at HEAD, none in lead: deal default owner `deal/app/api/routes.py:112`, `owner=me` `:163,175`, activity forcing `:241,243`; customer `_assert_owner_assignable` `customer/app/services.py:205`). The count is smaller than the discovery digests' "7-8" because they counted docstrings |
| A7 | `ScopeContext.subject_ref` | property | SP2/R2.2 (the three CRM docstrings: SP2/R2.7) | HR code (attendance, leave, approval, employee, operations workers) | exists | `auth.py:317-319`; keeps meaning the raw employee link. Docstring-only changes: module docstring `auth.py:3-8`, `Principal.subject_ref` comment `auth.py:69`, `ScopeResolver` docstring `auth.py:285-290`, `docs/architecture.md:261,304`, and the "their rep id is `subject_ref`" line in each `scope.py` module docstring (`services/{customer,deal,lead}/app/scope.py:7`). The three CRM docstrings change in **R2.7**, not R2.2: until the flip the sentence is true |
| A8 | `ScopeContext.apply(stmt, *, owner_col, team_col=None, location_col=None)` | method | SP2/R2.2 | every CRM selector; SP3/R3.8 | changed | Signature unchanged. OWN: `owner_col == own_ref`. TEAM: `team_col == own_ref`. Both keep `where(sa_false())` when `own_ref is None` (`auth.py:337-340, 345-349`; never `col == NULL`). ORG short-circuits first; LOCATION untouched. **Registered default (pending D-24):** for `MEMBERSHIP` only, TEAM is `or_(owner_col == own_ref, team_col == own_ref)` so a manager's own rows stay inside their team scope; the `EMPLOYEE_LINK` TEAM path stays exactly as today |
| A9 | `ScopeContext.assert_can_touch(*, owner_id, team_owner_id=None, location_code=None)` | method | SP2/R2.2 | every CRM detail and mutate route | changed | Signature unchanged. OWN: `owner_id == own_ref`; TEAM: `team_owner_id == own_ref`, and for `MEMBERSHIP` also `owner_id == own_ref` (mirrors A8); the `is not None` guards at `auth.py:373,375` are retained |
| A10 | `Principal.membership_id` | field | — | A6 | exists | `auth.py:68`; filled from `mem` (`auth.py:182`) and `X-Membership-Id` (`auth.py:248`); minted at `services/auth/app/tokens.py:108-109`; forwarded by `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/gateway.py:666`. No change |
| A11 | `tests/infra/test_no_raw_subject_ref.py` | static AST guard | SP2/R2.2 | SP2/R2.7 | new | AST match on `subject_ref` on **any** object (not only `ctx`, `ctx.principal` or `principal`) and on its string spellings (`"subject_ref"`, `"sref"`, `"X-Subject-Ref"`, `getattr`, subscripts), in the service directories on an allowlist of opted-in services. Inside an opted-in service it also requires every hand-built `ScopeContext` to be `Scope.ORG` and the first argument of every `require(...)` to resolve to a `crm.` permission (a bare name is resolved through the file's module-level string constants, which is how the two reveal guards pass theirs: `REVEAL_PERMISSION`, `services/customer/app/api/routes.py:72,80` and `services/lead/app/api/routes.py:70,78`; an argument it cannot resolve is itself a finding), and the opted-in set is tied to the services that register `OwnerSubject.MEMBERSHIP` (A13). Exactly one string spelling is allow-listed, by file **and** constant name: `"X-Subject-Ref"` in `_IDENTITY_HEADERS` (`services/customer/app/c1_360.py:77,83`), which the 360 keeps forwarding to upstreams that may still key OWN on the employee link. R2.2 ships it with an **empty** opted-in set (no service switches in R2.2) that **ratchets** the six known raw reads (A6) and that one spelling, so a seventh read cannot land before R2.7 (an empty set scans nothing); R2.7 adds `services/customer`, `services/deal`, `services/lead` and the ratchet empties. Modelled on `tests/infra/test_io_outside_transaction.py`. A text grep is wrong: docstrings and `libs/contracts/openapi/deal.json:1166,1208` mention the word |
| A12 | `libs/platform-core/tests/test_scope_subject.py` | unit tests | SP2/R2.2 | — | new | registry round trip; `require()` snapshots the subject; unregistered and prefix-miss default to employee link; `reset_scope_resolvers`; `Principal` mapping of `mem` and `sref` from claims and from forwarded headers; hand-built context defaults. `test_auth_scope.py` gains membership-subject cases for OWN and TEAM and the no-subject fail-closed case. "A membership id from another organization matches nothing" is **not** provable here: `apply` adds no organization predicate (the selector does), so R2.2 pins the bound parameter instead (only the caller's own membership id is compared) and R2.7 proves the cross-organization case against real rows. A **frozen oracle** proves the employee-link paths unchanged: verbatim copies of the pre-change `apply` and `assert_can_touch` bodies are compared with the new methods over the whole grid of scope, subject, membership, column presence and owner inputs |
| A13 | CRM resolver registrations | call sites | SP2/R2.7 | — | changed | `register_scope_resolver(PRODUCT, <Resolver>, owner_subject=OwnerSubject.MEMBERSHIP)` in `services/customer/app/scope.py:47`, `services/deal/app/scope.py:48`, `services/lead/app/scope.py:44`, **all three in one release** (a deploy skew fails closed: OWN matches nothing, it never widens). Each service's `tests/helpers.py` `ctx_for` (customer/lead `:134`, deal `:40`) takes the subject as a parameter |
| A14 | CRM TEAM wiring | call sites and resolver methods | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.8 | changed | `ScopeContext.apply` never consults the registered resolver's columns: every selector passes its own (`customer/app/selectors.py:152,262`, `deal/app/selectors.py:50,122`, `lead/app/selectors.py:39`, each `owner_col=` only). R3.8 adds `team_col=<Model>.owner_manager_id` to those five calls and `team_owner_id=<row>.owner_manager_id` to the `assert_can_touch` calls (22 across the three services: customer 9, deal 7, lead 6. **Eighteen** check a row the caller reads or changes and pass its stamp. **Four** validate an owner the caller is assigning, where there is no row to read a stamp from (deal `services.py:209` and `api/routes.py:115`, lead `services.py:449` and `:592`), and pass `team_owner_id=await services.manager_of(session, organization_id, owner_id)`, a primary-key read of the service's own `owner_team` that answers `None`, and so fails closed, for an unknown owner; customer's `_assert_owner_assignable` and the target owner of C37's bulk reassign take the same lookup), and makes each resolver's `team_column()` return the same column (`customer/app/scope.py:38-39`, `deal/app/scope.py:39-40`, `lead/app/scope.py:35-36`) so the registered resolver stays an honest description; `owner_column()` unchanged. Each service's `owner_team` (B4) is fed by that service's own consumer, so R3.8 ships lead's fold (F9) with it: without it TEAM on leads would match nothing. A static guard, `tests/infra/test_team_wiring.py` (F14), walks the three services and fails on any `ctx.apply(` without `team_col=`, any `assert_can_touch(` without `team_owner_id=`, and any `team_owner_id=None` or other constant, so a call added later cannot silently leave TEAM unsupported |

### A.2 Other platform-core and shared-library interfaces

| # | Name | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Key shape and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A15 | `platform_core.gst` (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/gst.py`) | module | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.1 (customer), SP4/R4.3 (finance) | new | Extracts only the pure GSTIN code that lives today in `services/finance/app/domain.py:380-480`: `Gstin` (frozen dataclass: `value, state_code, pan, entity_code, check_digit`, `state_name`), `GstinError`, `GST_STATE_CODES`, `gstin_check_digit(base)`, `normalise_gstin(raw)`, `parse_gstin(raw)`, `is_valid_gstin(raw)`. Finance re-exports the same names so its tests are untouched. The register's O6 (`docs/adr/open-questions-register.md:38`) plans a wider `platform_core.gst`; R3.1 takes only this subset. A platform-core change reruns every service's tests |
| A16 | `cancel_scheduled_event(session, outbox_cls, *, organization_id, idempotency_key, aggregate_type, aggregate_id, causation_id=None) -> uuid.UUID` and `PLATFORM_SCHEDULE_CANCEL_REQUESTED = "platform.schedule.cancel_requested"` | function and constant in `platform_core.events` | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6 (follow-ups), SP4/R4.5 (quote expiry) | new | Beside `PLATFORM_SCHEDULE_REQUESTED` (`events.py:44`) and `schedule_event` (`events.py:120`). Stages the cancel on the caller's own outbox, same transaction. `aggregate_type` and `aggregate_id` are required because `publish_event` writes them to NOT NULL outbox columns (`events.py:58-59, 84`) and a cancel has no target payload to take them from: the caller passes the pair it scheduled with (`schedule_event`'s `target_aggregate_type`, `target_aggregate_id`; for a follow-up, `'follow_up'` and the follow-up id). Joins `OutboxRelay._GUARANTEED_ROUTING_EVENT_TYPES` (`events.py:260`) so it is never dropped for want of a bound queue. Scheduler (`services/scheduler/app/consumers.py`) binds it and marks the job `cancelled` (job states today `pending`, `fired`, `parked`: `services/scheduler/app/models.py:38-40`). **Ownership:** a cancel carries only an `(organization_id, idempotency_key)`, so without a check any producer could cancel another service's job (finance's filing is the obvious victim). The scheduler therefore honours a cancel only when the **stored job's** `target_event_type` passes the same producer-prefix rule as scheduling (A17); otherwise it logs `scheduler.schedule_cancel.rejected` (ids only) and drops it, mirroring `scheduler.schedule_requested.rejected` (`services/scheduler/app/consumers.py:83-111`). A cancel for an unknown key or one whose job is no longer `pending` is a no-op with a debug log (`scheduler.schedule_cancel.noop`). **A key is single-use for the retention window**: a cancelled job keeps its row (status `cancelled`, `cancelled_at`, B64) and its key, and ingest stays `ON CONFLICT (organization_id, idempotency_key) DO NOTHING`, so a repeated request for a used key creates no job; a producer must never re-request a used key and re-arms with a fresh one (follow-ups append an `arm_seq`, B25; a quote's expiry key is used once per quote row, EV28) |
| A17 | Scheduler ownership check | behaviour of `_rejection_reason` (`services/scheduler/app/consumers.py:83-111`) | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6, SP4/R4.5 | changed | Today `target_event_type` must start with `envelope["producer"] + "."` (producer is the **service** name, `services/deal/app/relay.py:38`), so `deal` cannot schedule `crm.*`. **Registered default, made concrete by SP3/R3.6:** accept the service-name prefix **or** a prefix from a static producer map in the scheduler. A new pure module `services/scheduler/app/domain.py` (the service has no such module today) holds `PRODUCER_PREFIXES: Mapping[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {"deal": ("deal.", "crm.pipeline.")}` and `producer_may_own(producer, event_type)`, which accepts `f"{producer}."` or any mapped prefix; `_rejection_reason` and the cancel handler (A16) both call it. Keeps the control narrow (module-level prefixes, never `crm.`, so `deal` cannot schedule `crm.lead.*` or `crm.book.*`), and the same map governs cancels (A16). Fallback if R3.6 leaves the rule alone: use `deal.pipeline.*` for every scheduled target in §D. **Deliberately not part of this change, and owner-visible:** a `notification` entry (`notification.` and `engage.`) would also make Engage's own scheduled sends pass: they schedule `engage.campaign.campaign.send_requested` (`services/notification/app/services.py:4803`, armed at `:5126`) from producer `notification`, which the current rule (inference, L8) rejects. That is a behaviour change to a product outside this programme (a campaign scheduled for later, which if the inference holds never fires today, would start sending on schedule), so it ships, if at all, as its own change with its own accepted/rejected test pair and a note to the owner, and nothing in the CRM design depends on it |
| A18 | Scheduler profile | compose/budget change | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6 | changed | `scheduler` runs only under the `finance` profile today (`memory-budget.json` `containers.scheduler.profile = "finance"`); R3.6 moves it to core (`profile: null`) and `scheduler.events` moves from `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES["finance"]` to `CORE_DLQ_QUEUES` (§F) |
| A19 | `crm` product tokens | none | — | — | exists | No new claim, header or audience: `mem`/`sref` already travel (A10), `amr` step-up marker stays `stepup` (`services/customer/app/api/routes.py:79-80`) |
| A20 | `platform_core.listing` (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/listing.py`) | module | SP3/R3.2 (extended in SP3/R3.8) | SP3/R3.2 and R3.8 (customer, deal, lead) | new | The pure helpers behind the list vocabulary (C35), so three services behave byte-identically (a copy in each would drift): R3.2 `escape_like`, `parse_sort`, `apply_sort`, `parse_ids`, `parse_tz`, `date_bounds`; R3.8 adds `normalise_tags` and `validate_view_filters` (B13, B14, C16). No such module exists at HEAD. A platform-core change reruns every service's tests |
| A21 | `platform_core.errors` documentation | additions to `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/errors.py` | SP3/R3.2 | every CRM service (SP3/R3.2 to R3.8); the SPA | new | `FieldError {field, code, message, existing_id?}`, `ProblemDocument` and `problem_responses(*statuses)`, so OpenAPI documents the problem shape the handlers really return (today `errors.py` defines `ProblemError` and `register_error_handlers`, and no OpenAPI document declares a problem response: `libs/contracts/openapi/customer.json` has no `application/problem+json`), and the `errors[].code` catalogue of SP3 §6.3.3 including 400 `invalid_capture_request` (C41). `existing_id` is the one extra key a duplicate (409) carries so a screen can say "Open the existing {noun}"; the client ignores keys it does not type (`frontend/opsgrids-client/src/problem.ts:1-20`) |
| A22 | `libs/contracts/vectors/conversion_ids.v1.json` | shared data file, read only by tests (the directory `vectors/` is new beside `events/`, `openapi/` and `permissions/`) | SP2/R2.4 | tests of lead, deal and customer (SP2/R2.4) | new | The uuid5 namespace, the name templates and 24 golden vectors `(lead_id, generation) -> (company_id, contact_id, deal_id)` that hold the three copies of `conversion_ids` equal: lead's `domain.conversion_ids` (`services/lead/app/domain.py:118-145`, used by EV13's consumers) and the copies that deal and customer gain in R2.4. `import app` is ambiguous across services and platform-core is not the home of a CRM algorithm, so each service keeps its own copy and asserts all 24 vectors in its own tests; the vectors are polyglot data like the event schemas, and no service reads the file at runtime |

## B. Schema

Rules for **every** new table below (stated once): `organization_id UUID NOT NULL` indexed and leading every index;
`TenantBase` columns unless the row says "composite PK" or "append-only"; `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` with
the `tenant_isolation` policy (`USING` and `WITH CHECK` on `<schema>.current_org()`) in the **same** migration, and the
table added to the service's `TENANT_TABLES` (deal: `services/deal/migrations/versions/e94d7a2c5f18_enable_row_level_security.py:41-46`,
`services/deal/tests/conftest.py:45-51`); no foreign key leaves the schema (links to other schemas are plain UUIDs); migrations are
expand-only and rollback-tolerant, one Alembic head per service, `make migration-roundtrip` and
`tests/infra/test_migration_revisions.py` green. A table without `organization_id` (and no foreign key into one) is not
reached by `tools/purge_test_orgs.py` and changes the unreached list that `tests/infra/test_purge_test_orgs_db.py` pins
(`docs/adr/0036-removing-test-organisations.md:126-129`). A delete-blocking trigger is **not** a problem: the tool discovers
every `DELETE` trigger from `pg_trigger` and lifts it by name inside each organisation's own transaction
(`tools/purge_test_orgs.py:62-72`, ADR-0036 decision 6, `docs/adr/0036-removing-test-organisations.md:82-86`), so the
append-only trigger pattern this registry requires for `party_timeline` and `deal.activity_attachments` needs no purge-side change.

### B.1 `customer` schema (owners per row; the chain starts at `e230c025337e`)

Chain order is merge order: `e230c025337e` → R2.6 (`owners.membership_id`, `owner_rekey_journal`) → R3.1 (tax ids, addresses, contact email, `email_index`) → R3.8 (`owner_team`, `owner_manager_id`, `tags`, `saved_views`) → R4.1 (`erased_at`, `customer.scrub_contact_audit`) → R4.3 (`party_documents`, `party_payments`, `party_rollup`, `party_timeline` with its conversation-day index, `lifecycle_promoted_at`, `customer_settings`; the sweep-discovery grant is in `migrations/env.py`, not in a revision) → R4.7 (`owner_team.employee_id`, where R3.8 did not add it, B71); R2.9's optional sealing is a separate revision.

| # | Name | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Key shape and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | `customer.owners.membership_id` | column `UUID NULL` | SP2/R2.6 | SP2/R2.7, SP3/R3.4 (Team), SP3/R3.8, SP4/R4.2, SP4/R4.7 | new | Plain UUID into `account.memberships.id`, no FK (pending D-7). Partial unique `uq_owners_org_membership (organization_id, membership_id) WHERE membership_id IS NOT NULL`. Written by Team activation (`POST /customers/owners` with `membership_id`, §C) and by `tools/map_crm_owners.py`. `owners.id` is **unchanged** (random uuid4 today: `Owner` `customer/app/models.py:65-95`) and stays a legacy key resolvable by name; it is not rekeyed (programme D-7, pending, chose a column, not a primary-key move) |
| B2 | `owner_rekey_journal` in each of `customer`, `deal`, `lead` | table, append-only (no `deleted_at`) | SP2/R2.6 | SP2/R2.6 (`tools/map_crm_owners.py verify` and `revert`) | new | `id UUID PK`, `organization_id`, `run_id UUID`, `table_name VARCHAR(40)`, `row_id UUID`, `old_owner_id UUID`, `new_owner_id UUID`, `evidence VARCHAR(12)` (`link`, `email`, `manual`), `applied_at TIMESTAMPTZ`; index `(organization_id, run_id)`. This is the **reversible mapping artifact** (P§R2.6): one row per **changed id** (a scalar owner column yields one row; an array column such as `assignment_rules.owner_ids` yields one per element), ids only, no PII; `table_name` carries a per-service CHECK listing that service's own owner-bearing tables. The reviewable plan file (roster emails, kept off the repo) is separate (§H). Same shape in all three schemas, one revision each |
| B3 | `owner_id` value semantics | data change, **no DDL, no rename** | SP2/R2.6 (data), SP2/R2.7 (cutover) | every CRM reader | changed | Columns `customer.companies.owner_id`, `customer.contacts.owner_id`, `deal.deals.owner_id`, `deal.activities.owner_id`, `lead.leads.owner_id` and the array `lead.assignment_rules.owner_ids` hold roster ids (`owners.id`) or, for deals and activities, employee ids today (`deal/app/api/routes.py:112,241-243`). After the mapping run they hold **membership ids**. Names are frozen (programme §4.5: codes and event types frozen). `deal.activities` is append-only by trigger (`e94d7a2c5f18:90-107`, raises for every role): the tool leaves it untouched (registered default, pending D-8), so `OwnerOut` must resolve a legacy id through `owners.id` **and** a current id through `owners.membership_id` |
| B4 | `owner_team` in each of `customer`, `deal`, `lead` | table, composite PK `(organization_id, membership_id)` | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.8, SP4/R4.2 (assignment), SP4/R4.7 (B71) | new | `manager_membership_id UUID NULL`, `status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL`, `source_occurred_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL` (the version: applied only if strictly newer, ADR-0020 §2 rule), CHECK `manager_not_self`; index `ix_owner_team_org_manager (organization_id, manager_membership_id, membership_id)`. Fed by the enriched `core.membership.*` events (EV29) through **each service's own consumer** on its own queue (EV43 customer, EV44 deal, EV45 lead). Lead has no consumer today (`services/lead/bin` holds `api`, `cron` and `relay` only, and `services/lead/app/config.py` has no `run_consumers_in_api`), so R3.8 ships the lead fold (F9) together with this table: the consumer deploys before account's EV29 enrichment, and without it `owner_team` stays empty and TEAM on leads matches nothing (fail closed). Pattern: `services/leave/app/models.py:116-165` (`EmployeeDirectory`, table `employee_directory`). Per-service copy: no shared database and no cross-schema read (pending D-24). The first population of a live organization is not an event replay (membership events cannot be replayed): the owner-run tool H10 seeds it and repairs it after any consumer outage. `customer.owner_team` and `lead.owner_team` gain `employee_id` for the HR exit path in R4.7 (B71) |
| B5 | `owner_manager_id` snapshot | column `UUID NULL` on `customer.companies`, `customer.contacts`, `deal.deals`, `deal.activities` (new rows only; trigger-safe), `deal.follow_ups`, `lead.leads` | SP3/R3.8 | A14 resolvers, SP3/R3.8 | new | Stamped from `owner_team` when the owner is set or changed; index `ix_<table>_org_owner_manager (organization_id, owner_manager_id)`. Snapshot semantics (stale after a re-org) mirror leave's `reporting_manager_id` snapshot (`leave/app/models.py:226-232,260`); one-level TEAM is a stated product limit |
| B6 | `customer.companies.legal_name` | column `VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.4, SP4/R4.1 (summary), SP4/R4.3 | new | `name` stays the unique display/trading name (`uq_companies_org_name` `customer/app/models.py:132-138`, case-sensitive, unchanged) |
| B7 | `customer.company_tax_ids` | table (TenantBase) | SP3/R3.1 | SP4/R4.1 (company summary), SP4/R4.3 (finance), SP3/R3.4 | new | `company_id UUID NOT NULL FK customer.companies.id ON DELETE CASCADE`, `kind VARCHAR(10) NOT NULL` with `CHECK (kind = 'gstin')` (the column stays so a later additive migration can widen the CHECK for another business registration number; a kind that is an individual's government identifier must take the sealed shape in L11), `value VARCHAR(15) NOT NULL` (the normalised GSTIN, verified by A15 `parse_gstin`), `state_code VARCHAR(2) NULL` (GST state code), `label VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, `is_primary BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`. Unique `uq_company_tax_ids_org_kind_value (organization_id, kind, value) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` (GSTIN is the preferred dedupe and link key); index `ix_company_tax_ids_org_company`. A company may hold several GSTINs (one per state registration). Exposed on `/companies/{id}/tax-ids` and the internal company summary; **never in events**. **No PAN is stored, in any form.** ADR-0000 "Also settled" (`docs/adr/0000-phase-0-decisions.md:171-174`) and the repo's root rules make government identifiers sealed, masked by default and revealed only through their own permission with an audit row every time (the repo's one stored PAN is sealed: `services/employee/app/models.py:208-209`, `pan_enc` and `pan_masked`). A plaintext PAN column would also have been written into `customer.audit_logs` unredacted, because `record_audit` redacts by **key name** only (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/audit.py:29-55, 106-115`: `pan` and `pan_enc` are listed, a value under a key such as `value` is not), and would then have come back through the history routes (C15, C32). The PAN is characters 3-12 of the GSTIN (A15 `Gstin.pan`, `services/finance/app/domain.py:468`), so a screen that needs it takes those characters from the GSTIN it already shows (A15's `Gstin.pan` is the one definition); it is never a column, a request or response field, an event field or an audit value. The GSTIN itself is a registration number that finance (`customers.gstin`, `invoices.buyer_gstin`: `services/finance/app/models.py:100, 207`) and storefront (`enquiries.gstin`: `services/storefront/app/models.py:806`) already hold in the clear. A standalone PAN (a buyer with no GSTIN) is not registered; the sealed shape that would be binding if the owner adds one is in L11 |
| B8 | `customer.addresses` | table (TenantBase) | SP3/R3.1 | SP4/R4.1, SP4/R4.3, SP3/R3.4 | new | `company_id FK ON DELETE CASCADE`, `kind VARCHAR(12)` (`billing`, `shipping`, `registered`), `line1 VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL`, `line2 VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, `city VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, `state_code VARCHAR(2) NULL`, `state VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, `postal_code VARCHAR(12) NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, `country VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'India'`, `is_default BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`. Partial unique one default per `(organization_id, company_id, kind)`. Not in events. `companies.city` and `.country` (`services/customer/app/models.py:110-111`) stay for the list view |
| B9 | `customer.contacts.email` | column `NOT NULL` → `VARCHAR(254) NULL` | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.2, SP4/R4.1 (phone-only `parties:ensure`), SP4/R4.2 | changed | Today `NOT NULL` (`services/customer/app/models.py:171`); the live-row unique index `uq_contacts_org_email` (`:222-228`) is kept (NULLs do not collide). New CHECK `ck_contacts_has_email_or_phone`: `email IS NOT NULL OR phone_index IS NOT NULL`. `ContactIn.email` becomes optional (`services/customer/app/schemas.py:179`); `ContactOut.email` becomes `str \| None` (`services/customer/app/schemas.py:218`). R4.1 replaces the CHECK (drop, re-add `NOT VALID`, `VALIDATE`) with `erased_at IS NOT NULL OR email IS NOT NULL OR phone_index IS NOT NULL`, because an anonymised contact (B12) has neither an email nor a phone index and the erase would otherwise violate it |
| B10 | `customer.contacts.email_index` | column `VARCHAR(64) NULL` + `ix_contacts_org_email_index (organization_id, email_index)` | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.1 (`contacts:resolve` by email), SP3/R3.7 (dedupe), SP2/R2.9 (optional sealing) | new | `platform_core.pii.blind_index(canonical, kind="email")` of the plaintext email. **Existing rows are backfilled by the owner-run tool H8, never inside the migration**: the index is a keyed HMAC that needs the PII key, and the repo's own precedent for the phone index declined a bulk key-dependent backfill in a migration (`services/customer/migrations/versions/b5c81e02a7d9_contact_phone_index.py:1-17`). Until H8 has run for an organization, `email_index IS NULL` reads as "not findable by email yet" and the email lookups (C38, C46) do not see its older contacts, so the owner runs H8 for every organization after the R3.1 deploy and before the R3.7a deploy; new and edited contacts are indexed at write time. Never crosses a service boundary (lookups send plaintext over the authenticated internal call, the way `contacts:resolve` already does for phone: `customer/app/api/internal.py:197-241`) |
| B11 | `uq_contacts_org_phone_only` | partial unique index | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.7, SP4/R4.1 | new | `(organization_id, phone_index) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL AND email IS NULL AND phone_index IS NOT NULL`: "phone-first uniqueness" for contacts with no email; contacts with an email keep email uniqueness. Two contacts sharing a phone are still legal when each has an email |
| B12 | `customer.contacts.erased_at`, and the audit-copy scrub functions | column `TIMESTAMPTZ NULL` plus `SECURITY DEFINER` functions | SP4/R4.1 (column and `customer.scrub_contact_audit`); SP4/R4.2 (`notification.scrub_contact_audit`), SP4/R4.3 (`finance.scrub_customer_audit`); SP3/R3.7a (`lead.scrub_lead_audit`, B86) | SP4/R4.2, SP4/R4.4, SP4/R4.7 | new | Marks anonymise-in-place: `email` NULL, `full_name` `'Erased contact'`, `phone` `''`, `phone_enc`, `phone_index`, `email_index` NULL; the row id is kept so `deal.primary_contact_id` and activities still resolve (design: ADR slug `crm-consent-and-erasure`, §I). **The audit copies are part of the erasure.** Customer's own audit rows hold the same personal data: `contact.created` writes `{full_name, email, status}` as `after` (`services/customer/app/services.py:685`), `contact.updated` writes `before` and `after` for every changed field (`:752-753`, and `:1134-1139` on the lead path) and `contact.reused_for_lead` writes masked phones (`:1155`); `record_audit` cannot catch them because it redacts by key name and neither `email` nor `full_name` is a redacted key (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/audit.py:29-55, 112`). Anonymising the row alone would leave all of it behind. Registered default (pending the ADR) is **both halves**: (1) from R3.1 on, customer's contact and lead-conversion audit rows carry `changed_fields` names only, never `email`, `full_name` or phone values, and (2) the erase rewrites the PII keys (`email`, `full_name`, `title`, `phone`, `phone_from_lead`, `kept_existing_phone`, `raw_phone`: `title` is a contact field that `contact.updated` copies, `services/customer/app/models.py:170`) in `before` and `after` of every existing `customer.audit_logs` row for that contact (`full_name` to `Erased contact`, the rest to `[erased]`), in the same transaction, and writes its own `contact.erased` audit row (ids and counts only). **The rewrite is a database function, not an `UPDATE` from the app:** `apply_schema_grants` revokes `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `audit_logs` from the app role (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/db.py:140-146`), so `customer.scrub_contact_audit(p_organization_id uuid, p_contact_id uuid) -> integer` is `SECURITY DEFINER` with `SET search_path = customer, pg_temp`, owned by `opsgrids_admin` (`db_admin_role`, `services/customer/app/config.py:14`, which is `BYPASSRLS`), `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC` and granted to the service's app role only, and bound to the caller's tenant (it raises unless `p_organization_id` equals `customer.current_org()`, so a missing tenant GUC fails closed). It reaches only rows of one `entity` and `entity_id` in one organization, only the fixed key list, only to a tombstone, and its two helpers act on JSON **objects only**: `record_audit` stores an absent snapshot as JSON `null`, which `jsonb_each` raises on, so a helper that tested only `IS NULL` would roll back the erase of nearly every API-created contact. **The same scrub exists in every schema whose audit rows copy a contact's personal data**, each created by the release that first erases there, with the same shape and guards: `customer.scrub_contact_audit` (R4.1), `notification.scrub_contact_audit` (R4.2: `engage.contact.*` rows copy the name and the masked email), `finance.scrub_customer_audit` (R4.3: `customer.updated` rows copy `contact_name` and `email`) and `lead.scrub_lead_audit` (B86: created **once, by SP3 in R3.7a**, over the union of the key lists; R4.7 only calls it and asserts its two guards) |
| B13 | `tags` | column `JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'` on `customer.companies`, `customer.contacts`, `deal.deals`, `lead.leads` | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.2 (filter `tag`), SP3/R3.4, SP4/R4.6 | new | List of lower-case strings, at most 20 per record, each at most 40 characters; same shape as `support.tickets.tags` (`services/support/app/models.py:127`). API field `tags: string[]` on every `*Out` and `*In`; the 360's `customer.tags` (always `[]` today, `customer/app/c1_schemas.py:36-37`) is filled from it. Index choice is SP3's (a GIN index cannot lead with `organization_id` without `btree_gin`) |
| B14 | `saved_views` in each of `customer` (`entity` `company`, `contact`), `deal` (`deal`), `lead` (`lead`) | table (TenantBase) | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.8 | new | `membership_id UUID NOT NULL` (the view's owner, taken from `ctx.principal.membership_id`, **not** `own_ref`, so views do not depend on the C15 flip), `entity VARCHAR(12)`, `name VARCHAR(80)`, `filters JSONB NOT NULL` (exactly the query parameters the list accepts, validated against that list's allowed keys), `sort VARCHAR(40) NULL`, `visibility VARCHAR(8) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'private'` (`private`, `org`; `org` requires the caller's read grant to be ORG), `position INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`. Unique `(organization_id, membership_id, entity, name) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`. Per-service because each service validates its own list's filter keys; no gateway change |
| B15 | `customer.party_rollup` | table, composite PK `(organization_id, company_id, currency)` | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3 (360, company page), SP4/R4.6 | new | Per company **per currency, never summed across currencies**: `receivable_minor`, `overdue_minor`, `billed_lifetime_minor`, `paid_lifetime_minor` (`BIGINT`), `open_orders INTEGER`, `last_order_at`, `last_invoice_at`, `last_payment_at`, `as_of TIMESTAMPTZ` (latest applied `source_occurred_at`), `updated_at`. Folded by the existing customer consumer (about +1 MB), version-guarded upserts, idempotent via `processed_events`. Readable under CRM scope on `companies.owner_id` and `crm.book.company.read_commercial` (§E). Labelled "as of"; a parity test against finance's own receivables. **Derived, not counted:** the rollup is computed from the per-document and per-allocation facts of B79 and B80, because counters cannot produce `overdue_minor`, cannot reconcile with a backfill (H4) and lose a payment that arrives before its invoice event |
| B16 | `customer.party_timeline` | table, append-only (trigger as `deal.activities`, `e94d7a2c5f18:90-107`) | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3, R4.4, R4.5; SP3/R3.5 (merged timeline) | new | `id`, `organization_id`, `company_id UUID NOT NULL`, `contact_id UUID NULL`, `kind VARCHAR(40)` (`invoice_issued`, `payment_received`, `order_placed`, `order_delivered`, `ticket_opened`, `ticket_solved`, `conversation_touched`, `quote_sent`, `quote_accepted`, `project_started`), `occurred_at`, `ref_type VARCHAR(40)`, `ref_id UUID`, `title VARCHAR(160)` (document number or generic label, never a person's name or free text), `source_event_id UUID NOT NULL`. Unique `(organization_id, source_event_id)` (idempotency); index `(organization_id, company_id, occurred_at DESC)`; and `uq_party_timeline_org_conversation_day` on `(organization_id, ref_id, UTC date of occurred_at)` where `kind = 'conversation_touched'`, because the touch event is staged per message and the timeline keeps one line per conversation and day (the shape of deal's `uq_activities_org_conversation_day`, B31). PII-free by construction because it cannot be erased. **Source events, one per kind** (all bound on `customer.events`, EV43): `invoice_issued` ← `finance.billing.invoice.issued`; `payment_received` ← `finance.billing.payment.received`; `order_placed` ← `operations.orders.order.placed`; `order_delivered` ← `operations.orders.order.handed_over` (the handover **is** delivery, ADR-0025 §1, and operations publishes no separate delivered event: `services/operations/app/orders/services.py:63-68`); `ticket_opened` ← `support.desk.ticket.created`; `ticket_solved` ← `support.desk.ticket.status_changed` with `to = solved`; `conversation_touched` ← `engage.inbox.conversation.touched`; `quote_sent`, `quote_accepted` ← `crm.pipeline.quote.sent`, `.accepted`; `project_started` ← `projects.portfolio.project.created`, `pulseboard.engagements.project.created`. There is deliberately **no contact, follow-up, enquiry or discount kind**: EV4, EV19, EV24 and EV37 are not timeline sources |
| B17 | `customer.company_status` values | vocabulary | — | — | exists | `customer, prospect, lead, churned` (`customer/app/schemas.py:38`, committed in `crm.book.company.created/v1`); contact status `customer, qualified, lead, dormant` (`:39`). **Frozen**: events and tenant automations carry them. The glossary's `dormant` for companies is a copy decision, not a new value (§L) |
| B71 | `employee_id` on `customer.owner_team` and `lead.owner_team` | column `UUID NULL` plus partial index `ix_owner_team_org_employee (organization_id, employee_id) WHERE employee_id IS NOT NULL` | SP4/R4.7 (SP3/R3.8 may add the column itself with `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`) | SP4/R4.7 (HR exit, leave availability) | new | HR exit and leave events carry employee ids, not membership ids. EV29 does carry `employee_id`, but B4 stores none, so without this column an HR exit is "unmapped" for ever and every rep reads as available (a silent no-op). **Its writer is part of this row:** R4.7 extends R3.8's `owner_team_changed` handler in customer and lead to write it from the enriched `core.membership.*` payload under the same strictly-newer guard, extends H10's `apply` to write it from the C57 rows (and `plan` to count a differing row as drift), and the owner re-runs H10 `apply` once after the R4.7 deploy, because membership events cannot be replayed and existing rows stay NULL until it runs |
| B79 | `customer.party_documents` | table, composite PK `(organization_id, source, document_id)` (no `id` or soft-delete column, as B15) | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3 (B15, C24), SP4/R4.5 | new | The per-document state beneath B15, version-guarded (applied only if `source_occurred_at` is strictly newer): `source` (`finance`, `operations`), `document_id` (the invoice or order id), `company_id`, `contact_id NULL`, `kind` (`invoice`, `credit_note`, `order`), `number VARCHAR(40)` (a document number, never a person's name), `currency`, `total_minor BIGINT` (negative for a credit note), `status`, `issued_on` and `due_on` (invoices), `occurred_at`. Never summed across sources or currencies. Index `(organization_id, company_id, kind, occurred_at DESC)`. From R4.3 `DELETE /companies/{company_id}` (C21) answers 409 `has_commercial_history` while any row exists for the company |
| B80 | `customer.party_payments` | table, composite PK `(organization_id, payment_id, invoice_id)` | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3 (B15) | new | One row per payment-to-invoice allocation beneath B15: `company_id`, `currency`, `amount_minor BIGINT CHECK (> 0)`, `received_on DATE`, `source_occurred_at`. An invoice's paid figure is the sum of its rows, so a payment that arrives before its invoice event is not lost. Indexes `(organization_id, invoice_id)` and `(organization_id, company_id, received_on DESC)` |
| B81 | `customer.companies.lifecycle_promoted_at` | column `TIMESTAMPTZ NULL` | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3 | new | The claim behind D-34's automatic promotion of a company to `customer` (EV2): at most one automated promotion per company, claimed atomically, so a person's later edit of the status is never overwritten. H4 and every replay never promote |
| B83 | `customer.customer_settings` | table (TenantBase), one row per organization, lazily created by the first `PUT` (C106) | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3 | new | `lifecycle_promotion VARCHAR(24) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'off'` with CHECK in (`off`, `first_order`, `first_commercial_event`) (D-34); unique `uq_customer_settings_org (organization_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`; an absent row means every default. Customer has no settings table at HEAD (its models are `Owner`, `Company`, `Contact` and the three platform tables). Its sweeps discover organizations on the narrow outbox role, so the grant lives in customer's `migrations/env.py`, not in a revision (F20) |

### B.2 `deal` schema (owners per row; the chain starts at `e94d7a2c5f18`)

Chain order is merge order (programme §7): `e94d7a2c5f18` → `a1c9e2f7b3d4` (adopted, B18) → R2.3 stages (`f3b8d1a65c02`)
→ R2.6 `owner_rekey_journal` (`d8a35c17e920`) → R3.1 → R3.4 → R3.6 → R3.8 → R4.x (the two SP2 revision ids are new files,
unused anywhere in the tree). **For R2.3 and R2.6 the order is fixed, not first come:** the adopted `a1c9e2f7b3d4` is
byte-pinned and is never re-parented, so R2.3 merges first and R2.6's deal revision is written with `down_revision =
"f3b8d1a65c02"` (R2.6 needs R2.3 for deal's chain). Everywhere else whoever merges second sets `down_revision` to the
first one's head (marker `# RE-PARENT HERE`, precedent `services/operations/migrations/versions/d7a3c9e5b184_*.py`); a revision
carrying the marker may only move forward from `f3b8d1a65c02`.

| # | Name | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Key shape and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B18 | `deal.deals.closed_at` | column `TIMESTAMPTZ NULL` | SP2/R2.3 (moved from R3.1, §J) | SP3/R3.1 (win/loss analytics), SP3/R3.8, insights, R2.3 event schemas | new | Adopted **unchanged** as revision `a1c9e2f7b3d4` ("add closed_at", `down_revision e94d7a2c5f18`; its audit backfill is `closed_won`/`closed_lost`-literal, correct while only the legacy six exist) from `sprint/insights-q2` (commit `a4b248f1`), so the insights rebase is a no-op and there is one head (pending D-12). Set when a deal enters a stage whose `outcome` is `won` or `lost`; never changed afterwards. The committed deal schemas already require it (`crm.pipeline.deal.created/v1.schema.json` `required`) |
| B19 | `deal.pipelines` | table (TenantBase) | SP2/R2.3 | SP2/R2.5, SP3/R3.4 | new | `key VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL`, `name VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL`, `is_default BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true`. Unique `uq_pipelines_org_key (organization_id, key)`; partial unique `uq_pipelines_org_default (organization_id) WHERE is_default AND deleted_at IS NULL`. One pipeline per org today, tables shaped for more (pending D-5). The materialised default pipeline is `key = 'sales'`, `name = 'Sales pipeline'` (B22). A unique `uq_pipelines_org_id (organization_id, id)` is the target of B20's composite foreign key. ADR-0017 decision 4: deal's own schema, never a `pipeline` service |
| B20 | `deal.pipeline_stages` | table (TenantBase) | SP2/R2.3 | SP2/R2.4, R2.5; SP3/R3.4, R3.5; SP4/R4.5, R4.6; customer 360 | new | `pipeline_id UUID NOT NULL`, with a **composite organization-scoped foreign key** `(organization_id, pipeline_id)` to B19's `uq_pipelines_org_id` `ON DELETE RESTRICT` (a single-column key would let a cross-tenant pointer through the referential check); `key VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL` (immutable slug, fits `deals.stage`, server-generated, a client-supplied key is refused); `label VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL`; `position INTEGER NOT NULL`; `outcome VARCHAR(8) NOT NULL` CHECK in (`open`, `won`, `lost`); `default_probability SMALLINT NOT NULL` CHECK 0-100 (won = 100, lost = 0, enforced in `move_stage` too); `tone VARCHAR(10) NOT NULL` from `StatusTone` (`neutral`, `info`, `success`, `warning`, `danger`: `frontend/opsgrids-ui/src/lib/status.ts:19`); `archived_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL`. **Unique `uq_pipeline_stages_org_pipeline_key (organization_id, pipeline_id, key)` is not partial**: a key is never reused, even after archive. Index `ix_pipeline_stages_org_pipeline_position`; a partial unique index on the **active** labels of a pipeline, case-insensitive; CHECKs for the closing pin (won = 100, lost = 0), the tone vocabulary, the key pattern and a non-negative position. Invariants: at least one active `open`, one `won`, one `lost`; closed outcomes ordered last; a trigger freezes `key`, `pipeline_id`, `organization_id` **and `outcome`** (the API never changes an outcome, so it is immutable outright, not only once a deal has sat in the stage); archive, never delete (pending D-5). The word is `outcome`, not `kind` (P§4.1; the discovery digests said `kind`) |
| B21 | `deal.deals.stage` | column | — | — | exists | `VARCHAR(20)` key with no FK and no CHECK (`deal/app/models.py:74`); stays the stage reference in this programme (pending D-5). Unknown stage stays **422** via `ValidationProblem` with a `stage` field error (pinned by `deal/tests/test_write_routes.py:195,216`); the entry stage for a new deal and for lead conversion becomes "first active open stage by position" (today literal `'lead'`, `deal/app/api/internal.py:135`, `deal/app/schemas.py:69`) |
| B22 | Virtual default pipeline | behaviour | SP2/R2.3 | all | new | An org with no `pipelines` rows reads the code default: `lead` (open, 20), `qualified` (open, 40), `proposal` (open, 55), `negotiation` (open, 70), `closed_won` (won, 100), `closed_lost` (lost, 0) (`deal/app/services.py:51-58`, `deal/app/domain.py:21`). The first stage-config write materialises the pipeline (`key = 'sales'`, `name = 'Sales pipeline'`) and the six stage rows under a transaction-scoped advisory lock keyed on the organization (`pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(...))`, precedent `services/storefront/app/services.py:567-576`): while the default is virtual there is **no pipeline row to lock**. The write re-reads after taking the lock, so two concurrent first writes materialise once. Keeps bare-org fixtures, seeds and e2e green with zero provisioning (`e2e/edge_product_crud.py:401-425`) |
| B23 | `deal.deals.number` and `deal.deal_counters` | column `INTEGER NULL` + table | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.4, R3.5; SP4/R4.5 (quote numbering) | new | `deals.number` is nullable so the expand step is cheap, and is backfilled **inside the same Alembic migration** (registered default: a set-based `UPDATE` numbering each organization's rows by `created_at, id` where `number IS NULL`, then `deal_counters.next_number = max(number) + 1`; idempotent, counts-only log; it runs at the owner-run deploy and needs no PII key), partial unique `uq_deals_org_number (organization_id, number) WHERE number IS NOT NULL`. `deal_counters`: primary key `organization_id` (a single-column key, like `leave_settings`), `next_number BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, allocated with `UPDATE ... RETURNING` inside the create transaction. API field `number: int`; display `"{deal_number_prefix}-{number}"` (B27) is a UI concern |
| B24 | `deal.close_reasons` and `deal.deals.close_reason_id`, `deal.deals.close_note` | table (TenantBase) + two columns | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.4, R3.5, R3.8; SP4/R4.6 | new | `close_reasons`: `outcome VARCHAR(8) NOT NULL` (`won`, `lost`), `key VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL`, `label VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL`, `position INTEGER`, `archived_at`; unique `(organization_id, outcome, key)`. One table serves "win/loss reasons" (R3.1) and the prompts and analytics that follow. `deals.close_reason_id UUID NULL FK deal.close_reasons.id ON DELETE RESTRICT`, `deals.close_note VARCHAR(500) NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`. The release list says "loss reasons" in places; this is the same table (§J). **The default reasons are virtual** until the first write that needs them (the shape of B22): the code defaults for `won` and `lost` carry deterministic ids `uuid5(NS, "{organization_id}:{outcome}:{key}")` (`NS` a constant fixed in code), so `StageMoveIn.close_reason_id` (C8) has an id the browser can send before any row exists; the first write that needs one materialises the defaults under an organization-keyed advisory lock, and `GET /deals/close-reasons` answers `materialised: false` meanwhile (C12). A read never writes |
| B25 | `deal.follow_ups` | table (TenantBase) | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6; SP4/R4.4 (automation `task_requested`), R4.7 (reassignment task) | new | `owner_id UUID NOT NULL` (a membership id once C15 flips), `title VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL` (UI hint: no personal data), `due_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL`, `status VARCHAR(12) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'open'` CHECK (`open`, `done`, `cancelled`), `completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL`; targets `deal_id UUID NULL FK deal.deals.id ON DELETE CASCADE`, `company_id`, `contact_id`, `lead_id` (plain UUIDs, `lead_id` points into the lead schema); CHECK at least one target; `arm_seq INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (every arm takes the next value) and `schedule_key VARCHAR(120) NULL` (the armed job's idempotency key, `follow_up:{id}:{due_at_utc_iso}:{arm_seq}`, **single-use**: the scheduler keeps a cancelled job's row and key for its retention window, A16, so a reused key would silently create no job); `origin VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'rep'` (`rep`, `automation`, `system`) and `origin_ref VARCHAR(80) NULL` with partial unique `(organization_id, origin, origin_ref)` (automation idempotency on `run_id:node_id`). Indexes `ix_follow_ups_org_owner_status_due`, `ix_follow_ups_org_deal`. **Deliberately not an activity**: `deal.activities` is past-only and append-only (`services/deal/app/models.py:99-138`); a follow-up is mutable |
| B26 | Follow-up reminder arming | behaviour on `deal.follow_ups.schedule_key` | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6 | new | See §D (`crm.pipeline.follow_up.reminder_requested`); armed with `schedule_event` on create and reschedule (each arm increments `arm_seq`, B25, so no key is requested twice), cancelled with A16 on complete, cancel and reschedule, and **every consumer re-checks `status = 'open'`, `due_at` unchanged and `arm_seq` equal** so a stale firing is harmless |

Lazy get-or-create like `lead.assignment_rules` (`lead/app/models.py:158-185`); one `GET` and one `PUT` with an audit row. The rows added by later releases are additive columns |

Lives in deal's schema, never the `quotation` scaffold (pending D-19) |
| B29 | `deal.quote_lines` | table (TenantBase) | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 | new | `quote_id FK ON DELETE CASCADE`, `position INTEGER`, `inventory_item_id UUID NULL` (plain UUID into `operations.inventory_items`; pending D-19 item master; **NULL, and the line is free text, whenever the organization is not entitled to Operations**: the composer reads the master only through C87, which calls C80, and both answer only for an entitled organization), snapshots `name VARCHAR(200)`, `sku VARCHAR(60) NULL`, `hsn_sac VARCHAR(12) NULL` (copied from the item's `hsn_code`, `services/operations/app/models.py:536`), `uom VARCHAR(20)`, then `quantity_milli BIGINT` (milli-units, ADR-0014), `unit_price_minor BIGINT`, `discount_bps INTEGER`, `tax_rate_bps INTEGER`, and three **derived amounts stored once**, as `operations` order lines store theirs: `line_gross_minor` (quantity times price, rounded half-to-even once), `line_total_minor` (the **taxable value**: gross less the line discount; the invoice handoff takes this figure) and `line_tax_minor` (half-up of the taxable value at `tax_rate_bps`, as finance's `gst_amount_minor`) |

The deal's existing free-text `source VARCHAR(60)` (`services/deal/app/models.py:82`) is untouched |
| B31 | `deal.activities` additions | columns | SP4/R4.2 | SP4/R4.2, R4.4 | new | `origin VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'rep'` (`rep`, `system`), `ref_type VARCHAR(40) NULL`, `ref_id UUID NULL`; new `activity_type` values `message` (WhatsApp conversation) and `ticket` (system-authored, **PII-free**, body NULL); coalescing unique index `uq_activities_org_conversation_day` on `(organization_id, ref_id, ((occurred_at AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')::date), direction) WHERE ref_type = 'conversation'`. The append-only trigger is untouched. System activities written by a handoff (C20) or the won-deal kickoff (EV8, R4.4) use `activity_type = 'note'`, `origin = 'system'`, a generic subject ("Order created", "Invoice created", "Project created") and no body, with `ref_type` in (`order`, `invoice`, `project`), `ref_id` the callee's id and `owner_id` the deal's owner; they carry no title and no customer text |
| B57 | `deal.activity_attachments` | table (TenantBase), **append-only** (trigger as `deal.activities`, `e94d7a2c5f18:90-107`) | SP4/R4.5 (after R19) | SP4/R4.5 | new (registered default) | The notes-attachments promise of P§6 (Document row). `activity_id UUID NOT NULL FK deal.activities.id ON DELETE CASCADE`, `private_object_id UUID NOT NULL` (plain UUID into `document.private_objects`, ADR-0009 Q1; never a URL), `content_type VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL`, `size_bytes BIGINT NOT NULL`, `checksum_sha256 VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL`, `original_name VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL` (a file name the user chose, so it may be personal data: shown only on the authorised read, never in an event, an audit `after` or a log line). Index `(organization_id, activity_id)`. The allowed content types and the per-activity count are the SP4 plan's; the byte ceiling is the gateway's (C83). No event: nothing consumes attachments. An attachment may contain personal data that the append-only activity row never did, which is the erasure question L19 |
| B61 | `deal.deals.stale_notified_on`, `deal.deals.overdue_notified_on` | columns `DATE NULL` | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6 | new (registered default) | The storage behind EV21's "idempotent on `deal_id:kind:as_of`". The handler sets the column to `as_of` in the same transaction that stages the event and does nothing when it already holds `as_of` or a later date; both are cleared when the condition stops holding (an activity is logged, the close date moves, the stage changes), so there is one event per deal per threshold crossing. The handler sets the marker to the review's `as_of` whether or not the matching `notify_*` switch (B65) is on, so switching a notification back on never floods. It does not depend on EV27's mechanism: it would hold for a scheduler-armed review or a folded sweep alike |
