R3.6: `notify_follow_up_due`, `notify_stale`, `notify_overdue BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true` (producer-side opt-outs: a tenant rule cannot switch off EV54's system alerts, so the organization's off switch is a column), `review_armed_for DATE NULL` and `review_baselined_on DATE NULL` (the state of the daily review chain, EV27: the date it is armed for, and the date its silent baseline ran, so the first review after the deploy does not alert on every old deal) |
| B68 | `deal.quote_handoffs` | table (TenantBase) | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 | new | What a quote has already been handed off to, for the UI and for idempotency: `quote_id FK deal.quotes.id ON DELETE CASCADE`, `target` (`order`, `invoice`, `project`), `ref_id UUID` (the callee's id). Unique `uq_quote_handoffs_org_quote_target (organization_id, quote_id, target) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` |

### B.3 `lead` schema (the chain starts at `91dd3ca663fd`)

Chain order is merge order: `91dd3ca663fd` → R2.6 (`owner_rekey_journal`) → R3.1 (`marketing_campaign_id`, UTM) → R3.7a (one revision: capture endpoints, settings, `external_ref`, clocks, `gstin`, `consent_evidence`, the duplicate pointers B62, the additions B63, `capture_messages` B85 and the audit-scrub function B86; R3.7b adds no DDL) → R3.8 (`owner_team`, `owner_manager_id`, `tags`, `saved_views`) → R4.7 (`rep_unavailability` B75, `owner_team.employee_id` B71, `lead_settings.timezone` B76; B39's `ref` is still a key inside an existing JSONB value, and `lead.scrub_lead_audit` is already there from R3.7a).

| # | Name | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Key shape and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B32 | `lead.leads.marketing_campaign_id`, `utm_source`, `utm_medium`, `utm_campaign`, `utm_term`, `utm_content` | columns | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.7, SP4/R4.6 | new | `marketing_campaign_id UUID NULL` (marketing campaign id; **not** an idempotency key, see B35), UTMs `VARCHAR(120) NULL`; `LeadIn` and `LeadOut` gain them; `ix_leads_org_marketing_campaign_id` |
| B33 | `lead.leads.source` values | vocabulary (`VARCHAR(40)`, no DDL) | SP3/R3.7a (the tolerant `LeadSource`; the first lead with a new source is made in R3.7b) | SP4/R4.7, SP4/R4.6 | changed | `form, import, manual` (`lead/app/models.py:98`; `crm.lead.created/v1` enum) gain `webhook`, `whatsapp`, `storefront`. The three committed lead schemas' `source` enums and `LeadSource` change in the same commit as the producer, and the producer here is R3.7's own change to the lead events: the gap closure of EV10-EV12 (emit `status`, `owner_id`, `converted_at`) ships in R3.7 too (§D intro), so the producer-side test through the real write path can pass for `crm.lead.created` and `crm.lead.updated` |
| B34 | `lead.leads.capture_endpoint_id` | column `UUID NULL FK lead.capture_endpoints.id ON DELETE SET NULL` | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.7a, R3.7b | new | Which form or webhook produced the lead |
| B35 | `lead.leads.external_ref` | column `VARCHAR(120) NULL` + partial unique `uq_leads_org_source_external (organization_id, source, external_ref) WHERE external_ref IS NOT NULL AND deleted_at IS NULL` | SP3/R3.7a | SP4/R4.7 (storefront enquiry id), SP3/R3.7b (Engage conversation id) | new | The idempotency key of `leads:capture`, **required** on the signed webhook (C42), where it is also what makes a replayed body inside the signature's time window return the existing lead. The discovery digest reused `source_ref` for both the enquiry id and the campaign; this registry gives them separate names, `external_ref` and `marketing_campaign_id` (§J) |
| B36 | `lead.capture_endpoints` | table (TenantBase) | SP3/R3.7a (the table; its first row is made in R3.7b) | SP3/R3.7b (the Sources page of the settings hub) | new | `kind VARCHAR(10)` CHECK (`form`, `webhook`), `name VARCHAR(120)` (unique live per org), `token_prefix VARCHAR(12)` (shown in the UI; the full token or secret is shown once), `secret_enc BYTEA NULL` (webhook signing secret sealed by `platform_core.pii.seal`; NULL for forms, whose token is `<organization_id>.<endpoint_id>.<mac>` in base64url, where `mac` is an HMAC-SHA256 of the first two parts under the lead service's own `capture_token_secret` (F12): the service re-derives it with no stored secret, and the token itself carries the organization, because the gateway forwards no organization on a public path (C41)), `allowed_origins JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'` (forms), `consent_text VARCHAR(500) NULL` (the text beside the consent control; its hash, `consent_text_hash`, is the evidence), `success_url VARCHAR(500) NULL` (both added by B63), `default_owner_id UUID NULL` (else the assignment rule), `is_active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true`, `last_used_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL`. Public capture ADR: slug `crm-public-lead-capture` (§I) |
| B37 | `lead.lead_settings` | table (TenantBase), one row per org | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.4 (settings hub), SP3/R3.7a | new | `first_response_sla_minutes INTEGER NULL`, `aging_after_days INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 7`, and the columns added later: `whatsapp_leads_enabled` (B63, R3.7a DDL, used by R3.7b), `notify_assignments BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true` (R4.2: the organization's off switch for assignment alerts, as on B27) and `timezone` (B76, R4.7). Beside `lead.assignment_rules` (exists, `lead/app/models.py:158`, one row per org by `uq_assignment_rules_org`), which gains no column |
| B38 | `lead.leads.first_response_at`, `last_touched_at` | columns `TIMESTAMPTZ NULL` | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.7a | new | SLA and aging clocks |
| B39 | `lead.leads.consent_evidence` | column `JSONB NULL` | SP3/R3.7a (column), SP3/R3.7b (the evidence written at capture), SP4/R4.7 (`ref`, no DDL) | SP3/R3.7b, SP4/R4.7 | new (pending the consent ADR) | **Evidence only, never a decision.** R3.7 stores what the capture form recorded, once, at capture: `{granted: bool, text_hash: string, captured_at: iso8601}` (the hash of the endpoint's `consent_text`, B36). It is never read to decide whether anyone may be messaged: CRM never stores or decides consent (P§7), and notification's ledger decides (ADR-0021). The writer C58 does not exist until R4.1/R4.2, so R3.7 forwards nothing; once it exists, R4.7 forwards the stored evidence through C58 with the lead id as `evidence_ref` and records the ledger's reference as `ref` inside the same object. The one exception is a lead whose `source` is `storefront`: storefront forwards its own consent through C58 (W25), so lead stores that block as evidence and never forwards it (C56). C58 accepts only `source = "web_form"`, so only a form lead is forwarded. A lead captured with no consent block (the signed webhook, an Engage stranger) has `NULL`. Dropped entirely if the capture ADR (I2) decides CRM should hold not even the evidence |
| B40 | `lead.leads.phone_index` | column | SP1/R1.2b (stops writing), SP3/R3.7a (resumes writing, backfill) | SP3/R3.7a | exists | `String(64)` (`lead/app/models.py:95`) with no reader today. R1.2b stops computing it and maps the attribute `deferred=True` so no query selects it (the column is kept: L2 is closed; the contractive step, which removes the attribute and drops the column in one change, ships only if R3.7 does not resume the index); R3.7's phone-based duplicate detection needs it again, so R3.7a resumes writing it at write time. Its backfill from the sealed phone is the owner-run tool H8 (`tools/backfill_pii_indexes.py`): opening every sealed phone is a bulk PII read, so it needs an operator, a dry-run default and one audit row per organization naming the field and the count, and it is never done inside a migration (the same precedent as B10); until H8 has run, `phone_index IS NULL` reads as "not findable by phone yet". The conflict is settled in §L (L2, closed) |
| B60 | `lead.leads.gstin` | column `VARCHAR(15) NULL` | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.7b (capture), SP4/R4.7 (storefront), `leads:convert` (C48) | new | A15-validated GSTIN supplied with the capture. Storefront enquiries already capture one (`services/storefront/app/models.py:806`), but neither `leads:capture` (C56) nor `leads:convert` (C48) carried it, so B7's "GSTIN is the preferred dedupe key" was unreachable on that path. A registration number held in the clear like B7's; no PAN is derived from it or stored. Passed to C48 as `company_gstin`, which writes it to `customer.company_tax_ids` and may match an existing company by it. An invalid value is dropped at capture, never a reason to refuse the lead |
| B62 | `lead.leads.duplicate_of_lead_id`, `lead.leads.duplicate_contact_id` | columns `UUID NULL` (plain UUIDs: the target lead may be soft-deleted, and the other points into customer) | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.7a (C36, C38) | new | Persisted **at capture**, when the plaintext email and phone are in hand: recomputing a duplicate later needs the sealed values, and opening them for a display would write a sensitive-read audit row per view. Partial index `ix_leads_org_duplicate (organization_id) WHERE duplicate_of_lead_id IS NOT NULL OR duplicate_contact_id IS NOT NULL`. **Shown by permission, never raw** (C36): a boolean `possible_duplicate` reaches every lead reader; `duplicate_of_lead_id` only when that lead is inside the reader's `ctx.apply` scope; `duplicate_contact_id` only to a caller holding `crm.book.contact.read` at ORG. A stored id must not carry the existence of a contact, or of another rep's lead, across a permission boundary that P§7 says no new route may widen. Cleared by C108 |
| B63 | `lead.capture_endpoints` and `lead.lead_settings` additions to B36 and B37 | columns | SP3/R3.7a (DDL, in the one R3.7a revision), used by SP3/R3.7b | SP3/R3.7b | new | `capture_endpoints.success_url VARCHAR(500) NULL` (a browser form post needs a redirect target: C41 answers 303 to it), `capture_endpoints.consent_text_hash VARCHAR(64) NULL` (consent evidence is the hash of the words shown, B39) and `lead_settings.whatsapp_leads_enabled BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (a stranger's WhatsApp message becomes a lead only on this opt-in, C56) |
| B75 | `lead.rep_unavailability` | table (TenantBase) | SP4/R4.7 | SP4/R4.7 (round-robin availability) | new | Approved leave projected from HRMS events, one row per leave request, so assignment skips a rep who is away: `source_ref VARCHAR(80)` (`leave:{leave_request_id}`), `employee_id UUID`, `starts_on` and `ends_on DATE`, `state` (`active`, `cancelled`), `source_occurred_at` (the version: applied only if strictly newer). Unique `uq_rep_unavailability_org_source (organization_id, source_ref)`; index `(organization_id, employee_id) WHERE state = 'active'`. Fed by lead's own consumer on `lead.events` (EV45) from `hrms.time.leave.applied` and `.cancelled`, backfilled by H13; the rep is found through `owner_team.employee_id` (B71). A half-day counts as a day, "today" is the organization's date in `lead_settings.timezone` (B76), and when every active rep is away the rotation ignores availability, so a lead is never left unowned |
| B76 | `lead.lead_settings.timezone` | column `VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'Asia/Kolkata'` | SP4/R4.7 | SP4/R4.7 | new | "Today" for leave availability (B75). `account.organizations.timezone` (`services/account/app/models.py:83`, the same default) is not readable by lead, and B65's `deal.pipeline_settings.timezone` is deal's. Written by `PUT /leads/settings` (C40) |
| B85 | `lead.leads.capture_messages` | column `JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'` | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.7a (C109), SP3/R3.7b (C41, C42, C56 write it) | new | The one place an anonymous party's own words are stored, **never `notes`**: `notes` is rep-authored and rep-edited, and an address is public knowledge, so appending to it would let an unauthenticated party write unverified text inside a trusted record. CHECK `ck_leads_capture_messages`: a JSON array of at most five `{at, source, text}` (`source` in `form`, `webhook`, `whatsapp`, `storefront`; `text` 1 to 2,000 characters); appended, never edited; the application counts and drops a sixth. Rendered as plain, labelled, read-only text; read through C109; cleared by C108; only its count reaches `LeadOut` (`capture_message_count`, C36) and it never rides an event or an audit row |
| B86 | `lead.scrub_lead_audit(p_organization_id uuid, p_lead_id uuid) -> integer` | `SECURITY DEFINER` function | SP3/R3.7a (creates it, once) | SP3/R3.7a (C108); SP4/R4.7 (calls it) | new | The audit-copy scrub of B12 for a lead: `SET search_path = lead, pg_temp`, owner `opsgrids_admin` (`db_admin_role`, `services/lead/app/config.py:14`), `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC` and granted to `opsgrids_lead` only, with B12's two guards (its helpers act on JSON objects only; it raises unless `p_organization_id` equals `lead.current_org()`). The app role cannot `UPDATE` `audit_logs` (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/db.py:140-146`), which is why this is a function. It rewrites, in `before` and `after` of every `audit_logs` row with `entity = 'lead'`, `entity_id = p_lead_id` and that organization, the keys `full_name` (to `Erased lead`) and `company`, `notes`, `email`, `phone`, `raw_phone` (to `[erased]`), returns the number of rows touched, and reaches nothing else. **Created once, by SP3's R3.7a revision, with the union of the two specs' key lists** (SP3's six keys; SP4's `full_name`, `notes`, `email`, `phone` is a subset): public capture is what first stores a stranger's details, so the administrator's erase (C108) cannot ship before it. R4.7 needs R3.7 and therefore creates nothing; it calls the function from the contact-erase handler for a converted lead and asserts the two guards |

### B.4 Other products' link columns and projections (every column is a plain nullable indexed UUID unless stated)

| # | Name | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Key shape and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B41 | `finance.customers.crm_company_id` | column | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3, R4.5, R4.6 | new | The billed party's CRM company. Unique `uq_customers_org_company_gstin (organization_id, crm_company_id, coalesce(gstin, '')) WHERE crm_company_id IS NOT NULL AND deleted_at IS NULL`: one company, several GSTIN profiles. Finance keeps its table name `customers` (the glossary calls it the **billed party**) |
| B42 | `finance.customers.crm_contact_id` | column | — | SP4/R4.3 | exists | `finance/app/models.py:111`, partial unique `uq_customers_org_crm_contact` `:141-147`, migration `faf3ff81d039`. Becomes the **billing contact**, and is set on **at most one billing profile per contact**: the company's primary profile. Further GSTIN profiles of the same company (B41) carry `NULL` here and are found by `crm_company_id` and GSTIN. The partial unique `uq_customers_org_crm_contact` therefore **stays unchanged through this programme**, which keeps R4.3 rollback-tolerant: the previous finance release's `ensure_customer_for_crm_contact` looks the row up by contact and catches an `IntegrityError` on exactly that index (`services/finance/app/services.py:506-640`), so a rollback never meets a duplicate. Loosening the index to a plain one is a later contractive release, after R4.3 has soaked (nothing calls the bridge yet: `operations/app/orders/services.py:871-892` hard-returns `None`, finance has no `CUSTOMER_INTERNAL_URL` in compose) |
| B43 | `finance.customers.payment_terms_days`, `credit_limit_minor`, `credit_limit_currency` | columns `INTEGER NULL`, `BIGINT NULL`, `VARCHAR(3) NULL` | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3 (order accept credit check, invoice `due_date`), R4.5 (quote terms snapshot) | new | Finance owns accounts receivable, so terms and limit live here and CRM displays them |
| B44 | `finance.payments.currency` | column `VARCHAR(3) NULL` with `ck_payments_currency_iso CHECK (currency IS NULL OR length(currency) = 3)` | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3 (capture consumer) | new | `payments` has no currency column today (discovery); needed before online captures are recorded. **Nullable, not `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'INR'`:** a default would stamp every legacy payment `INR` and, after a rollback, let the previous image write `INR` for a USD payment, a wrong value that looks right. The backfill is **batched and outside the DDL transaction**: after the DDL commits, autocommit batches of 1000 under `lock_timeout 5s` set `currency` from the payment's allocations joined to their invoices, `HAVING count(DISTINCT i.currency) = 1` and only `WHERE p.currency IS NULL`, idempotent (an `UPDATE` inside the `ALTER`'s transaction would hold `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on `payments` for the whole update). A payment whose allocations span several currencies, or none, keeps `NULL` for an owner decision and is counted in the migration log; `NOT NULL` is a later contractive release. From R4.1 `record_payment` (`services/finance/app/services.py:1295`) refuses an allocation set spanning currencies (422 `mixed_currency_payment`), and from R4.3 it writes `currency` on every payment. **A named exception to "the payment ledger is append-only"** (the rule is the model docstring's, `services/finance/app/models.py:11-20`, and no trigger guards `payments`): one new column of existing rows, written once, never an amount, date, reference or allocation |
| B45 | `operations.orders.crm_company_id`, `crm_contact_id`, `finance_customer_id` | columns | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3, R4.5 | new | `orders.customer_id` (`operations/app/models.py:1648`, documented as a plain UUID into `services/customer`, in practice a CRM contact id) is kept and mirrors `crm_contact_id` until a later contractive release; `customer_name`, `customer_phone` snapshots stay. `finance_customer_id` is resolved **at accept**, in `accept_order`'s **call** step, and READY only reads it. READY runs inside the transaction that holds the order row lock (`request_invoice_for_order`'s docstring, `services/operations/app/orders/services.py:965-1013`), while the resolve is a network call that `_resolve_finance_customer_id`'s own docstring says must run "with no session and no transaction open" (`:871-892`); accept already has that shape: claim, call, record (`:483-600`, the credit check at `:423-444`). The record step persists the id; the sequence is in C61. An order with no `crm_company_id` (a walk-in) keeps `NULL`, and READY keeps its visibly-logged skip (`order.invoice_request_skipped`) |
| B46 | `operations.orders.source_type VARCHAR(20) NULL`, `source_id UUID NULL` | columns | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 | new | `source_type = 'quote'`, `source_id` the `deal.quotes.id` of an accepted quote (ADR-0024 §2 provenance) |
| B47 | `notification.contacts.crm_contact_id` and `origin` | columns `UUID NULL`, `VARCHAR(12) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'manual'` | SP4/R4.2 | SP4/R4.2 | new | Partial unique `uq_contact_org_crm_contact (organization_id, crm_contact_id) WHERE crm_contact_id IS NOT NULL`; `origin` in (`manual`, `import`, `signup`, `crm`). **Only contacts with an email are projected**: `notification.contacts` keeps `email_enc`, `email_masked` and `email_index` NOT NULL and hard-deletes its rows (`services/notification/app/models.py:830-850`), so a phone-only CRM contact (B9, D-14) has no row to occupy; C53 exports such a contact with `email: null` and the projection skips it (it stays reachable through the 360's channels section and through C60, which resolve through customer's messaging target, not through this table). **Existing Engage contacts are linked by the owner-run tool H9** (`tools/sync_engage_contacts.py`, dry-run default): it pages C53 and, inside notification, compares notification's own blind index of each CRM email with `contacts.email_index` (no decrypt), writing `crm_contact_id` and `origin = 'crm'` on a match and creating a row for an unmatched CRM contact that has an email; afterwards the EV46 triggers keep it current. A projected contact is **never** auto-opted-in: the sync writes no consent event (pending D-15; ADR-0021 "silence is not consent"). The Engage contact surface changes with the projection: a projected row is read-only in Engage (C84, G17) |
| B48 | `notification.conversations.crm_contact_id` | column, rename with alias | SP4/R4.2 | SP4/R4.2, R4.4 | changed | Today `contact_id UUID NULL` (`notification/app/models.py:539`) holds a CRM contact id. R4.2 adds `crm_contact_id` and backfills it from `contact_id` (`UPDATE ... SET crm_contact_id = contact_id WHERE crm_contact_id IS NULL`: same schema, no PII, no key, idempotent, counts-only log) **in autocommit batches of 1000 after the DDL has committed**, not inside the `ALTER`'s transaction, where one `UPDATE` would hold `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on the table for its whole run; it runs at the owner-run deploy. R4.2 dual-writes, serialises `contact_id` as a deprecated alias for one release train, then a contractive release drops the old column. Also `conversations.crm_company_id UUID NULL` beside `crm_contact_id` (so `engage.inbox.conversation.touched` carries the company without a read, EV30) and `conversations.owner_membership_id UUID NULL` (the contact owner snapshotted at resolve time). `assignee_membership_id` (`:551`) is unchanged |
| B49 | `support.tickets.crm_contact_id`, `crm_company_id`, `conversation_id` | columns | SP4/R4.4 | SP4/R4.4 | new | Beside the requester snapshot (`support/app/models.py:111-114`) which stays the fallback so support works when CRM is not entitled; `channel` gains `whatsapp` (`String(20)`, no DDL). The release list says `contact_id` and `company_id`; the programme's naming rule wins (§J) |
| B50 | `project.projects.crm_company_id`, `crm_deal_id`; `projects.projects.crm_company_id`, `crm_deal_id` | columns | SP4/R4.4 | SP4/R4.4, R4.5 | new | One shared contract for both project products so the second adopts without redesign (pending D-17). `client` free text (`project/app/models.py:107`, `projects/app/models.py:102`) stays as the display snapshot. The won-deal kickoff target is chosen by D-17; the columns are not |
| B51 | `storefront.enquiries.crm_lead_id`, `crm_contact_id`, `crm_company_id` | columns | SP4/R4.7 | SP4/R4.7 | new | Set after `leads:capture` and conversion; the enquiry row stays the immutable line list; the displayed status derives from the lead/deal when CRM is entitled and is stored beside the merchant's own `status` as `crm_status` (B78), never over it, because overwriting `status` would reverse a merchant's manual move and make the committed `storefront.enquiry.status_changed` contract false. Consent evidence is B77; the enquiry docstring's "only `status` ever changes" (`services/storefront/app/models.py:780-796`) is amended in R4.7, since the CRM link columns and the erase are two more writers |
| B52 | `storefront.products.inventory_item_id` | column | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5, R4.7 | new | Plain UUID into `operations.inventory_items` (the one item master, pending D-19) plus a mapping screen. The release list's "inventory item-master link" in R4.7 is this same interface (§J) |
| B53 | `marketing.campaign_attribution` | table, composite PK `(organization_id, campaign_id, currency)` | SP4/R4.6-A | SP4/R4.6-A | new | The derived rollup, **per currency** (one campaign can hold deals in several currencies; counts have no currency): `open_deals INTEGER`, `conversions INTEGER` (won deals), `lost_deals INTEGER`, `pipeline_minor BIGINT`, `revenue_minor BIGINT` (all non-negative), `source_occurred_at`, `as_of`. **`leads` is not stored:** it is a live count of B69. Derived from the facts of B69 and B70, which are state, not counters, and version-guarded (ADR-0020 §2). The existing `marketing.campaigns` columns `leads`, `conversions`, `revenue_minor` and `pipeline_minor` keep their names, types and meaning on the wire (`CampaignOut`, `services/marketing/app/schemas.py:146-167`, is a published contract) and become the typed adjustment; `CampaignOut` gains `attribution` and `effective` (the sum). The earlier plan to rename them `adjustment_*` is dropped |
| B54 | `account.memberships.manager_membership_id` | column | — | SP3/R3.8 (via events and R2.6 bulk read) | exists | `services/account/app/models.py:341`; tenant-editable, works with no HRMS. Not new |
| B55 | `finance.invoice_documents` | table (TenantBase), **insert-only** (refused `UPDATE` and `DELETE` by trigger, the shape `einvoice_filings` already has: `services/finance/app/models.py:1-38`) | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 | new (registered default) | `invoice_id UUID NOT NULL FK finance.invoices.id ON DELETE RESTRICT` (same schema, so a foreign key is allowed), `pdf_object_id UUID NOT NULL` (plain UUID into `document.private_objects`, ADR-0009 Q1, after R19), `rendered_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL`, `renderer_version VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL`. One row per render; a later re-render inserts a newer row and the invoice's current PDF is the newest by `rendered_at`; unique `(organization_id, invoice_id, pdf_object_id)`; index `(organization_id, invoice_id, rendered_at DESC)`. It replaces the earlier `invoices.pdf_object_id` column: an invoice is frozen at finalize and the repo's root rules make invoices append-only (`services/finance/app/models.py:1-38`), so the invoice row is never written after finalize. Never a URL (C74) |
| B56 | `customer.contacts.email_enc`, `email_masked` (optional sealing of `contacts.email`) | columns `BYTEA NULL`, `VARCHAR(254) NULL` | SP2/R2.9 Part C (optional, C15/Q2) | SP2/R2.9 | new (pending D-26) | The sealed-envelope pattern `contacts.phone` already uses (`customer/app/models.py:176-206`) and `lead.leads.email_*` (`lead/app/models.py:84-90`): sealed value, masked display, `email_index` (B10) for matching. ADR-0017 accepted plaintext as business contact data (register row `docs/adr/open-questions-register.md:95`: "not scheduled (accepted debt)"). If R2.9 does it, a reveal route `POST /contacts/{contact_id}/email:reveal` mirrors `phone:reveal` (`customer/app/api/routes.py:423-424`: `crm.book.contact.read_sensitive`, step-up, audit, shared reveal budget) and the plaintext column is nulled in a **later** contractive release. **Built only on a yes to D-26 (default no)** and only when R2.9's gates all hold, each recorded by the owner: R3.1 has shipped and H8 has run for `email_index`; the readers of the plaintext email (C45, C47, C53) are sealed-aware or consciously left on the plaintext column; and the reveal route's negative-authz set is green |
| B58 | `finance.invoices.crm_company_id`, `source_type`, `source_id`, `buyer_snapshot` | columns `UUID NULL`, `VARCHAR(20) NULL`, `UUID NULL`, `JSONB NULL` | SP4/R4.3 (`crm_company_id`), SP4/R4.5 (`source_*`) | SP4/R4.3, R4.5 | new | Written **once, at creation**, as part of the frozen header, and never updated (invoices are append-only: `services/finance/app/models.py:1-38`). `crm_company_id` is the party snapshot the invoice events carry (EV33); `source_type = 'quote'` with `source_id` the `deal.quotes.id` is the back-reference C64 promises for `invoices:create-from-quote`. An invoice raised for an order keeps using its existing `order_id` (`services/finance/app/models.py:216-226`, unique per `invoice` document), so `source_type` is `NULL` there. Plain UUIDs, indexed, no foreign key. **`buyer_snapshot`** (R4.3) is written once at creation from the billing profile and, when the profile is linked, from C50: `{legal_name, billing_address: {line1, line2, city, state_code, state, postal_code, country}, gstin_state_code}`, business identity only (never an email, a phone or a contact name; the column has a CHECK that it is a JSON object). An issued invoice today snapshots only the two GSTINs and the place of supply, so renaming a billed party would rewrite how every issued invoice reads; invoices that predate the column keep `NULL` and render from the live profile, flagged "as at today" |
| B59 | `notification.campaigns.marketing_campaign_id` | column `UUID NULL` | SP4/R4.6-A | SP4/R4.6-A (marketing reads Engage, C85) | new | The link behind D-16's "Marketing reads Engage": a plain UUID naming the Marketing campaign an Engage campaign delivers, set through an optional `marketing_campaign_id` on the campaign create and patch bodies (validated by shape only, never by a call to marketing). Engage keeps owning sending and delivery analytics; Marketing owns planning and attribution and reads the counts through C85. Index `ix_campaigns_org_marketing_campaign_id` |
| B64 | `scheduler.scheduled_jobs.cancelled_at` and status `cancelled` | column `TIMESTAMPTZ NULL` plus partial index `ix_scheduled_jobs_cancelled_at (cancelled_at) WHERE status = 'cancelled'` | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6; SP4/R4.5 | new | A16 names the status but no column or retention. `status` is `VARCHAR(16)` with no CHECK (`services/scheduler/migrations/versions/13ef09d48a2b_scheduled_jobs.py:37`), so `cancelled` needs no DDL; `STATUS_CANCELLED` joins the job states of `models.py` (today `pending`, `fired`, `parked`). The index is the retention sweep's cross-tenant discovery on the outbox role, so it does not lead with `organization_id` (the shape of its `fired` sibling). `grant_sweep_reader` (`services/scheduler/migrations/env.py:33-60`) grants five columns today and gains the sixth, `GRANT SELECT (organization_id, id, due_at, status, fired_at, cancelled_at)`, with its `REVOKE ALL` first and its run after every alembic call unchanged. Retention: `purge_stale_fired_jobs` (`services/scheduler/app/services.py:199`) becomes `purge_stale_jobs` and also deletes `cancelled` rows past `cancelled_at` with the same window (`scheduled_job_retention_days`, 7), which is the window in which a key is single-use (A16) |
| B66 | `approval.approval_requests.requester_membership_id`, `decided_by_membership_id`; `approval.approval_steps.approver_membership_id` | columns `UUID NULL` | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 (EV23, EV24, EV50) | new | The membership requester (D-18): a CRM-only tenant has no HR employee link, so a request needs a requester, an approver and a decider that are memberships. CHECK `ck_approval_requests_membership_decider` (a decider is set only on a non-pending request and differs from the requester); partial indexes on `(organization_id, requester_membership_id)` and, for open steps, `(organization_id, approver_membership_id)`. **The same revision replaces the two guard functions** `approval.guard_approval_request_change()` and `approval.guard_approval_step_change()` (`services/approval/migrations/versions/c8d3e6f4a1b2_guard_triggers.py:98` and `:40`): each compares an explicit column tuple, so a column outside it stays mutable by the one `UPDATE` the guard permits. `requester_membership_id` joins the identity tuple of both states, `decided_by_membership_id` the decided-state tuple and `approver_membership_id` the step tuple, and the downgrade restores the previous bodies first; adding the columns alone would leave the decide-once and append-only guarantees unprotected for them |
| B67 | `notification.messages.crm_contact_id`, `entity_ref` | columns `UUID NULL`, `JSONB NULL` | SP4/R4.2 | SP4/R4.2 (C72, EV18) | new | An erase finds the sends that carry no address (`crm_contact_id`), and the bell opens the record (`entity_ref` `{product, kind, id}`, C72) |
| B69 | `marketing.attribution_leads` | table, composite PK `(organization_id, lead_id)` | SP4/R4.6-A | SP4/R4.6-A (B53) | new | One row per attributed lead, first touch (the campaign the lead was captured under): `lead_id` and `campaign_id` (plain UUIDs; the campaign may name no live campaign, C97), `lead_created_at`, `converted_at NULL`, `deal_id NULL`, `source_occurred_at` (the version: applied only if strictly newer). Index `(organization_id, campaign_id)`. State, not counters (ADR-0020 §2) |
| B70 | `marketing.attribution_deals` | table, composite PK `(organization_id, deal_id)` | SP4/R4.6-A | SP4/R4.6-A (B53) | new | One row per attributed deal; money stays in the deal's own currency: `campaign_id`, `currency`, `value_minor BIGINT` (>= 0), `stage_outcome` (`open`, `won`, `lost`; CHECK), `closed_at NULL`, `deleted_at NULL`, `source_occurred_at`. Index `(organization_id, campaign_id, currency) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` |
| B72 | `insights.deal_facts` and `insights.company_facts` reshaped | tables | SP4/R4.6-B | SP4/R4.6-B | changed | The insights projection stores **no names**: a lead-converted deal's name holds a person's name, against ADR-0020's own non-goal. `deal_facts` drops `name` and the stage-vocabulary CHECK and gains `deal_number INTEGER NULL`, `stage_outcome` (`open`, `won`, `lost`; CHECK) and `crm_company_id UUID NULL`; `company_facts` drops `name`; `owner_ref` is nullable in both (the membership owner is null until mapped). These tables live in the unmerged `sprint/insights-q2` stack (L10): the migration is edited in place only after the owner's database check shows it has run nowhere, and otherwise a forward revision does the same to the live tables, backfilling `stage_outcome` from the stored key (`closed_won` to `won`, `closed_lost` to `lost`, anything else `open`) |
| B73 | `insights.stage_facts` | table, PK `(organization_id, id)` | SP4/R4.6-B | SP4/R4.6-B | new | The stage catalogue behind the funnel board (`id` is the stage id): `pipeline_ref`, `stage_key`, `label`, `outcome` (CHECK), `position`, `archived`, and the projection's version columns |
| B74 | `insights.quote_facts` | table, PK `(organization_id, id)` | SP4/R4.6-B | SP4/R4.6-B | new | The quote metrics (`id` is the quote id): `deal_ref`, `quote_number VARCHAR(40)` (a number, never text a person typed), `version`, `status`, `currency`, `total_minor`, `owner_ref NULL`, `company_ref NULL`, `sent_at`, `accepted_at` |
| B77 | `storefront.storefronts.enquiry_consent_text`; `storefront.enquiries.marketing_consent`, `consent_text`, `consent_at` | columns `VARCHAR(500) NULL`; `BOOLEAN NULL`, `VARCHAR(500) NULL`, `TIMESTAMPTZ NULL` | SP4/R4.7 | SP4/R4.7 (the forwarding through C58, W25) | new | The consent control on the buyer form and its evidence, written once at creation: `enquiry_consent_text` NULL means the form shows no control; on an enquiry `marketing_consent` NULL means no control was shown, and the three columns are all NULL or all set (CHECK `ck_enquiries_consent_shape`). D-31: single opt-in recorded with evidence |
| B78 | `storefront.enquiries.crm_deal_id`, `crm_status`, `crm_status_at` | columns `UUID NULL`, `VARCHAR(12) NULL`, `TIMESTAMPTZ NULL` | SP4/R4.7 | SP4/R4.7 | new | Beside B51's three link columns: the deal the lead converted into, and the displayed status derived from the linked lead or deal (`new`, `in_progress`, `won`, `lost`; CHECK), stored beside the merchant's own `status` and never over it. A partial index on each link column |
| B82 | `finance.online_captures` | table (TenantBase) | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3 (`reconcile_online_captures`, C101); SP4/R4.5-C | new | A verified online capture, parked until it can be applied to its order or invoice: `charge_id` (the payment-collections charge id, unique per organization), `reference_type` (`operations.order`; `finance.invoice` from R4.5-C) and `reference_id`, `amount_minor BIGINT > 0`, `currency`, `provider`, `captured_at`, `state` (`pending`, `applied`, `unmatched`), `allocated_minor`, `payment_id` (a same-schema foreign key to `finance.payments`, set exactly when `state = 'applied'`), `applied_at`, `unmatched_reason`, `refunded_total_minor` and `refund_noted_at` (D-28: a refund is recorded, never turned into a credit note). **Its sweep discovery needs a column grant:** `reconcile_online_captures` finds organizations on the narrow outbox role, whose finance grant is `_SWEEP_READER_COLUMNS` (`services/finance/migrations/env.py:36`), so the revision adds `"online_captures": ("organization_id", "id", "state", "created_at", "deleted_at")`; without it the sweep fails every tick with `42501` or finds nothing, and no parked capture is ever applied: the silent stall of the outbox role |
| B84 | `notification.templates.category` | column `VARCHAR(12) NULL` with `ck_templates_category` | SP4/R4.2 | SP4/R4.2 (C60) | new | `marketing` or `transactional`; NULL is unclassified and counts as marketing for a CRM send. `TemplateIn` and `TemplateOut` gain it. Setting `transactional` needs `notification.delivery.template.write` at ORG and writes the audit row `template.category_set`, so the server derives an email's purpose from its template and a rep cannot label marketing content transactional |

## C. HTTP routes

Every new **public** route declares its permission through `require(code, minimum=...)` and `Entitled`
(`require_product("crm")`), injects scope with `ctx.apply` on lists and `ctx.assert_can_touch` on details, and ships a
negative-authz test (the repo's root rules). Every action that has no honest OWN reading sets `minimum=Scope.ORG` so a
customer-granted OWN rung cannot widen it (`max_scope` is a ladder ceiling, not an allowed set: ADR-0007 §1 amendment).
Every new **internal** route follows the grammar in §1 and ships three negative tests: no token (401), wrong caller
(403), wrong organization claim (403) (pattern `services/deal/tests/test_lead_conversion_internal.py:52-56,123-140`).
Static paths are declared before `/{id}` paths. Routers use `Depends(get_tenant_session, scope="function")`
(`tests/infra/test_session_dependency_scope.py`). Paths below omit the `/api/v1` mount for public routes.

### C.1 `deal` public routes (gateway prefix `/api/v1/deals`, plus `/api/v1/activities` from R3.2)

| # | Route | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Permission; key request and response fields |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | `GET /deals/stages` | public | SP2/R2.3 | SP3/R3.4, R3.5 (board, forms, import/export), customer 360, insights | new | `crm.pipeline.deal.read`, any scope, **organization filter only** (reference data, like `GET /customers/owners`, `customer/app/api/routes.py:182-193`; `DealScopeResolver` only knows `Deal.owner_id`). Query `include_archived` (default false), `include=counts` (adds `deal_count`, counting **live deals only**, and is honoured **only for a caller whose `crm.pipeline.deal.read` grant is ORG**: for any other scope the parameter is ignored and no stage carries `deal_count`, because a count is deal-derived data and must not disclose what no read route shows, and `GET /deals/summary`, which the same reader gets, is scoped; R3.4 shows its count column only when the API returns the field). Response `StageListOut {pipeline: {id, key, name} \| null, materialised: bool, stages: StageOut[]}`; `StageOut {id \| null, key, label, position, outcome, default_probability, tone, archived: bool, deal_count?}`. `materialised=false` is the virtual default (B22, `id` null) |
| C2 | `POST /deals/stages` | public | SP2/R2.5 | SP3/R3.4 (settings hub) | new | `crm.pipeline.stage.write`, `minimum=Scope.ORG` (pending D-6). Body `StageIn {label (1-80), outcome, default_probability (0-100; a won stage is pinned at 100 and a lost stage at 0: a different supplied figure is 422 `probability_pinned`, an omitted one means the pin, because silently overriding a figure hides a client bug), tone, after_stage_id?}`. **No `key` accepted**: server-generated slug <= 20 characters, collision-suffixed, the six legacy keys reserved. 201 `StageOut`; audit `pipeline.stage_created`; event `crm.pipeline.stage.created`; first write materialises B22 under an organization-keyed advisory lock |
| C3 | `PATCH /deals/stages/{stage_id}` | public | SP2/R2.5 | SP3/R3.4 | new | Same permission and floor. Body `StagePatch {label?, tone?, default_probability?}`; `key` and `outcome` are refused (422); probability refused on won/lost. Audit `pipeline.stage_updated`; event `crm.pipeline.stage.updated` |
| C4 | `POST /deals/stages:reorder` | public | SP2/R2.5 | SP3/R3.4 | new | Same permission and floor. Body `StageReorderIn {stage_ids: uuid[]}` = the complete ordered list of active stages, closed outcomes last. Answers `StageListOut` (C1). Audit `pipeline.stage_reordered`; event `crm.pipeline.stage.reordered`. Reorder control is Up/Down (`RowActions`), drag optional (G) |
| C5 | `POST /deals/stages/{stage_id}:archive` | public | SP2/R2.5 | SP3/R3.4 | new | Same permission and floor. Body `StageArchiveIn {move_open_deals_to: uuid \| null}`. `move_open_deals_to` is required whenever the stage holds any deal, **soft-deleted ones included**, but only live deals move: more than 2,000 live deals is 409 `too_many_deals` (a bounded lock time), and the target keeps each moved deal's own probability. Open deals move through `move_stage` semantics (one audit row and one `crm.pipeline.deal.stage_changed` each, chunked, each event carrying `reason = 'stage_archived'`, EV8). Those are system moves, not a rep's decision, so notification's rule and automation fan-out **skips** a `stage_changed` whose `reason` is `stage_archived` (a tenant automation such as "deal enters negotiation, send a WhatsApp" must not fire for every moved deal: P§3 F3), while insights and every other consumer still see the move. The filter deploys in notification **before** this route is enabled (the consumer-first rule of §D); a won/lost stage is archive-only and closed deals are never touched; refused (409) if it would leave no active open, won or lost stage. Answers `StageArchiveOut {stage, moved_deals}`; archiving a stage that is already archived is a 200 no-op. Audit `pipeline.stage_archived`; event `crm.pipeline.stage.archived` |
| C6 | `GET /deals` | public | SP2/R2.3, SP3/R3.2 | SPA lists, Overview, customer 360 | changed | `crm.pipeline.deal.read`. Existing params `q`, `stage`, `owner_id`, `company_id`, `page`, `size` (`deal/app/api/routes.py:78-96`). R2.3 adds `outcome`; R3.2 adds the shared list vocabulary (C35); R3.6 adds `attention` |
| C7 | `DealOut` additive fields | response model | SP2/R2.3, SP3/R3.1, R3.6, R3.8, SP4/R4.6-A | SPA, customer 360 (`c1_360.py`), visual fixtures | changed | R2.3: `stage_label`, `stage_outcome` (`open`, `won`, `lost`), `closed_at`, and `stage_position` and `stage_is_last_open` (the 360's "hot" rule becomes `probability >= 70 or stage_is_last_open`, identical to today's on the default pipeline, and needs neither a key literal nor a second call in its two-second fan-out). R3.1: `number`, `close_reason_key`, `close_note`. R3.6: `attention: string[]` (`stale`, `overdue`, `closing_soon`, computed from B27 and B65). R3.8: `tags`. R4.6: `marketing_campaign_id`, `utm_*`. Existing fields frozen (`services/deal/tests/test_authz.py:255-266` pins the exact key set and must change on purpose; `frontend/opsgrids-visual/fixtures/api/crm/deals*.json` must carry every new field or `contracts.test.ts` fails). `owner_id` is a membership id after R2.7 |
| C8 | `POST /deals/{deal_id}/stage` | public | SP2/R2.3, SP3/R3.5 | SPA board and detail | changed | `crm.pipeline.deal.write` + `assert_can_touch`. Body `StageMoveIn {stage, probability?}` (`deal/app/schemas.py:120`) gains `close_reason_id?`, `close_note?` (R3.5; required in the UI on a won or lost move). Unknown stage stays **422** (`ValidationProblem` with a `stage` field error), and a move into an archived stage is 422 `stage_archived`. R2.3's one deliberate behaviour change: a closing move whose `probability` differs from the pin (won 100, lost 0) is 422 `probability_pinned`, and an omitted one means the pin (`move_stage` stores any supplied figure today, `services/deal/app/services.py:314-316`, against its own docstring, and a won deal stored at 37 percent corrupts every rollup). Moving into a closed outcome sets `closed_at`; a closed deal moves nowhere (409), unchanged |
| C9 | `GET /deals/summary` | public | SP2/R2.3, SP3/R3.2 | Overview, ADR-0020 parity test | changed | `crm.pipeline.deal.read`. Rollups key on `outcome` instead of the literal six (`domain.py` win rate, won value, funnel). Legacy fields `pipeline_value_minor`, `weighted_pipeline_minor`, `open_deals`, `win_rate_pct`, `won_value_minor`, `activities_this_week`, `currency`, `trend`, `funnel` are **frozen** (contract tests, visual fixtures, ADR-0020's parity test); `funnel[]` gains `outcome` only (`label` already exists: `StageRollupOut {stage, label, count, value_minor, pct}`, `services/deal/app/schemas.py:221-230`), and `StageRollupOut.stage` becomes a free key string because the six-value `DealStage` literal (`services/deal/app/schemas.py:37`) is replaced by the key pattern of EV6; R3.2 adds per-currency splitting additively. `owner=me` resolves through `own_ref` (A6), not `subject_ref` |
| C10 | `GET /deals/reports/forecast`, `GET /deals/reports/win-loss` | public | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.8, SP4/R4.6 (insights adoption) | new | `crm.pipeline.deal.read` (reports ride the existing read: they aggregate rows the caller can already list; registered default, no `report.read` code). Query, forecast: `period` (`month` default, `quarter`), `periods` (1 to 12, default 6), `owner` (`me`, `team`), `owner_id`, `tz`; win-loss: `from` and `to` (dates in `tz`, default the last 90 days; `from` after `to`, or a range over 366 days, is 422 `invalid_report_range`), `outcome` (`won`, `lost`), `owner`, `owner_id`, `tz`. Responses `ForecastOut {period, periods, as_of, tz, currencies: [{currency, overdue, buckets[], totals}]}` and `WinLossOut {from, to, tz, currencies: [{currency, won_count, lost_count, won_value_minor, lost_value_minor, win_rate_pct, reasons[], owners[]}]}`. Per currency, never summed across currencies; win/loss grouped by `close_reasons.key` |
| C11 | `GET /deals/settings`, `PUT /deals/settings` | public | SP3/R3.4 | SP3/R3.6, R3.8; SP4/R4.2, R4.4, R4.5 | new | Read: `crm.pipeline.deal.read`. Write: `crm.pipeline.settings.write`, `minimum=Scope.ORG`, one audit row per `PUT`. Body and response are the B27 columns |
| C12 | `GET /deals/close-reasons`, `POST /deals/close-reasons`, `PATCH /deals/close-reasons/{reason_id}`, `POST /deals/close-reasons/{reason_id}:archive` | public | SP3/R3.4 | SP3/R3.5, R3.8 | new | Read `crm.pipeline.deal.read` (query `outcome`, `include_archived`); writes `crm.pipeline.settings.write`, ORG floor. Body `{outcome, key, label, position?}`; `key` immutable once created. A read never writes: while the defaults are virtual (B24) the list answers them with their deterministic ids and `materialised: false` |
| C13 | `GET /deals/follow-ups`, `POST /deals/follow-ups`, `GET /deals/follow-ups/{follow_up_id}`, `PATCH /deals/follow-ups/{follow_up_id}`, `POST /deals/follow-ups/{follow_up_id}:complete`, `POST /deals/follow-ups/{follow_up_id}:cancel` | public | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6, R3.8; SP4/R4.7 | new | Read `crm.pipeline.follow_up.read`; writes `crm.pipeline.follow_up.write` + `assert_can_touch(owner_id=...)`; handing a follow-up to another rep is a non-OWN act (as `log_activity`, `deal/app/api/routes.py:240-243`). List params `owner=me`, `owner_id`, `status`, `due_before`, `due_after`, `deal_id`, `company_id`, `contact_id`, `lead_id`, `page`, `size`. `FollowUpOut {id, owner_id, title, due_at, status, completed_at, deal_id, company_id, contact_id, lead_id, origin, overdue: bool}`. Create and reschedule arm the scheduler; complete, cancel and reschedule cancel by key (A16) |
| C14 | `GET /deals/follow-ups/summary` | public | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6 (my follow-ups tile, Overview) | new | `crm.pipeline.follow_up.read`; `{due_today, overdue, upcoming_7d}`, disjoint, for the caller's scope narrowed by query `owner` (`me`), `owner_id` and `tz` (a "my follow-ups" tile needs mine, not the whole scope); counts only. A static path declared **before** `GET /deals/follow-ups/{follow_up_id}`, the way `GET /deals/summary` is declared before `/{deal_id}` |
| C15 | `GET /deals/{deal_id}/history` | public | SP3/R3.5 (the deal variant, moved forward from R3.8 because `move_stage` already writes `deal.stage_changed` audit rows with the stage before and after and a second source of truth would disagree with them), SP3/R3.8 (the company, contact and lead variants and every History tab) | SP3/R3.5 | new | `crm.pipeline.deal.read` + `assert_can_touch`; reads `audit_logs` rows for the entity (no new table), newest first, paged (`page`, `size` default 25, at most 100); same route shape on `/companies/{id}/history`, `/contacts/{id}/history`, `/leads/{id}/history` (C32, C43). **The response is an allow-listed projection, never the stored row**: `HistoryEntryOut {id, at, actor_id, actor_membership_id, action, changed_fields: string[], before, after}`. `actor_membership_id` is lifted out of the audit row's `after`, where every CRM audit row carries it from the release that ships the variant (R3.5 in deal, R3.8 in customer and lead) whenever the principal has a membership, and is never returned inside `after`; `actor_id` is the user id, which no roster maps to a name, and an older row shows "A teammate". `before` and `after` are returned for **deals and companies only** and only for an allow-list of non-personal business fields (deal: `stage`, `probability`, `value_minor`, `currency`, `close_date`, `owner_id`, `closed_at`, `close_reason_key`; company: `status`, `owner_id`, `industry`), every other field appearing by name in `changed_fields` alone. For **contacts and leads no before or after value is ever returned**. The `action` allow-list excludes `entity.sensitive_read` (who revealed a number is a security trail, not record history), `contact.messaging_target_resolved` and `*.phone_set`. Reason: customer's audit rows hold plaintext email and full name in `contact.created` and `contact.updated` (`services/customer/app/services.py:685, 752-753`) and the reveal trail in `entity.sensitive_read` (`:946`), so returning rows as stored would hand both to anyone with the plain read permission; B12 also removes those values from the stored rows |
| C16 | `GET /deals/views`, `POST /deals/views`, `PATCH /deals/views/{view_id}`, `DELETE /deals/views/{view_id}` | public | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.3 (saved-view primitive), R3.4 | new | `crm.pipeline.deal.read` (a view is a stored query, needs no write code); `visibility: org` requires an ORG-scope read grant. Body `{name, filters, sort?, visibility}` (B14). Same shape on `/companies/views`, `/contacts/views`, `/leads/views` |
| C17 | `GET /activities`, `POST /activities` | public alias | SP3/R3.2 | SPA, Engage panel | new | Same handlers and permissions as `GET/POST /deals/activities` (`deal/app/api/routes.py:194-270`: `crm.pipeline.activity.read/write`); `/deals/activities` stays and is **alias-frozen**: besides the SPA, `frontend/opsgrids-dashboard/src/api/index.ts:388` (`listDealActivities`) calls it. Gateway prefix `/api/v1/activities` added to both tables (C68). R3.2 gives the list the query `owner_id`, `owner` (`me`), `occurred_from`, `occurred_to` and `tz`, ordered by `occurred_at desc, id desc`. R4.2 adds `origin`, `ref_type`, `ref_id` to `ActivityOut` and types `message`, `ticket` |
| C18 | `GET /deals/{deal_id}/quotes`, `POST /deals/{deal_id}/quotes`, `GET /deals/quotes/{quote_id}`, `PATCH /deals/quotes/{quote_id}`, `PUT /deals/quotes/{quote_id}/lines`, `GET /deals/quotes/{quote_id}/pdf` | public | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5, R4.6 | new | Read `crm.pipeline.quote.read`, write `crm.pipeline.quote.write` + `assert_can_touch` through the deal; PATCH and lines only while `draft`; a line may carry `inventory_item_id` only for an organization entitled to Operations, which picks it through C87 (otherwise lines are free text, B29); `pdf` checks scope, writes the audit row, asks document to presign (C75) and answers a 302 to the 60-second URL (after R19) |
| C19 | `POST /deals/quotes/{quote_id}:submit`, `:send`, `:accept`, `:reject` | public | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 | new | `crm.pipeline.quote.write`. `:submit` compares `discount_bps` with `deal.pipeline_settings.discount_approval_threshold_bps`: above it, status `pending_approval` and an approval request of type `crm.pipeline.discount` is raised through the approval engine (pending D-18): deal publishes EV23 (ids and numbers only), and approval's consumer reads the quote summary and the rep's `discount_reason` through C78 **before** opening its own transaction; at or below, `approved`. `:submit` takes `discount_reason?` (required above the threshold; stored on the quote, B28, and never carried by an event or an audit row). The deal may not reach a won outcome with an unapproved discount. `:accept` moves the deal to the org's first won stage |
| C20 | `POST /deals/quotes/{quote_id}:handoff` | public | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 | new | `crm.pipeline.quote.write` + `assert_can_touch` through the deal, with `minimum=Scope.ORG` when `target` is `order` or `invoice`. **Why a floor:** the callee's internal route proves which *service* called (`deal`), never that the human may create an order or an invoice, so without it a rep holding only `crm.pipeline.quote.write` at OWN could cause finance or operations records to be created under deal's service token; authorization is server-side and a hidden button protects nothing. `target = 'project'` keeps the OWN reading **only** while `deal.pipeline_settings.create_project_on_won` is true (the organization opted in), otherwise it needs ORG as well. The callee also re-checks that the organization is entitled to **its own** product (finance, operations, pulseboard or projects) through product-service's entitlements read, because an internal route is never behind the gateway's entitlement gate. A dedicated code `crm.pipeline.quote.handoff` for tenants that want a lower scope to hand off is the alternative, not registered (L21). Body `{target: "order" \| "invoice" \| "project", idempotency_key}`; calls the callee's internal route (C64) with claim/call/record, never inside a transaction; records the callee's id as `ref_type`/`ref_id` on a system activity (B31). **Payment links do not ride this route:** collection is staff-assisted online collection on an issued invoice, the finance route C103 (a customer-facing pay page is D-30, default deferred); the handoff `target` stays `order`, `invoice` or `project`. When finance's totals differ from the quote's, C64 returns `quote_total_delta_minor`, finance's totals win and the delta is audited. **Negative tests, all five:** no token (401); wrong caller (403); wrong organization claim (403); an organization not entitled to the callee's product (refused, not created); a caller below the floor (403) |
| C81 | `POST /deals/attachments` | public | SP4/R4.5 (after R19) | SP4/R4.5 | new (registered default) | The upload half of the notes-attachments promise (P§6). `crm.pipeline.activity.write` + `assert_can_touch` through the activity's deal. `multipart/form-data` with `activity_id` and `file`, on a **static path**: the route table matches by prefix, so an upload route with a variable segment could not carry its own body cap (C83). There is **no browser upload to the private bucket** (ADR-0009 Q1: uploads come from the product, server-side), so deal receives the bytes and runs claim/call/record: claim re-checks scope; call asks document's server-side upload route (its name is R19's, as C75) to store the object, **outside any transaction**; record inserts B57 and an audit row `activity.attachment_added` (ids only, never the file name). document's allow-list on that route gains `deal` (C75). Refused with 413 above the gateway cap and 415 outside the allow-listed content types. Negative tests: no `crm.pipeline.activity.write` (403), an activity outside the caller's scope (404), another organization's activity (404) |
| C82 | `GET /deals/attachments/{attachment_id}` | public | SP4/R4.5 (after R19) | SP4/R4.5 | new (registered default) | `crm.pipeline.activity.read` + `assert_can_touch` through the owning activity's deal; writes an audit row (`activity.attachment_read`, ids only), asks document to presign (C75) and answers a **302 to the 60-second URL** (ADR-0009 Q1: the owning product authorizes and audits, document only presigns). The URL is never stored and never returned in a body. Negative tests as C81 |
| C87 | `GET /deals/quote-items:search?q=&limit=` | public | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 (the quote composer, G18) | new (registered default) | `crm.pipeline.quote.write`, organization filter only (reference data, like `GET /customers/owners`, so no `ctx.apply`), `Entitled` for `crm`. A static path declared before `/{deal_id}`. It calls operations' C80 server-side under deal's service token, outside any transaction, and returns the same trimmed item list; answers **404** (not an empty list) when the organization is not entitled to Operations, so the composer cannot confuse "no match" with "no catalogue". The SPA never calls an internal route, and a CRM-only rep needs no `operations.*` permission. Negative tests: no `crm.pipeline.quote.write` (403), an organization without Operations (404) |
| C91 | `POST /deals/quotes/{quote_id}:revise` | public | SP4/R4.5-A | SP4/R4.5-A | new | `crm.pipeline.quote.write` + `assert_can_touch` through the deal. Versioning: from a `sent`, `rejected`, `expired` or `approved` quote it creates a new `draft` row with `version + 1` (B28); sending the new version marks the previous one `superseded` and cancels its expiry job (A16). History is never edited. A discount rejected in approval returns the quote to `draft`, and the rep revises and resubmits (a resubmission opens a new approval request, B28) |
| C92 | `POST /deals/{deal_id}:create-project` | public | SP4/R4.4 | SP4/R4.4 | new | The manual recovery for the won-deal kickoff (EV8). `crm.pipeline.deal.write` + `assert_can_touch`, with `minimum=Scope.ORG` unless `deal.pipeline_settings.create_project_on_won` is true (the rule of C20 for `target = project`); body `{idempotency_key}`; claim, then the C64 call outside any transaction, then record a system activity (B31: `activity_type = 'note'`, `origin = 'system'`, `ref_type = 'project'`, subject "Project created", no body) and an audit row. 409 `deal_not_won` unless the deal's stage outcome is `won`; 404 for a foreign or out-of-scope deal; 503 `project_unavailable`. A caller below the floor, an organization not entitled to PulseBoard and a callee that refuses each have a named negative test |

### C.2 `customer` public routes (gateway prefixes `/api/v1/customers`, `/api/v1/contacts`, plus `/api/v1/companies` from R3.2)

| # | Route | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Permission; key request and response fields |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C21 | `GET\|POST /companies`, `GET\|PATCH\|DELETE /companies/{company_id}` | public, canonical | SP3/R3.2 | SPA, SP4 (company picker) | new | Same handlers, permissions (`crm.book.company.read/write/delete`) and schemas as the `/customers` routes (`customer/app/api/routes.py:136-293`). `/customers`, `/customers/{id}` stay as an alias (frozen: the SPA, Engage's `Customer360Panel`, the dashboard's `countCrmCustomers`, which calls `GET /customers?status=customer&size=1` at `frontend/opsgrids-dashboard/src/api/index.ts:382`, `e2e/edge_product_crud.py:337-350` and the seeds call them). The router is mounted under both prefixes; the gateway gets `/api/v1/companies` (C67) |
| C22 | `GET\|POST /companies/{company_id}/tax-ids`, `PATCH\|DELETE /companies/{company_id}/tax-ids/{tax_id}`, `GET\|POST /companies/{company_id}/addresses`, `PATCH\|DELETE /companies/{company_id}/addresses/{address_id}` | public | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.4 (company page), SP4/R4.1 | new | `crm.book.company.read` / `crm.book.company.write` + `assert_can_touch(owner_id=company.owner_id)`. Tax-id body `{kind, value, state_code?, label?, is_primary?}` where `kind` has the single value `gstin` (a `pan` kind or field is a 422: no PAN is stored, B7); `value` is verified with A15 and a bad checksum is a 422 field error; duplicate GSTIN in the org is a 409 naming the existing company id (the `existing_id` key of A21); `TaxIdOut` carries `state_name`, derived from `GST_STATE_CODES` (A15) and read-only |
