Response `Customer360 {customer, crm, orders, finance, support, channels, timeline, unavailable}` (`customer/app/c1_schemas.py:105-113`); `SectionName` (`:17`) is `crm, orders, finance, support, channels` and gains `delivery` in R4.4. A section that cannot be produced is `null` and named in `unavailable`; `customer.tags` is filled from B13 in R3.8. R1.2a also fixes the deal leg: `services/customer/app/c1_360.py:167` calls `{deal_internal_url}/deals` while routers mount under `/api/v1` (`app.py:363`), and its test asserts the same wrong path (`services/customer/tests/test_c1_customer360.py:154`). The fix is `{deal_internal_url}/api/v1/deals`, proved by a mount-aware real-path test (the fake upstream must route under the same `/api/v1` prefix the real app mounts), plus a same-class audit of every other internal client (lead, finance, notification), whose one required change is C77 |
| C24 | `GET /companies/{company_id}/360` | public | SP4/R4.3 | SP3/R3.4 (company page tabs) | new (registered default) | `crm.book.company.read` + `assert_can_touch`; party-level counterpart of C23 built from `party_rollup`, `party_timeline`, contacts, deals and the support and delivery sections; money section additionally needs `crm.book.company.read_commercial` or it is `null` and named in `unavailable` |
| C25 | `GET /customers/owners` | public | SP2/R2.6 | SPA directory, lead (via internal route) | changed | `crm.book.company.read`, organization-scoped, no `ctx.apply`, plain array (`customer/app/api/routes.py:182-193`). `OwnerOut {id, full_name, email, role, is_active}` (`services/customer/app/schemas.py:79-87`) gains `membership_id: str \| null`. After R2.7 the SPA resolves any `owner_id` by `membership_id` first and by `id` second (B3) |
| C26 | `POST /customers/owners` | public | SP2/R2.6 | SPA Team screen | changed | `crm.book.owner.write`, ORG (`services/customer/app/api/routes.py:196-209`). `OwnerIn {full_name, email, role}` (`services/customer/app/schemas.py:50-58`) gains `membership_id: uuid \| null`; with it, customer verifies the membership through the account bulk read (C57, outside any transaction) and copies name and email. Activation errors (R2.6): 409 `already_activated` (the membership already holds a roster row), 409 `owner_email_exists` (naming that roster row; the owner links it with the mapping tool instead of creating a second person), 404 `membership_not_found`, 422 `membership_not_active`, and 503 `upstream-unavailable` with nothing written when account cannot answer. Free-form creation without `membership_id` is refused with 422 `membership_required` once R2.7 is live; the one-click "make me the owner" path (`frontend/product-crm/src/forms/OwnerPicker.tsx`) and employee-id fallbacks retire in R2.9. `e2e/edge_product_crud.py:337-350` (the production smoke creates an owner with only `{full_name, email}` and uses the returned roster id as `owner_id` for the company, contact and deal) is unchanged through R2.6 and **rewritten in R2.7** to the activation path: read C27, `POST /customers/owners` with a `membership_id`, and use that owner's **membership id** as `owner_id`. Left alone it fails at its first step the moment the 422 lands, and it is the owner's verify step after every deploy (the owner runs it; agents never do, §H) |
| C27 | `GET /customers/owners/candidates` | public | SP2/R2.6 | SP3/R3.4 (Team screen) | new | `crm.book.owner.write`, ORG. Org members not yet activated as reps: `{membership_id, full_name, email, status, is_owner, manager_membership_id}`, assembled from the account bulk read (C57). Declared before `/customers/owners/{owner_id}` |
| C28 | `PATCH /customers/owners/{owner_id}` | public | SP4/R4.7 (the widened guard) | SPA | exists, changed in R4.7 | `crm.book.owner.write`; deactivation refuses while the rep owns live companies or contacts (`customer/app/services.py:290`); R4.7 widens the guard to open deals and open leads through C94 (customer sends **both ids** of the roster entry and the callee counts rows owned by either, because after R2.7 a mapped row's `owner_id` is a membership id while legacy and unmapped rows keep the roster id), answers 503 when a count is unavailable, and stages EV25 |
| C29 | `POST /contacts/{contact_id}:erase` | public | SP4/R4.1 | SP4/R4.2, R4.4 | new | `crm.book.contact.erase`, `minimum=Scope.ORG`, `amr={"otp", "stepup"}` (the reveal guard's pairing, `customer/app/api/routes.py:79-80`), one audit row (ids and counts only); anonymises in place (B12, **including customer's own audit copies of the contact's name, email and phone**) and publishes `crm.book.contact.erased` (EV18); idempotent; 409 never needed (erasing an erased contact returns 200). Design in the consent and erasure ADR (§I) |
| C30 | `POST /contacts:lookup-channels` | public | SP4/R4.2 | SP3/R3.4 (list rows), R3.5 | new | `crm.book.contact.read`; body `{contact_ids: uuid[]}` (<= 100), each id re-checked with `assert_can_touch`; customer calls notification's batch route (C59, `POST /internal/v1/contacts:summarize-messaging`) once, 2 s budget, per-item `null` on failure, so list rows show consent badges without N+1 calls. Response `{items: {<contact_id>: ChannelsSection \| null}}` |
| C31 | List query vocabulary | request parameters of the four list routes | SP3/R3.2 | SP3/R3.4, R3.8; SP4/R4.6 | changed | See table C35 below |
| C32 | `GET /companies/{company_id}/history`, `GET /contacts/{contact_id}/history`, `GET /companies/views` (and `/contacts/views`) with the C16 verbs | public | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.5 | new | As C15 (including its projection and action allow-list: **contact history never returns before or after values**) and C16, with `crm.book.company.read` and `crm.book.contact.read` |
| C33 | Contact create and update | request and response | SP3/R3.1 | SP3/R3.4, SP4/R4.1 | changed | `ContactIn.email` and `ContactPatch.email` optional (B9); `raw_phone` stays the only way to supply a number; `ContactOut` gains `tags` (R3.8), `erased: bool` (R4.1); a contact needs an email or a phone (422 otherwise); `phone` mask remains read-only |
| C76 | `owner_id` as a request value | request semantics on every body that carries it | SP2/R2.7 | SP3/R3.4-R3.8 (every form and import), SP4 | changed | After R2.7 `owner_id` in `CompanyIn/Patch`, `ContactIn/Patch`, `DealIn/Patch`, `LeadIn/Patch`, `ActivityIn` and `assignment-rule` `owner_ids` is a **membership id**. Customer's `_assert_owner_assignable` (`services/customer/app/services.py:187-206`) resolves it through `owners.membership_id` (an OWN caller may assign only to themselves, as today); lead validates through `owners:validate` (C49); deal validates nothing, as today (`deal/app/api/internal.py:14-22` says why), and strays are reported by `tools/map_crm_owners.py verify`. `owner_id` omitted on a deal or activity defaults to `own_ref` (A6), and a caller whose `own_ref` is `None` gets a 422 (the existing no-owner refusals, `deal/app/api/routes.py:114,245`) instead of a silent employee id |
| C34 | Tags | field and list filter, **no routes** | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.2 (filter), R3.4, R3.5 | new | `tags: string[]` on the create and patch bodies and the responses of companies, contacts, deals and leads (B13), written under the entity's own write permission; list filter `tag` (C35). No tag catalogue and no tag routes: a free-form lower-case list keeps filtering inside each service (a tag table could not be joined across schemas) |
| C86 | `POST /contacts:search` | public | SP3/R3.2 | SP3/R3.4 (contact list search box) | new (registered default) | `crm.book.contact.read` + `ctx.apply`. Body `{email?, phone?}`, at least one (else 422 `needs_identifier`); an exact match on the normalised address through B10's `email_index` (canonical index first, then exact), or on the phone through the existing `contacts.phone_index`, returning `Page[ContactOut]` for the caller's scope. POST so the address stays out of URLs and their logs (G12); the replacement for the legacy email match in C35's `q`, which stays until a later contractive release. Needs H8 to have indexed the older contacts (B10) |
| C106 | `GET /companies/settings`, `PUT /companies/settings` (the `/customers/settings` alias comes with the shared router) | public | SP4/R4.3 | SP4/R4.3 (the settings hub) | new | The organization's own switch for automatic lifecycle promotion (D-34). `GET`: `crm.book.company.read` at any scope. `PUT`: `crm.book.company.write` with `minimum=Scope.ORG`, because an OWN holder cannot change what promotes every company in the organization. `CustomerSettingsOut {lifecycle_promotion: "off" \| "first_order" \| "first_commercial_event", materialised}`; with no row the defaults are answered with `materialised: false` and a `GET` never writes; `PUT` upserts the one row (B83), writes the audit row `customer_settings.updated` (before and after, an enum) and no event; any other value is 422. Declared before `/{company_id}` |

**C35. List query vocabulary (SP3/R3.2; applies to `GET /companies`, `/contacts`, `/deals`, `/leads`).** Unknown
parameters are ignored as today; unknown `sort` keys are a 422. Pagination (`page`, `size` <= 200) and the `Page[T]` response
are unchanged.

| Parameter | Lists | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `q` | all | text search. Companies: name, industry, city (exists). Contacts: name, title, email (exists, and a **documented legacy exception**: an email typed into `?q=` lands in gateway and proxy access logs, which is the reason leads search by POST, and `services/customer/app/selectors.py:272` is the match. It stays for existing callers until a later contractive release; new code looks an address up through `POST /contacts:search`, C86). Deals: name (exists). Leads: **name and company only**: email and phone are sealed, and identifier lookups go through `POST /leads:check-duplicates` because gateway and proxy logs retain URLs |
| `sort` | all | one key, `-` prefix for descending, always tie-broken by `id` so pages are stable. Companies `name`, `created_at`, `status`, `annual_revenue`; contacts `name`, `created_at`, `last_activity_at`; deals `name`, `value`, `close_date`, `created_at`, `closed_at`, `probability`, `stage`; leads `created_at`, `name`, `status` |
| `owner_id`, `owner` | all | `owner_id` exists on deals and leads (`deal/app/api/routes.py:88`, `lead/app/api/routes.py:171`), added to companies and contacts; `owner=me` generalises the existing summary parameter and resolves server-side from `own_ref`; `owner=team` (R3.8) resolves on the server to `owner_id = own_ref OR owner_manager_id = own_ref` (A8's reading of TEAM), and an absent `own_ref` yields an empty page, never the book; leads also accept `owner=unassigned` (R3.7a) |
| `status` | companies, contacts, leads | exists; deals use `stage` (key) and `outcome` (`open`, `won`, `lost`) |
| `source` | leads, deals | leads: the channel enum (B33); deals: the free-text `source` |
| `created_from`, `created_to` | all | inclusive dates, interpreted in the organization's timezone |
| `close_from`, `close_to` | deals | expected close date range |
| `attention` | deals | `stale`, `overdue`, `closing_soon`, from `deal.pipeline_settings` (R3.6) |
| `tag` | all | repeatable, AND semantics (B13) |
| `ids` | all | comma-separated UUIDs, at most 100, only rows inside the caller's scope: the by-id resolution that replaces the 200-row directory and the 200-card board cap |
| `sla`, `aging`, `duplicate`, `capture_endpoint_id` | leads (R3.7a) | `sla` accepts `breached`, `due_soon`, `ok`, `met`, `missed` (computed at read time from `lead_settings`, B37); `aging` accepts `aged`; `duplicate` accepts `true` (possible duplicates, B62); `capture_endpoint_id` is the form or webhook that produced the lead (B34) |
| `marketing_campaign_id` | deals and leads (R4.6) | one campaign id (B30, B32): "leads from this campaign" |
| `q` on `GET /operations/orders` | the operations orders list, not a CRM list (R4.6) | an order number prefix or the buyer's name, never a phone, for the shell palette's Orders group (G23) |

### C.3 `lead` public routes (gateway prefix `/api/v1/leads`)

| # | Route | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Permission; key request and response fields |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C36 | `GET /leads`, `LeadIn`, `LeadOut` | list, request and response models | SP3/R3.1, R3.2, R3.7a | SPA | changed | `crm.lead.lead.read`. List params from C35 (new: `q`, `source`, `created_from/to`, `tag`, `ids`, `sort`). `LeadIn` and `LeadOut` gain `marketing_campaign_id`, `utm_*` (R3.1) and `tags` (R3.8). In R3.7a the list also takes `owner=unassigned`, `sla`, `aging`, `capture_endpoint_id` and `duplicate` (C35), and `LeadOut` gains `first_response_at`, `last_touched_at`, `sla`, `aged`, `capture_endpoint_id`, `capture_endpoint_name`, `gstin`, `consent_evidence`, `capture_message_count`, `possible_duplicate` (always present), `duplicate_of_lead_id` (only when that lead is inside the reader's `ctx.apply` scope, one batched id lookup per page, else null) and `duplicate_contact_id` (only for a caller holding `crm.book.contact.read` at ORG, else null: B62); `source` gains `webhook`, `whatsapp` and `storefront` (B33). The visitor's own messages are never in a list row (C109) |
| C37 | `POST /leads:reassign` | public | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.7a | new | `crm.lead.lead.write`, `minimum=Scope.TEAM`; body `{lead_ids: uuid[] (<= 200), owner_id}`; each lead re-checked with `assert_can_touch`; leads with a live conversion claim are skipped and reported. Response `{updated: int, skipped: [{lead_id, reason}]}`; each reassignment emits `crm.lead.assigned` (EV15: its **producer is this release**; the notification rule and the subscribable key are R4.2). Until R3.8 wires TEAM (A14) and its reconcile widens managers to TEAM (E.2 †), only an ORG-scope holder passes the floor |
| C38 | `POST /leads:check-duplicates` | public | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.7a | new | `crm.lead.lead.write`; body `{email?, phone?}`, at least one (else 422 `needs_identifier`; POST so identifiers stay out of URLs); matches this service's blind indexes (the lead half is cut by `ctx.apply`) and calls customer `contacts:resolve` (C46) with plaintext. **The contact half runs only when the caller holds `crm.book.contact.read` at ORG** (`ctx.principal.scope_for(...)` is `Scope.ORG`); the route has no floor of its own because it returns only what the caller could already list (P§7: a customer-granted OWN rung cannot widen it). Response `{leads: [{id, status, owner_id}], contacts: [{id}], contacts_checked: bool}`, ids only. Without the ORG contact read the customer call is never made and the answer is `contacts: []`, `contacts_checked: false`, which says nothing about whether a contact exists. No `company_id` is returned, because `contacts:resolve` answers a contact id only: the page resolves the company through the scoped `ids=` read |
| C39 | `GET /leads/capture-endpoints`, `POST /leads/capture-endpoints`, `PATCH /leads/capture-endpoints/{endpoint_id}`, `DELETE /leads/capture-endpoints/{endpoint_id}`, `POST /leads/capture-endpoints/{endpoint_id}:rotate-secret` | public | SP3/R3.7b | SP3/R3.7b (the Sources page of the settings hub, which R3.4's hub does not yet list) | new | Read `crm.lead.lead.read`; writes `crm.lead.settings.write`, ORG floor. **A webhook's `signing_secret` is returned once**, at create and at rotate, and no read ever returns it. **A form's token is public by design** (it sits in the page source) and is re-derivable from the endpoint with no stored secret (B36), so every read returns it as `endpoint_token`; it is permanent, so a form cannot be rotated (422 `kind_not_rotatable`: delete it and create another). The list and detail reads also carry `success_url`, `consent_text_hash` (B63) and, per item, `blocked_24h` and `discarded_24h` (posts refused by a spent budget and honeypot posts discarded in the last 24 hours), and on the list `org_blocked_24h` and `capture_configured`; the counters are Redis reads, `null` when Redis cannot answer, and a failed counter never fails the read. `capture_configured` is false while the server has no valid signing secret (F12), `endpoint_token` is then null and create answers 422 `capture_not_configured`. Create and rotate are R3.7b |
| C40 | `GET /leads/settings`, `PUT /leads/settings` | public | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.4 | new | Read `crm.lead.lead.read`; write `crm.lead.settings.write`, ORG floor; B37 columns. `PUT` validates `lead_settings` (SLA `null` or 5 to 43200 minutes, aging 1 to 365) and writes one audit row `lead_settings.updated`; a `GET` with no row answers the defaults with `materialised: false` and never writes. R4.7 adds `timezone` to the body (an IANA name checked against `zoneinfo`; 422 `timezone_invalid`, B76). The assignment rule routes `GET\|PUT /leads/assignment-rule` (`lead/app/api/routes.py:131-160`) keep `crm.lead.lead.read` / `crm.lead.lead.write` with the service-level ORG check (`lead/app/services.py:181-182`); round-robin gains "skip inactive members" in R3.7a and, in R4.7, "skip a rep on approved leave" (B75), whose one visible field is `unavailable_owner_ids: uuid[] \| null` on `AssignmentRuleOut` (the rotation members on approved leave today, **only for a caller whose scope is ORG**; `null` for any narrower scope, because an absence is a colleague's personal fact) |
| C41 | `POST /leads/public/capture` | public, anonymous | SP3/R3.7b | SP3/R3.7b (embedded forms) | new | No principal. Exact path with **no path variable**: the form token travels in the body as `form_token` (gateway rule, §1 and `services/app-gateway/app/main.py:36-63`). **How lead learns the organization:** the gateway forwards no organization on a public path (that branch writes at most `x-org-slug`, from the host: `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/gateway.py:586-609`), and lead's tables are `FORCE` row-level security under a `NOBYPASSRLS` role, so the token carries it. `form_token` is `<organization_id>.<endpoint_id>.<mac>` (base64url parts; `mac` is HMAC-SHA256 of the first two parts under lead's `capture_token_secret`, F12, B36). Lead verifies the MAC in constant time **before any database work**, then binds the tenant GUC from the verified organization for that transaction and loads the endpoint (`is_active`, `allowed_origins`) under RLS: there is no cross-tenant lookup and no directory role (the storefront precedent needs `opsgrids_storefront_directory`, a `BYPASSRLS` role with a boot assertion, ADR-0010 §4; lead needs none of it). Per-IP anonymous limit; the gateway skips the entitlement gate on public paths, so lead checks activation itself (L12); body `{form_token, full_name, email?, phone?, company?, gstin?, message?, consent?: {granted: bool, text_hash}, utm_*?, marketing_campaign_id?}` (`consent` is required when the endpoint has a `consent_text` and is stored as evidence only, B39; `gstin` is B60); sealed at the edge; never echoes whether an address already exists (200 either way, to prevent enumeration). **Content types and abuse controls (R3.7b):** besides JSON it accepts `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, a plain HTML form post (`consent_granted`, `consent_text_hash`), and answers **303 to the endpoint's `success_url`** for one; it carries a honeypot field `opsgrids_hp` (a name no autofill matches; off-screen, unlabelled, `tabindex="-1"`, `autocomplete="off"`, `aria-hidden`) whose filled posts are discarded as a normal answer; it spends four rate budgets, with anonymous and signed traffic in **separate organization buckets**; every refused request (malformed, untrusted, unknown endpoint) is the one 400 `invalid_capture_request` (A21); the visitor's `message` goes to `capture_messages` (B85), never to `notes`. ADR slug `crm-public-lead-capture` (§I) |
| C42 | `POST /webhooks/leads` | public, signed | SP3/R3.7b | SP3/R3.7b | new | In `WEBHOOK_PUBLIC_PATHS` (`services/app-gateway/app/main.py:150-157`) as `{"POST"}` and a route-table entry with `max_body_bytes` (both tables, C70). **How lead learns the organization:** the webhook branch forwards no organization and no token (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/gateway.py:557-584`: "the host says nothing about which tenant a provider is calling for"), so the sender names it. Registered default headers: `X-Opsgrids-Endpoint: <organization_id>.<endpoint_id>.<mac>` (the same token shape as C41, so the MAC is checked before any database work and the tenant GUC is bound from the verified organization), `X-Opsgrids-Timestamp` (unix seconds) and `X-Opsgrids-Signature` (HMAC-SHA256 over `<timestamp>.` followed by the raw body, with the endpoint's sealed secret, loaded under RLS). **Replay:** the timestamp must be within 5 minutes of the server clock (stateless), and a replayed body inside that window is harmless because `external_ref` is **required** here and `leads:capture` is idempotent on `(source, external_ref)` (B35), so the replay returns the existing lead; there is no separate replay store. Body is `LeadIn`-shaped plus the required `external_ref`; an unknown endpoint, a bad MAC and a bad signature are the same 400. It spends the organization's **webhook** bucket and the endpoint's budget, never the anonymous bucket of C41 |
| C43 | `GET /leads/{lead_id}/history`, `GET\|POST /leads/views`, `PATCH\|DELETE /leads/views/{view_id}` | public | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.5 | new | As C15 (including its projection and action allow-list: **lead history never returns before or after values**) and C16, with `crm.lead.lead.read` |
| C44 | `POST /leads/{lead_id}:convert` | public | — | SPA | exists | `crm.lead.lead.convert` (`lead/app/api/routes.py:239`); behaviour changes only through A6 and the internal routes below. There is still no lead delete route; the one removal of a lead is the administrator's per-lead erase, C108 (R3.7a) |
| C88 | `GET /leads/summary` | public | SP3/R3.2 | SP3/R3.4 (the Leads list header and the Overview) | new | `crm.lead.lead.read` with `ctx.apply` (it counts only what the caller can list); `LeadSummaryOut {total, by_status, unassigned}`. A static path declared before `GET /leads/{lead_id}`. It replaces five `size=1` list calls |
| C108 | `POST /leads/{lead_id}:erase` | public | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.7a (the lead page's Erase action) | new | The means of removing a person's details from the CRM, and it exists **before** anything can store a stranger's (R3.7b): an administrator's act on one lead. `crm.lead.settings.write` (E22, admin templates only) with `minimum=Scope.ORG` and `amr={"otp","stepup"}`, the step-up a reveal needs, so **no new permission code** (the alternative, a dedicated `crm.lead.lead.erase`, is L23). Body `LeadEraseIn {confirm_lead_id}` (`extra="forbid"`; equal to the path id, else 422 `confirm_mismatch`); 200 `LeadEraseOut {lead_id, erased_at, audit_rows_scrubbed}`. In one transaction: refused while a conversion is live (409 `conversion_in_progress`; a converted lead may be erased, only the lead's own copy is anonymised); `full_name` becomes `Erased lead`; `company`, the sealed and masked email and phone and their indexes, `gstin`, `external_ref` and B62's two pointers become NULL, `notes` becomes empty and `capture_messages` `[]`; `deleted_at` is set; **kept** are `id`, `source`, `status`, `owner_id`, `capture_endpoint_id`, the UTM and campaign columns, `consent_evidence` (a boolean, a hash and a time: no identifier), the `converted_*` ids, the clocks and the timestamps; the audit copies are rewritten through B86; one audit row `lead.erased` (`after = {"audit_rows_scrubbed": n}`) and one event, EV53. Erasing the same lead again is a 404, and there is **no bulk erase** (junk is disqualified in bulk through the Excel bulk-update engine, ADR-0022). It does not reach free text a rep typed on a company, contact, deal or activity (the D-27 residue), a sealed number in notification's conversation (SP4's contact erase owns that) or backups. Pending D-27 |
| C109 | `GET /leads/{lead_id}/capture-messages` | public | SP3/R3.7a | SP3/R3.7a (the lead page) | new | `crm.lead.lead.read` + `assert_can_touch(owner_id=lead.owner_id)`; `{items: [{at, source, text}]}`, oldest first, plain text (B85). 403 out of scope for an OWN grant, 404 cross-tenant; the messages never appear in a list row |

### C.4 Internal routes (service token addressed by name; `/internal/v1/organizations/{organization_id}/...` unless stated)

| # | Route | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Caller allow-list; key request and response fields |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C45 | customer `GET .../contacts/{contact_id}/messaging-target?channel=` | internal | SP3/R3.1 (allow-list), SP4/R4.1 (email channel) | notification (rules and automations, SP3/R3.7 and SP4/R4.2; support-ticket notifications, SP4/R4.4) | changed | Exists (`customer/app/api/internal.py:138`) with **no** caller check and **no** org-claim check; R3.1 adds `_require_caller(request, "notification")` and `_require_org_claim` before any new service may call customer, **but only after C77 has shipped**. Notification is the only caller of this route and mints its token with **no claims** (`mint_service_token(settings, audience=CUSTOMER_AUDIENCE, caller=settings.service_name)`, `services/notification/app/targets.py:78-80`), while `_require_org_claim` answers 403 whenever `claims.get("org")` is not the path's organization (`customer/app/api/internal.py:110-121`); finance (`services/finance/app/clients.py:69-74`) and lead (`services/lead/app/clients.py:106-111`) already pass `claims={"org": ...}`. Enforcing first would 403 every Engage and automation address lookup, which `targets.py` logs and answers as "not messageable" (`:101-110`). So the order is binding: **(a)** C77 (R1.2a), notification adds the claim, additive, no behaviour change; **(b)** R3.1, customer enforces, with three negative tests (no token 401, wrong caller 403, wrong organization claim 403), **one of them using a token minted by notification's real client helper** (`targets.resolve_contact_target`) rather than a hand-built one. (b) may not ship until (a) has been deployed and has soaked (J13). R4.1: `channel=email` allowed (an allowlist beside `_PHONE_CHANNELS`, `:83`; no free-form channel list); `MessagingTargetOut {contact_id, channel, phone}` (`services/customer/app/schemas.py:278-288`) gains `email: str \| None`; the `contact.messaging_target_resolved` audit row is kept for parity even though email is plaintext. In the same commit R4.1 lifts the first of the two Engage builder rules of EV39 (drop `recipient_kind` when the channel is not WhatsApp) and the helper's WhatsApp wording; the second rule (a "Send to" path that does not end in `contact_id` is an address) stays. The rule exists only because this route refuses `email` until then |
| C46 | customer `POST .../contacts:resolve` | internal | SP3/R3.1 (email), SP4/R4.2 (ids) | notification, lead (R3.7 dedupe) | changed | Exists (`services/customer/app/api/internal.py:197`, phone only, body `ContactResolveIn {phone}` `services/customer/app/schemas.py:291-302`). R3.1: body `{phone?, email?}`, at least one, plaintext over the authenticated call, matched on the blind indexes (B10); caller allow-list `notification`, `lead` plus org claim. **Same binding order as C45:** the allow-list and the claim check ship in R3.1 only after C77 (R1.2a). Notification's `resolve_contact_by_phone` also mints with no claims (`services/notification/app/contacts.py:79-81`) and maps every non-200 to `_UNREACHABLE` (`:95-99`); `fold_inbound_contact` raises on that (`services/notification/app/consumers.py:247-258`), so with enforcement first every inbound WhatsApp message's phone-to-contact step would be redelivered once and then dead-lettered, and no conversation would ever be linked to its contact. The three negative tests include one with a token from that real helper. R4.2: response `ContactResolveOut {contact_id}` gains `company_id`, `owner_membership_id` (ids only; today it deliberately returns only the id, `services/customer/app/schemas.py:305-309`) |
| C77 | notification's outbound tokens carry the organization claim | internal-client change (no new route) | SP1/R1.2a | SP3/R3.1 (enforcement: C45, C46) | new | Notification mints three service tokens for calls under `/internal/v1/organizations/{org}/` with no claims: `resolve_contact_target` (`services/notification/app/targets.py:78-80`) and `resolve_contact_by_phone` (`services/notification/app/contacts.py:79-81`), both into customer, and its call to account (`services/notification/app/memberships.py:53-55`, the assignment-target check). R1.2a adds `claims={"org": str(organization_id)}` to **all three** `mint_service_token` calls, **additive, with no behaviour change** (customer reads no claims on its two routes until R3.1, and account's route checks only `iss` and goes on ignoring the claim, `services/account/app/api/internal.py:304-346`). The third mint is there so that the cross-service guard of the same-class audit can assert the organization claim for **every** call under that path with no exception list. A test per helper (`test_the_token_names_the_organization` in `test_targets.py`, `test_contacts.py` and `test_memberships.py`) decodes the token the client sent and asserts the `org` claim equals the organization in the URL and `iss == "notification"`, the pattern customer's own test uses in the reverse direction (`services/customer/tests/test_c1_customer360.py:168-175`). It is part of R1.2a's same-class audit of every internal client (C23). Enforcement (C45, C46) ships in a **later deploy** and never before this one is live; rolling this change back requires rolling enforcement back first (J13) |
| C47 | customer `GET .../contacts/{contact_id}` | internal | — | finance | exists | Caller `finance` (`services/customer/app/api/internal.py:244`); `ContactSummaryOut.email` becomes `str \| null` with B9 (finance must tolerate null) |
| C48 | customer `POST .../leads:convert` | internal | SP3/R3.1 | lead | changed | Caller `lead` (`services/customer/app/api/internal.py:290`). A contact is created when the lead has an email **or** a phone (today email only: `LeadConversionIn.contact_email` doc, `services/customer/app/schemas.py:327-331`); `LeadConversionOut.contact_id` is therefore non-null for a phone-only lead. Ids stay the caller's deterministic uuid5 (`lead/app/domain.py:118-145`). `LeadConversionIn` gains an optional `company_gstin` (lead stores it from R3.7, B60; storefront enquiries already capture one, `services/storefront/app/models.py:806`): conversion writes it to `customer.company_tax_ids` (B7) and, when another company already holds that GSTIN, **reuses that company** rather than matching by name alone, so B7's "GSTIN is the preferred dedupe key" holds on the lead path too |
| C49 | customer `POST .../owners:validate` | internal | SP2/R2.7 | lead | changed | Caller `lead` (`services/customer/app/api/internal.py:351`). Request and response shapes unchanged; the ids are membership ids after R2.7 and "active" means a roster row with that `membership_id` and `is_active`. Deal does not validate owners (as today); strays are reported by `tools/map_crm_owners.py verify` |
| C50 | customer `GET .../companies/{company_id}` | internal | SP4/R4.1 | finance (R4.3), operations (R4.3), project and projects (R4.4), support (R4.4), storefront (R4.7), deal | new | Callers `finance, operations, project, projects, support, storefront, deal`. `CompanySummaryOut {company_id, name, legal_name, status, owner_membership_id, tax_ids: [{kind, value, state_code, is_primary}], billing_address: {...} \| null}`; business facts only, no contact PII; 404 for a foreign or deleted id. The validate-on-write call products make instead of trusting a plain UUID. **Entitlement re-check (C50, C51, C52, C53 and C89):** the caller allow-list proves only which service called, the customer process is profile-wide and product-service answers for any organization, so each of these routes re-checks that the organization is entitled to `crm` through product-service's entitlements read (customer gains `product_service_url` and `product_timeout_seconds`, the shared `PRODUCT_SERVICE_URL` of W36, and a 60-second in-process cache) and answers **403 `crm_not_active`** otherwise, or 503 `entitlement_unavailable` when product-service cannot answer (fail closed). Each ships an unentitled-organization test beside its three negatives |
| C51 | customer `POST .../parties:ensure` | internal | SP4/R4.1 | operations (R4.3) | new | Caller **`operations` only**: an order for a walk-in is the one case in which a product creates a party (SD-7), so finance, storefront and notification, which the first draft listed, are not callers; a later caller needs an amendment that adds its `source.kind` and its allow-list entry together. Body `{company: {company_id?, name, legal_name?, gstin?, individual?: bool}, contact?: {contact_id?, full_name?, email?, phone?}, owner_membership_id?, source: {kind: "order", ref}}`; `individual: true` marks a one-person company, which is never reused by name. Idempotent by the caller's deterministic ids first (the `leads:convert` pattern, `services/customer/app/api/internal.py:290-348`), then GSTIN, company name, email and phone blind indexes; raw phone is sealed on arrival; creates phone-only contacts (B9); audit rows with `acting_as=<caller service>`. Response `{company_id, contact_id \| null, company_created: bool, contact_created: bool}`; a phone that belongs to another company's contact is 409 `contact_company_mismatch`, never a link. Callers use claim/call/record; never inside a transaction. Owner for a created party is an open question (§L). Like C50, C52, C53 and C89 it re-checks the organization's `crm` entitlement (see C50) |
| C52 | customer `POST .../parties:resolve` | internal | SP4/R4.1 | support, notification, project, projects, finance, operations, lead, storefront (R4.2-R4.7) | new | Callers `finance, operations, storefront, notification, support, project, projects, lead` (the first four are C51's first-draft list, kept here because resolving is read-only; only creating was narrowed to `operations`). Body `{email?, phone?, company_name?, company_domain?, gstin?}` (plaintext in the body, never a URL). Response `{contact_id \| null, company_id \| null, owner_membership_id \| null, ambiguous: bool}`; case-variant duplicates answer `ambiguous: true`, never a guess; ids only, no sealed read, no audit row (same posture as `contacts:resolve`) |
| C53 | customer `GET .../contacts:export` | internal | SP4/R4.1 | notification (SP4/R4.2 Engage projection and H9); finance, operations (SP4/R4.3), support (SP4/R4.4), lead, storefront (SP4/R4.7) and deal (SP4/R4.5, L19) for the one-off erase catch-up (EV18, H14) | new | Callers: **`notification`** receives full rows and may pass `ids` (at most 100); `finance`, `support`, `lead`, `operations`, `storefront` and `deal` may call it **only with `erased_only=true`** (otherwise 403 `erased_only_required`) and then receive `{contact_id, company_id, erased_at, updated_at}` and nothing else, so the catch-up hands no other service a contact's name or email. Query `after_updated_at`, `after_id`, `limit` (<= 500), `ids`, `erased_only`, keyset on `(updated_at, id)`. Rows `{contact_id, company_id, full_name, email, status, owner_membership_id, updated_at, deleted_at, erased_at}`; **no phone**, and `email` is `null` for a phone-only contact, which the projection skips (B47). The source of H9's first link and of the reconcile for the projection (events are thin triggers; an event with no bound queue is dropped, `events.py:250-262`). Same shape family as the unmerged `GET /internal/v1/insights/export/companies` (`sprint/insights-q2`: `customer/app/api/internal.py:350`) |
| C54 | deal `POST .../leads:convert` | internal | SP2/R2.3, SP4/R4.6 | lead | changed | Caller `lead` (`deal/app/api/internal.py:94`). R2.3: the entry stage is the first active open stage by position (today literal `'lead'`, `:135`); R4.6: `LeadConversionDealIn {deal_id, company_id, contact_id, owner_id, name, close_date, source}` (`deal/app/schemas.py:275-287`) gains `marketing_campaign_id?`, `utm_*?` |
| C55 | deal `GET .../references?company_id=&contact_id=&exclude_deal_id=` | internal | SP2/R2.4 | customer (NEW-9 handler) | new | Caller `customer`, org claim required. The path is `GET /internal/v1/organizations/{organization_id}/references`. Response `{company_deal_count: int, contact_deal_count: int, company_activity_count: int, contact_activity_count: int}` over **live (non-deleted) deals only** (the activity counts are of `deal.activities` rows that name the company or the contact). `exclude_deal_id` removes the orphan deal **only when it is disposable** (already deleted, or live and pristine), so the answer is identical in either consumer order: if deal kept a touched orphan, customer would otherwise delete a company that deal still points at. Counts only, no ids. Customer calls it **before** opening its transaction (the repo's I/O-outside-transactions rule, named D3 in `docs/io-outside-transactions.md:1`, enforced by `tests/infra/test_io_outside_transaction.py`) |
| C56 | lead `POST .../leads:capture` | internal | SP3/R3.7b | notification (Engage stranger to lead, R3.7b), storefront (R4.7) | new | Callers `notification`, `storefront`. New file `services/lead/app/api/internal.py` (lead has no internal router today; the insights branch also adds one, a collision to resolve at merge). Body `{source (whatsapp or storefront), external_ref, full_name, company?, gstin?, email?, phone?, message?, consent?: {granted, text_hash}, marketing_campaign_id?, utm_*?}` (`gstin` is B60, validated with A15 and dropped when invalid; `message`, when sent, goes to `capture_messages`, B85; `consent` is stored as evidence only, B39, and forwarded through C58 from R4.7 **except** that a block with `source = "storefront"` is never forwarded by lead, because storefront writes its own ledger row); idempotent on `(source, external_ref)` (B35); raw email and phone re-sealed on arrival. Response `{lead_id \| null, created: bool, duplicate_of_lead_id \| null, duplicate_contact_id \| null, skipped_reason \| null}`. **A definite "no" is a 200, not an error:** `skipped_reason` is `disabled` (source `whatsapp` and the organization's `whatsapp_leads_enabled`, B63, is off), `rate_limited` (an hourly cap, default 60) or `not_entitled`, with `lead_id` null. Lead re-checks the organization's `crm` entitlement itself, through product-service's entitlements read (L12, W36), because an internal route sits behind no entitlement gate; SP3, which owns this route, fixed the answer as `not_entitled`, so SP4's callers read `skipped_reason = "not_entitled"` where SP4's first draft expected a 403 `crm_not_active` (that code stays on customer's C50 to C53 and C89) |
| C57 | account `GET .../memberships` | internal | SP2/R2.6 (SP3/R3.8 widens the callers, SP4/R4.5 adds one) | customer (Team activation, `tools/map_crm_owners.py`), deal and lead (SP3/R3.8 projections and H10, R2.7), approval (SP4/R4.5) | new | Callers, **added with each one's first use (least privilege)**: `customer` from R2.6; `deal` and `lead` from **R3.8's Deploy 2**, because H10 is their first caller (R3.8's account change makes the check `_require_caller(request, "customer", "deal", "lead")` with `_require_org_claim` kept, in the same release as the enriched events); `approval` from R4.5, for the manager reads of the approver search (`ids=`, at most three per request, outside any transaction), since the approver of a membership-keyed request is the requester's direct manager. Each added caller ships the three negative tests in account (no token 401, a service not on the list 403, another organization's claim 403) and a test through that service's **real** client helper; org claim required; read-only on the directory session. Query `after_id`, `limit` (<= 500), `ids` (<= 100, comma list for point reads). Rows `{membership_id, full_name, email, status, is_owner, employee_id, manager_membership_id}` (`is_owner`, `employee_id` and `manager_membership_id` are existing `Membership` columns: `account/app/models.py:329,334,341`). Modelled on the notification-only single read `GET .../memberships/{membership_id}` (`account/app/api/internal.py:304-346`, `{exists, active}`), which stays unchanged |
| C58 | notification `POST .../consent-events` | internal | SP4/R4.1 (contract), SP4/R4.7 (callers) | lead (R4.7), storefront (R4.7) | new | Callers **`storefront` and `lead` only**: customer and support own no capture surface, so the first draft's two other callers are dropped (W24 withdrawn). Body `{channel, purpose, status, source, evidence_ref, address, occurred_at, idempotency_key}`; `source` is **`web_form` only**, every other value being 422 `source_not_allowed` (a bulk `import` or `api` source could attest an opt-in that only an opt-out undoes, the ledger is append-only, and system sources cannot be forged, ADR-0021); the plaintext address crosses the authenticated call, **never a blind index** (a later per-service HKDF would break cross-service equality); reuses `record_consent_event`. Response `{recorded: bool, effective}`. CRM stores no consent |
| C59 | notification `POST /internal/v1/contacts:summarize-messaging` | internal | SP4/R4.2 | customer | new | Caller `customer`, organization from the token claim like its single sibling `GET /internal/v1/contacts/{contact_id}/messaging-summary` (`services/notification/app/api/c1_internal.py:81-114`, exists). Body `{contact_ids: uuid[] (<= 100), emails?: {<contact_id>: string}}` (emails in the body, never in a URL or header); response `{items: {<contact_id>: ChannelsOut \| null}}` |
| C60 | notification `POST /engage/messages:send-to-contact` | public | SP4/R4.2 | SP3/R3.5 (contact page), SP4/R4.2 | new (registered default) | User-authenticated (Engage entitlement and connections required; CRM-only orgs get no send affordance). Permission `engage.inbox.message.reply` pending an SP4 review of `engage.*` versus `crm.*`. Body `{crm_contact_id, channel, template_ref?, text?, variables?, purpose, idempotency_key}`; the address is resolved server-side through customer's `messaging-target`; derives the consent verdict first and refuses operational purpose on a marketing template; stages through `stage_message`; 202 with the staged message |
| C61 | finance `POST .../customers:resolve-for-crm-company` | internal | SP4/R4.3 | operations, customer | new | Caller `operations` (and `customer` for the link tool). Body `{crm_company_id, crm_contact_id?, gstin?}`; lookup-before-create inside the savepoint and unique-index race handling that `ensure_customer_for_crm_contact` already has (`finance/app/services.py:506-640`); company facts come from C50, fetched outside the transaction; response `{customer_id, created: bool}`. **Sequence at accept (B45):** operations calls this route in `accept_order`'s **call** step (claim, call, record: `services/operations/app/orders/services.py:483-600`), for every accept that has a `crm_company_id`, whatever the payment term, with no session open and before the order row is locked; the **record** step persists `orders.finance_customer_id`; READY only reads it, so no network call ever runs under the READY lock. When the resolve fails (finance unreachable or the organization is not entitled to finance) the order is still accepted with `NULL`, never a placeholder, and READY keeps today's visibly-logged skip (`order.invoice_request_skipped`); an order accepted that way is not invoiced automatically. The contact-keyed sibling `POST .../customers:resolve-for-crm-contact` (`finance/app/api/internal.py:366`, caller `operations`) is **kept** and re-pointed, not removed |
| C62 | finance `GET .../customers/credit-position?crm_company_id=<uuid>` | internal | SP4/R4.3 | operations (`check_credit_before_accept`) | new | Caller `operations`, org claim required. **Keyed on CRM identity, not on a finance customer id**: `check_credit_before_accept` runs at `PLACED -> ACCEPTED` (`services/operations/app/orders/services.py:423-444`, called from `accept_order` at `:532`), where it receives the order's own party id and where no finance id is guaranteed yet; the order carries `crm_company_id` (B45). Read-only: it creates no finance row. Response `{positions: [{currency, credit_limit_minor, outstanding_minor, overdue_minor}]}`, one entry per currency and **across all billing profiles of the company** (B41): `outstanding_minor` and `overdue_minor` are summed over the profiles, `credit_limit_minor` is the sum of the limits that are set and `null` when none is, and nothing is ever summed across currencies. A company with no billing profile answers `{positions: []}` and the check passes. Ships warn-only before it refuses an accept: operations' `credit_check_mode` (F19) defaults to `warn` |
| C63 | finance `GET .../invoices:export`, `GET .../payments:export`; operations `GET .../orders:export` | internal | SP4/R4.3 | customer (the `party_documents`, `party_payments` and `party_rollup` backfill, H4) | new | Caller `customer`. Keyset on `(updated_at, id)`, the insights export shape; extends each route's trusted-caller list with `customer` instead of writing a second set |
| C64 | operations `POST .../orders:create-from-quote`; finance `POST .../invoices:create-from-quote`; project and projects `POST .../projects:create-from-deal` | internal | SP4/R4.4, R4.5 | deal (C20) | new (registered default) | Caller `deal` plus the organization claim; idempotent on the quote or deal id; each stores the `crm_*` ids and a back-reference **where its schema holds one**: operations in B45 and B46 (`crm_company_id`, `crm_contact_id`, `source_type`, `source_id`), finance in **B58** (`invoices.crm_company_id`, `source_type`, `source_id`, written once at creation and never updated), project and projects in B50 (`crm_company_id`, `crm_deal_id`). **Authorization:** the human's floor is C20's, checked before the call; the callee proves the caller is `deal` and re-checks that the organization is entitled to its own product (`finance`, `operations`, `pulseboard`, `projects`) through product-service's entitlements read (`services/product/app/api/internal.py:57-79`), because internal routes sit behind no entitlement gate. Each route ships the three negative tests of §C plus an unentitled-organization test. The won-deal project is a **draft**, created only when `deal.pipeline_settings.create_project_on_won` is true (default off) or by an explicit action (C92 is the manual recovery). When finance's totals differ from the quote's, `invoices:create-from-quote` returns `quote_total_delta_minor`: finance's totals win and the delta is audited |
| C65 | support public filters and body | public | SP4/R4.4 | customer 360 (support section), SP4/R4.4 | changed | `GET /support/tickets` accepts `crm_contact_id`, `crm_company_id` (ORG-floor support permission applies, the section is `null` for a rep without it); `POST /support/tickets` accepts `crm_contact_id?`, `crm_company_id?`, `conversation_id?`, `channel: "whatsapp"`. The 360's support section reaches the summary route C90 (counts only, no ticket text) the way the deal leg does, with the caller's forwarded identity headers on the `/api/v1` mount (needs `support_internal_url` on customer, W10). No internal `tickets:from-conversation` route is registered: Engage's inbox calls the public route under the caller's own grant |
| C66 | approval `GET /approvals/request-types` and request type `crm.pipeline.discount` | public route and constant | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 (platform approvals inbox UI) | new | `REQUEST_TYPE_CRM_DISCOUNT = "crm.pipeline.discount"` joins `KNOWN_REQUEST_TYPES` (`approval/app/services.py:75`); the route serves `[{request_type, label, product}]` so a new type needs no HRMS release (`frontend/product-hrms/src/lib/approvalCopy.ts`). Requester abstraction (membership id beside employee id) and the chain source for tenants without HRMS are an ADR (§I) |
| C72 | notification `GET /notifications/me` response | public | SP4/R4.2 | SP4/R4.2 (bell deep links), SP3/R3.6 (follow-up reminders) | changed | The in-app feed exists (`GET /notifications/me`, `POST /notifications/me/{notification_id}:read`, `POST /notifications/me:read-all`: `notification/app/api/inbox.py:44,68,89`); its address is a **membership id** (`notification/app/domain.py:310-325`). `NotificationOut` gains `entity_ref: {product, kind, id} \| null` so the bell can open the record; a CRM rule is channel `in_app`, `recipient_path` = `owner_membership_id` (EV15, EV16, EV17, EV20, EV21), body text generic (ids and enumerations only), with a per-organization on/off for volume (the `notify_*` columns of B65, B27 and B37). These are code-defined **system rules**, not tenant rules: EV54 is the one mechanism (R3.6 creates it for EV20 and EV21, R4.2 adds the EV15, EV16 and EV17 entries and teaches it `entity_ref`) |

The same holds for C65's support section (W10) |

Invoices are frozen snapshots, so a rendered PDF never changes after finalize; the e-invoice QR requirement decides the renderer |

| C78 | deal `GET .../quotes/{quote_id}/approval-summary` | internal | SP4/R4.5 | approval (the `crm.pipeline.discount` consumer, EV23) | new (registered default) | Caller `approval`, org claim required; 404 for a foreign or deleted quote. It exists so that EV23 carries ids and numbers only: the rep's free-text `discount_reason` (B28) reaches the approval request's note through this read and not through an event or the broker (the §D rule on free text; the HRMS precedent `hrms.time.leave.requested` carries a free-text `reason` in its payload, a choice CRM's stricter rule declines). Approval calls it in its handler **before** opening its transaction (claim, call, record). Three negative tests |

Returns `{message_ref, author_type, body}` and answers 404 for an **internal note** (`internal = true`) and for any message whose `author_type` is not `agent`, so the read cannot leak an agent-only note; keyed on `(ticket_id, ref)`, which is unique per ticket (`uq_ticket_messages_ticket_ref`, `services/support/app/models.py:179`). It exists so that `support.desk.ticket.agent_reply_sent` (EV35) never carries the reply text. Three negative tests, plus one proving an internal note is refused |

Returns `{items: [{id, sku, name, uom, hsn_code, selling_price_minor, selling_price_currency}]}` (the item master's own columns, `services/operations/app/models.py:480-536`; the two price fields are both set or both null) and nothing else: **no stock figures, no cost**. Engage-only organizations (no `crm` entitlement) see no change: every row is `manual`, `import` or `signup`. Negative tests: PATCH and DELETE of a `crm` row (409), an import row matching a projected address (skipped and reported) |

C47 stays finance-only because it returns a name and an email. Three negative tests and a cross-tenant test |

Customer sends **both ids** of the roster entry, the path's `owners.id` and the query's `owners.membership_id` (null for an unmapped row), and the callee counts rows whose `owner_id` equals **either**: after R2.7 a mapped row's `owner_id` is a membership id while legacy and unmapped rows keep the roster id (B3), so counting one id would under-count and let the guard allow a deactivation it should refuse. Sources: finance `customers.crm_company_id`, `customers.crm_contact_id`, `invoices.crm_company_id`; operations `orders.crm_company_id`, `orders.crm_contact_id`; notification `contacts.crm_contact_id`, `conversations.crm_contact_id`, `conversations.crm_company_id`, `messages.crm_contact_id`; support `tickets.crm_company_id`, `tickets.crm_contact_id`; project and projects `projects.crm_company_id`; storefront `enquiries.crm_company_id`, `enquiries.crm_contact_id`; deal `deals.company_id`, `deals.primary_contact_id`, `activities.contact_id`, `follow_ups.company_id`, `follow_ups.contact_id`; lead `leads.converted_company_id`, `leads.converted_contact_id` (lead holds plain UUIDs into customer too, `services/lead/app/models.py:109-111`, so it is the ninth source) |

A phone match is a suggestion, never a link |

### C.5 Gateway route tables and public path constants

| # | Entry | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Key shape and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C67 | `/api/v1/companies` prefix | route-table row | SP3/R3.2 | customer, SPA | new | `{ "prefix": "/api/v1/companies", "upstream": "http://localhost:8130", "product": "crm" }` in `services/app-gateway/routes.json`; `"http://customer:8000"` in `infrastructure/edge/routes.prod.json`. Both tables, same commit (`tests/infra/test_frontend_paths_are_routed.py` checks the first segment of every literal `apiRequest`) |
| C68 | `/api/v1/activities` prefix | route-table row | SP3/R3.2 | deal, SPA | new | `http://localhost:8131` / `http://deal:8000`, `product: "crm"`, both tables |
| C69 | `LEAD_PUBLIC_PATHS`, and the `/api/v1/leads/public` route-table row | frozenset in `services/app-gateway/app/main.py`; route-table row in both tables | SP3/R3.7b | gateway | new | `{"/api/v1/leads/public/capture"}`, exact match, unioned into `public_paths` (`services/app-gateway/app/main.py:170`) in the gateway **service**, not in `platform_core.DEFAULT_PUBLIC_PATHS` (the reason is the comment at `services/app-gateway/app/main.py:36-63`). No entitlement check on the public branch. A route-table row `{ "prefix": "/api/v1/leads/public", "upstream": <lead>, "max_body_bytes": 16384 }` goes into both tables (`services/app-gateway/routes.json` and `infrastructure/edge/routes.prod.json`, same commit); the table resolves the longest prefix first (C83), so it wins over `/api/v1/leads` and only the public path gets the smaller cap |
| C70 | `/api/v1/webhooks/leads` | `WEBHOOK_PUBLIC_PATHS` entry and route-table row | SP3/R3.7b | gateway, lead | new | `"/api/v1/webhooks/leads": frozenset({"POST"})` and `{ "prefix": "/api/v1/webhooks/leads", "upstream": <lead>, "max_body_bytes": 65536 }` in both tables; counted in the webhook rate-limit buckets (`services/app-gateway/app/main.py:150-157`, `app-gateway/app/config.py:33`) |
| C71 | dev rows `/api/v1/campaigns`, `/api/v1/tickets` | route-table rows | SP1/R1.2b (SP3/R3.2 if the owner defers it) | — | exists, removed | `services/app-gateway/routes.json:79-80` (product `crm`, upstreams with no service); removed with a parity check against `routes.prod.json` (a new test in the existing `tests/infra/test_route_table_parity.py`, named `test_the_crm_prefixes_are_identical_in_dev_and_prod`). The removal has no runtime effect (production loads `routes.prod.json`), yet any change under `services/app-gateway/` rebuilds and recreates the front-door gateway, so the owner may defer this one item to R3.2's deploy, which rebuilds app-gateway anyway; R3.2's gateway commit then removes the two rows, adds the `/api/v1/companies` and `/api/v1/activities` rows (C67, C68) and adds the parity test. `/api/v1/quotations` (`:89`, product `pulseboard`) is removed with the scaffold in SP4/R4.5 (pending D-19) |
| C83 | `/api/v1/deals/attachments` | route-table row | SP4/R4.5 (after R19) | deal, SPA | new (registered default) | `{ "prefix": "/api/v1/deals/attachments", "upstream": "http://localhost:8131", "product": "crm", "max_body_bytes": 6291456 }` in `services/app-gateway/routes.json`, and `"http://deal:8000"` in `infrastructure/edge/routes.prod.json`: both tables, same commit. The table resolves the **longest prefix first** (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/gateway.py:233`), so this row wins over `/api/v1/deals` and only the upload path gets the larger cap; 6 MiB is `/api/v1/documents`' own ceiling (`docs/adr/0009-uploaded-images-and-object-storage.md:902-905`) and the default stays `DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES` of 2 MiB (`gateway.py:139`) for everything else. `tests/infra/test_route_table_parity.py`, `test_frontend_paths_are_routed.py` |

## D. Events

**Rules that apply to every row** (stated once). Envelope v1 is unchanged (`event_id, event_type, version,
organization_id, aggregate_type, aggregate_id, occurred_at, producer, correlation_id, causation_id, actor, schema_uri,
payload`: ADR-0000 §7 writes the tenant field `org_id`, but the wire carries `organization_id`,
`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/events.py:179`, and consumers read `envelope["organization_id"]`); `producer` is the
**service** name (`services/deal/app/relay.py:38`). Every change is additive within `v1`
and lands in one commit with its committed schema (`additionalProperties: false`) and a producer-side test that builds
the payload through the real write path and calls `validate_event_schema`. Payloads carry **ids and enumerations only**:
never an email, a phone, a person's name or free text, because `deal.activities` and the consent ledger are append-only
and all services share one PII key (no crypto-shredding). The one frozen exception is the legacy `full_name` on
`crm.book.contact.created` (below). A consumer that needs free text (a discount justification, a support reply body)
reads it through an allow-listed internal route at the moment it needs it (C78, C79), never from a payload. `owner_membership_id` is `uuid | null`: null until the owner is mapped or activated,
equal to `owner_id` after R2.7. `crm_company_id` equals the company's `company_id`; both names are emitted on CRM events
that already carry `company_id` and the old name is frozen. A new consumer is deployed **before** its producer's
behaviour changes, because an event with no bound queue is dropped while its outbox row is marked published
(`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/events.py:250-262`).

**Ahead-of-producer gap (fact).** 26 of 77 committed schemas are flagged "ahead of the producer"; the CRM ones
require fields the producers do not emit (`created_at`, `company_id`, `close_date`, `probability`, `closed_at`, `status`,
`owner_id`, `converted_at`, state-after-patch). A producer-side test cannot pass for an event whose schema requires
fields the producer does not emit, so each gap event is closed in the same commit as its first producer-side test:
the **deal** events (EV6-EV8) in SP2/R2.3, which has to add `stage_outcome` through the same producers anyway; the
**customer** company events (EV1, EV2) in SP4/R4.1; and the **lead** events (EV10-EV12) in **SP3/R3.7a**, the first release
that changes what the lead producer emits (B33 widens `source`), so its producer-side test through the real write path
can pass: R4.1 then adds only the id fields. Finance's invoice and payment events (EV33) have the same gap and close it in
SP4/R4.1, with their schemas. Each closing commit also deletes the event's row from the service's `AHEAD_OF_PRODUCER` pin
table (`<service>/tests/test_event_contracts.py`) and its name from
`test_the_ahead_of_producer_schemas_are_exactly_the_pinned_eight` (`libs/contracts/tests/test_event_schemas.py`); SP1 adds both
pins in R1.2b, so the closing is mechanical and the pinned set can only shrink. R1.2b's new contracts (EV4, EV5) are
written to match what is emitted today and so have no gap, and R2.1's `crm.lead.conversion_aborted` (EV13) has none
either. The unmerged `sprint/insights-q2` commit `a4b248f1` already contains the deal, lead and customer producer
changes; R2.3 and R4.1 adopt that field set (pending D-12).

**How to read a consumer cell** (stated once; this is the audit rule that §D.6 satisfies). `notification` means the key is, or
becomes, a subscribable key in §D.5 (a key absent from D.5 is **not consumed** by notification yet). `insights` means the
unmerged insights consumer (L10), which this registry does not register. An explicit `SPn/Rx.y` means a queue binding in
§D.6, and a bare service name is a binding that exists today and is listed there as `exists` (customer's handler of EV5). A service described as "reached over HTTP" is a call named in section C, not an event consumer. Every consumer
named in D.1-D.4 therefore appears in exactly one of those places, and a row that named a consumer with none of them has
been corrected (EV4, EV6, EV9, EV19, EV22, EV24, EV25, EV32, EV34, EV36, EV37).

### D.3 Scheduler-target events (internal; never subscribable; each still needs a committed schema)

Named under A17's default: `crm.pipeline.*` allowed for producer `deal`. If R3.6 leaves the scheduler's ownership rule
unchanged, the same tails move to `deal.pipeline.*`.

| # | Event type (v1) | Producer | Owner | Consumers | Status | Payload and arming |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EV26 | `crm.pipeline.follow_up.reminder_requested` | deal (via `schedule_event`) | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6 (`deal.events`, EV44) | new | `organization_id, follow_up_id, due_at, arm_seq`; idempotency key `follow_up:{follow_up_id}:{due_at_utc_iso}:{arm_seq}`, **single-use** (every arm takes the next `arm_seq`, so no key is requested twice: A16, B25); armed on create and reschedule, cancelled by key (A16) on complete, cancel and reschedule; the handler re-reads the row and is a no-op unless `status = 'open'`, `due_at` equals the payload and `arm_seq` equals the payload's |
| EV27 | `crm.pipeline.deal.review_requested` | deal | SP3/R3.6 (mechanism decided: **one scheduler-armed review per organization per day**, a self-re-arming chain, not a job per deal and kind) | SP3/R3.6 (`deal.events`, EV44) | new | `organization_id, as_of` (the local date), with scheduler key `deal_review:{organization_id}:{as_of}`, aggregate type `deal_review` and the organization id as aggregate id; never subscribable. The registry's first draft carried `deal_id, kind`; one review per organization replaces a scheduler job per deal and kind, which would add two scheduler rows per activity and could not arm deals that predate it. Armed for 06:00 in the organization's timezone (B65). The handler, in one transaction, recomputes the stale and overdue open deals from the rows and publishes EV21 once per deal per threshold crossing, unless the matching `notify_*` switch (B65) is off, and re-arms the next day. **The first review per organization is a silent baseline**: it marks the deals already stale or overdue as notified and publishes nothing, so go-live floods nobody. The chain is armed lazily by deal writes (`ensure_review_armed`) and the settings `PUT`, with no migration data step. Idempotency does not depend on the mechanism: B61's `stale_notified_on` and `overdue_notified_on` hold it |
| EV28 | `crm.pipeline.quote.expiry_requested` | deal | SP4/R4.5 | SP4/R4.5 (`deal.events`, EV44) | new | `organization_id, quote_id, valid_until`; expires a still-`sent` quote past `valid_until` and publishes EV22 `.expired`. Armed when the quote is sent, for `valid_until + 1 day, 00:00 UTC`, with scheduler key `quote:{quote_id}:{valid_until}` (single-use by A16: a quote row is sent once, and `:revise` creates a new row with its own key); cancelled by key on `:accept`, `:reject`, `:revise`-then-`:send` and a deletion; the handler re-reads the row and is a no-op unless the status is still `sent` and `valid_until` is unchanged. A lost expiry must not let an expired quote be accepted, so `:accept` also refuses a `sent` quote whose `valid_until` has passed (409 `quote_expired`) |

(`frontend/product-engage/src/routes/automations/recipes.ts:20-55`); the allow-list stays explicit by design.

The in-app alert for each of the six rep keys is a system rule of EV54, not a tenant rule. R3.6's three keys land with three Engage recipes (`follow-up-due`, `deal-stale`, `deal-overdue`) in the same commit as their schemas, which `tests/infra/test_engage_recipe_triggers.py` pins |

The Engage recipe "Follow up a storefront enquiry" triggers on a storefront key that is not subscribable at HEAD, so the builder flags it today; `tests/infra/test_engage_recipe_triggers.py` pins every recipe trigger once its key is added |
| EV54 | Notification's system in-app rules, which are not subscribable keys: `SYSTEM_IN_APP_RULES` in `services/notification/app/domain.py`, a code-defined mapping evaluated by `apply_rules` (`services/notification/app/services.py:608`) whose entries are the six rep keys of EV41; the bell shows the result (C72) | SP3/R3.6 (creates the mapping and its evaluator); SP4/R4.2 (adds three entries and `entity_ref`) | new | **The one mechanism** for system in-app alerts: tenant rules need an API that CRM roles cannot call, and a migration cannot enumerate organizations. `SYSTEM_IN_APP_RULES: Mapping[str, tuple[SystemInAppRule, ...]]` is keyed by event type, with `SystemInAppRule {code, recipient_path, subject, body, category = "operational", entity?}`, and `apply_rules` evaluates it after the tenant rules: the recipient is `owner_membership_id` normalised for the `in_app` channel, the wording is a fixed template over integers only (no title, name or note ever enters the text), a missing or non-UUID recipient is a logged skip, never an error, and `stage_message` stores the message with `dedupe_key = f"{code}:{event_id}"` (`in_app` has no consent regime). R3.6 creates it with three entries and adds their keys to `SUBSCRIBABLE_EVENTS` (EV41): `crm.pipeline.follow_up.due` (code `system.crm.follow_up_due`), `crm.pipeline.deal.stale` (`system.crm.deal_stale`) and `crm.pipeline.deal.overdue` (`system.crm.deal_overdue`). R4.2 adds `crm.lead.assigned` (`system.crm.lead_assigned`), `crm.pipeline.deal.owner_changed` (`system.crm.deal_assigned`) and `crm.book.company.owner_changed` (`system.crm.company_assigned`) to the same mapping, gives `SystemInAppRule` an optional `entity: {kind, id_path}` so the stored message carries `entity_ref` (B67, C72), and skips a recipient who is the acting membership: no second loop and no second table. The producer-side opt-outs are the `notify_*` columns of B65, B27 and B37. SP4's first-draft name for this mechanism, `BUILTIN_CRM_ALERTS`, is withdrawn |

### D.6 Queues and who binds them

A queue exists only once its consumer has run; deploy the consumer first. Every `bin/worker` service needs its DLQ in
ops-monitor (§F). Every queue that a consumer cell in D.1-D.4 names appears below, and a service that gains its **first**
handler needs the whole fold set of §F (a `run_consumers_in_api` field and the lifespan hooks, a `*-worker` rollback
container, a `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES` entry, `kind` `api` to `api_with_worker`, the RUNBOOK literals) at +1 MB each: deal (F1-F8),
lead (F9), support (F15), marketing (F16) and storefront (F17). A service that already folds (customer, finance, notification,
approval) adds handlers and no budget line. Operations already consumes inside its API process but is unwatched, so R4.3 gives it the queue entry, the rollback worker and the RUNBOOK part of the set, without repricing it (EV55).

A **handler-less scaffold** today (`services/marketing/app/consumers.py:15`). Fold set F16, +1 MB; the alternative, a read, is L17 |
| EV55 | `operations.events` | operations | SP4/R4.3 | exists (a live queue, `services/operations/app/consumers.py:81`). Adds: `crm.book.contact.erased` | changed | The erase handler (EV18) clears `customer_name` and `customer_phone` on every order whose `crm_contact_id` **or `customer_id`** equals the contact (`customer_id` is a CRM contact id in practice for orders that predate B45), leaving totals, lines, status and payment facts, and writes one counts-only audit row. The queue is folded into the API process (`RUN_CONSUMERS_IN_API` defaults true) but **unwatched**: ops-monitor's `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES` names `crm`, `hrms`, `finance` and `admin` only, and no `operations-worker` rollback container exists, and a handler that overwrites personal data must not sit on an unmonitored queue. R4.3 therefore gives operations the fold set of F5, F7 and F8 in one commit (`PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES["inventory"] = ("operations.events",)`, an `operations-worker` container with profile `split-workers` and `command: ./bin/worker`, `containers["operations-worker"]` priced 0 while its profile is off, the RUNBOOK literals), **without** repricing `containers.operations.kind`: its consumers already run inside its resident set, and the owner re-prices only if the PR's measurement with consumers on and off shows a delta above 1 MB (with D-13) |

## E. Permissions and role templates

**Frozen.** The 17 existing codes below and the six legacy role codes are customer data (saved in tenant roles,
automations and notification rules) and are never renamed, narrowed or removed in this programme (D-20; there is no
rename mechanism, and the removal tool `tools/revoke_permission.py` of ADR-0007 does not exist). Everything new is
**additive and admin-first**: a code joins `CRM_ADMIN` (every slice builds its admin template with
`CATALOGUE.all("ORG")`, so it is automatic), reaches live tenants through `make edge-reconcile PRODUCTS=crm`
(dry run first, `APPLY=1` second, off-peak, paced; it bumps the organization's `pv` and refreshes every token), and only
then do the non-admin templates pick it up in a **later** reconcile after the dry run is read. Customer-authored roles
never receive a new code. Every new route sets its floor with `require(code, minimum=Scope.ORG)` where the action has no
honest OWN reading, so a customer-granted OWN rung cannot widen it (`max_scope` is a ceiling, not an allowed set).
`is_dangerous` is metadata, not a control (ADR-0000 §5 note); what constrains a dangerous action is the floor, the
step-up `amr` and the audit row.

### E.1 The catalogue (17 existing + 9 new = 26 CRM codes, one owning service each, plus E27, which is operations')
