Deliberately **not** registered (no release needs a new code; the existing one is the rule): saved views and tags (ride
the entity's read and write), reports and forecast (ride E9), record history (rides the entity read), TEAM scope (a
scope on the existing codes, never a code), bulk lead reassign (E15 at `minimum=Scope.TEAM`), the quote hand-off to finance or operations (E26 at `minimum=Scope.ORG`, C20; a dedicated code is L21), the erase of a captured lead (C108 rides E22 behind a step-up; a dedicated `crm.lead.lead.erase` is L23), the discount decision (`core.approval.request.decide`, a `core` code: a CRM template can never hold it, the
exporter refuses `cross_product`: `tools/export_permissions.py:191-221`).

### E.2 The four role templates (same-code merging, SP2/R2.8)

Each of customer, deal and lead declares **all four codes**, each with only its own slice of the grants below; the
exporter unions them and the wider scope wins (`tools/export_permissions.py:116-137`). `name`, `is_default` and
`cross_product` must be identical in the three declarations or the exporter exits. `CRM_ADMIN` is built from each
slice's `.all("ORG")`, which is what keeps "a product's admin role is built from that product's own catalogue"
(ADR-0000 §5) true and is the template the exporter flags `is_admin` for every contributing service (the widest
template per service, `tools/export_permissions.py:224-249`), so new activations hand `CRM_ADMIN` to the owners.

| Code | `name` | `is_default` | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CRM_ADMIN` | CRM administrator | false | everything in the catalogue at ORG; the only template that configures anything |
| `CRM_MANAGER` | CRM sales manager | false | works the shared book, edits what their team owns |
| `CRM_REP` | CRM sales representative | **true** | works the shared book, edits what they own; the template a new member receives |
| `CRM_VIEWER` | CRM viewer | false | read-only seat: no writes, no reveal, no money |

The new display names differ from the three legacy "Sales representative" rows on purpose: role names are not unique,
only the code is, and one name on three roles is what makes assignment error-prone today.

Scope per code and template. `—` = not held. `†` = **enters as OWN, widened to TEAM** by the R3.8 reconcile, because
`ScopeContext.apply` refuses a TEAM grant when the selector passes no `team_col` (`auth.py:342-347`: "TEAM
scope is not supported on this resource"), which is every CRM selector until A14 lands; the reconcile raises the scope of an existing grant
(ADR-0007 §1), so the widening needs no new code. `‡` = held at **OWN**, the rep-equivalent, with TEAM **pending the owner's decision D-25** (managers may reveal the sealed phone or email of their whole team's records; registered default **no**, §L). TEAM `read_sensitive` would be a new access widening that P§8 D-9 does not approve (D-9 approves only the reps' own write, convert and reveal) and that D-20 does not settle (D-20 fixes the role names, not their grants), so it is not baked into this binding template.

| Code | `CRM_ADMIN` | `CRM_MANAGER` | `CRM_REP` | `CRM_VIEWER` | Joins the non-admin templates in |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `crm.book.company.read` | ORG | ORG | ORG | ORG | R2.8 |
| `crm.book.company.write` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | R2.8 |
| `crm.book.company.delete` | ORG | — | — | — | admin only |
| `crm.book.contact.read` | ORG | ORG | ORG | ORG | R2.8 |
| `crm.book.contact.write` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | R2.8 |
| `crm.book.contact.delete` | ORG | — | — | — | admin only |
| `crm.book.contact.read_sensitive` | ORG | OWN ‡ | OWN | — | R2.8 |
| `crm.book.owner.write` | ORG | — | — | — | admin only |
| `crm.pipeline.deal.read` | ORG | ORG | ORG | ORG | R2.8 |
| `crm.pipeline.deal.write` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | R2.8 |
| `crm.pipeline.deal.delete` | ORG | — | — | — | admin only |
| `crm.pipeline.activity.read` | ORG | ORG | ORG | ORG | R2.8 |
| `crm.pipeline.activity.write` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | R2.8 |
| `crm.lead.lead.read` | ORG | ORG | ORG | ORG | R2.8 |
| `crm.lead.lead.write` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | R2.8 |
| `crm.lead.lead.convert` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | R2.8 |
| `crm.lead.lead.read_sensitive` | ORG | OWN ‡ | OWN | — | R2.8 |
| `crm.pipeline.stage.write` | ORG | — | — | — | admin only |
| `crm.pipeline.settings.write` | ORG | — | — | — | admin only |
| `crm.lead.settings.write` | ORG | — | — | — | admin only |
| `crm.pipeline.follow_up.read` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | after R3.6 ships its routes |
| `crm.pipeline.follow_up.write` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | after R3.6 ships its routes |
| `crm.book.company.read_commercial` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | after R4.3 |
| `crm.book.contact.erase` | ORG | — | — | — | admin only |
| `crm.pipeline.quote.read` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | after R4.5 |
| `crm.pipeline.quote.write` | ORG | TEAM † | OWN | — | after R4.5 |

Reads stay at **ORG** for reps, managers and viewers: sales works from one shared book, and the programme's access
change in C15 is the reps' OWN writes, convert and reveal (pending D-9), not a narrower read. The `CRM_REP` rows for E1-E17
are exactly the union of the three legacy rep templates, so the merge changes nothing for a holder of all three.

### E.3 Legacy role codes (frozen) and what happens to them

| Legacy code | Declared by | Grants | Maps to | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CRM_BOOK_ADMIN` ("CRM administrator (book)") | customer | `CRM_BOOK.all("ORG")` (the eight `crm.book.*` codes today; follows its slice) | `CRM_ADMIN` | exists, frozen |
| `CRM_PIPELINE_ADMIN` ("CRM administrator (pipeline)") | deal | `CRM_PIPELINE.all("ORG")` (the five `crm.pipeline.*` codes today; follows its slice) | `CRM_ADMIN` | exists, frozen |
| `CRM_LEADS_ADMIN` ("CRM administrator (leads)") | lead | `CRM_LEADS.all("ORG")` (the four `crm.lead.lead.*` codes today; follows its slice) | `CRM_ADMIN` | exists, frozen |
| `CRM_SALES_REP` ("Sales representative") | customer | company.read ORG, company.write OWN, contact.read ORG, contact.write OWN, contact.read_sensitive OWN | `CRM_REP` | exists, frozen |
| `CRM_DEAL_REP` ("Sales representative") | deal | deal.read ORG, deal.write OWN, activity.read ORG, activity.write OWN | `CRM_REP` | exists, frozen |
| `CRM_LEAD_REP` ("Sales representative") | lead | lead.read ORG, lead.write OWN, lead.convert OWN, lead.read_sensitive OWN | `CRM_REP` | exists, frozen |

Registered default (pending D-20, D-21): the six legacy templates stay **declared, with code, name and description unchanged**.
The three legacy **rep** templates (explicit `pick(...)` lists) never gain a code. The three legacy **admin** templates
keep `.all("ORG")`, so they widen with their own slice exactly as the existing admin-first design intends (an existing
admin can configure stages without being moved to `CRM_ADMIN`); nothing narrows. The one change is `is_default`, which
becomes `false` on the three legacy rep templates in the same commit that sets it `true` on `CRM_REP`, so a new tenant and a new member
receive exactly one default CRM role. That change reaches new activations only: reconcile never updates `is_default`,
role names or descriptions (a live tenant's legacy rep roles stay `is_default` until a reviewed bespoke script changes
them). In a live tenant, reconcile **creates** the four new roles (a missing role is created, ADR-0007 §1), grants
`CRM_ADMIN` to the active owners, and leaves legacy roles and every customer-authored role as they are; moving other
members onto the new roles is a deliberate owner action after the dry run (no auto-grant, ADR-0007 §4). The swap
migration that removes legacy roles needs retire/revoke tooling that does not exist and is out of scope. The legacy
mapping is recorded in `docs/crm.md` (§I) and the ADR, not as runtime code.

### E.4 Rollout order for every new code (ADR-0007; stated once)

1. Add the `PermissionSpec` to the owning slice's catalogue; the admin template covers it through `.all("ORG")`;
   non-admin templates need an explicit `pick(...)` and their pinned tests updated (`services/*/tests/test_catalogue.py`).
2. `make openapi` regenerates `libs/contracts/permissions/crm.json` (CODEOWNERS review applies to `libs/contracts/`).
3. **Owner-run:** deploy the enforcing service; a route answers 403 to everyone but an admin who has been reconciled.
4. **Owner-run:** `make edge-reconcile PRODUCTS=crm` (the dry run is always printed first), then `APPLY=1`; from R2.5 an optional `ORG=<uuid>` passes the tool's existing `--org` in place of the wrapper's hard-wired `--all` (`infrastructure/edge/remote.sh:456`), so the first reconcile of a new code can hit one designated tenant before the fleet (H6); the manifest is a bind
   mount of the checkout on the box, so "live at checkout" is an ordering hazard: reconcile before the UI is published.
5. **Owner-run:** only then publish the SPA that shows the control (`make edge-web` from the merged checkout); the SPA's `useCan`
   carries the same `minimum` as the route and its fixture grants (`frontend/product-crm/src/auth/AuthProvider.tsx:41-56`,
   `frontend/opsgrids-visual/fixtures/**/grants.json`) carry the new codes. The dashboard persona reads
   `crm.book.company.read`, `crm.pipeline.activity.read` and `useEntitlement('crm')`, and calls `GET /customers?status=customer&size=1`
   and `GET /deals/activities` (`frontend/opsgrids-dashboard/src/api/index.ts:382, 388`): those codes and those two paths stay
   stable (C17, C21), so the dashboard needs no change in this programme.

**No agent runs steps 3-5.** Deploys, the `make edge-*` targets and the SPA publish are owner-gated, as are production reads
and writes (§H preamble; P§3 F11: review, merge, manual deploy, verify, stop on any failure).

## F. Settings, environment, flags and budget kinds

Compose-wide variables live in `x-service-env` (`infrastructure/edge/compose.prod.yml:57-105`); a service without the
field ignores the variable. There is no per-organization feature flag in the platform and none is added: staged rollout
is the admin-first role templates of §E, and an organization-level opt-in is a column of `deal.pipeline_settings` (B27) that
defaults off.

| # | Name | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Key shape and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | `RUN_CONSUMERS_IN_API` / `Settings.run_consumers_in_api: bool = False` on deal | env and setting | SP2/R2.4 | SP2/R2.4 | changed | The shared variable already exists and defaults **true** in `x-service-env` (`compose.prod.yml:94`); customer already reads it (`customer/app/config.py:23`); deal has no field, so it ignores it today. Adding the field plus the lifespan hooks (`_start_background_worker`, `_stop_background_worker`, `consumers.use_session_scope`, `create_bulkhead_engine(cfg, name="worker")`; template `customer/app/main.py:22-77`; consumer module seam `customer/app/consumers.py:31-51`) **starts the deal consumer on the box at deploy** (hence F2). The consumer runs under a **system context** `services.service_ctx(organization_id)`, mirroring `customer/app/services.py:141-155` (all-zero `sub`, ORG scope, `membership_id=None`, a permission string only to build the `ScopeContext`), so `own_ref` is `None` and nothing OWN-scoped can run under it; `delete_deal`, `move_stage` (deal) and `delete_company`, `delete_contact` (customer) gain optional `acting_as` and `reason` that flow to `record_audit`, which already supports both (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/audit.py:153-185`) |
| F2 | `crm_conversion_cleanup_mode` (env `CRM_CONVERSION_CLEANUP_MODE`) | setting on deal's and customer's `Settings`, and an `x-service-env` line | SP2/R2.4 | SP2/R2.4 | new | Values `off`, `log_only` and `on`, and not a strict `Literal` that fails at start: **an unknown value falls back to `log_only` with an error log**, so a typo can neither crash-loop customer and deal nor arm the cleanup, and a case slip is never read as `on`. Default **`log_only`** in code and in compose (`CRM_CONVERSION_CLEANUP_MODE: ${CRM_CONVERSION_CLEANUP_MODE:-log_only}`). `log_only`: the handler computes its decision, logs `crm.conversion_cleanup.would_apply` with ids and counts only, writes nothing, and acknowledges the event. `on`: acts. `off`: acknowledges and ignores. Flipping to `on` is a box `.env` edit: owner-only, after reading the logs (pending D-2). Events acknowledged in `log_only` are the sweep's input (pending D-3) |
| F3 | `deal_timeout_seconds: float = 3.0` on customer | setting | SP2/R2.4 | SP2/R2.4 | new | Beside the existing `deal_internal_url` (`customer/app/config.py:29`, compose `:1437`); bounds the references probe (C55) |
| F4 | `account_service_url: str = "http://localhost:8111"` and `account_timeout_seconds: float = 5.0` on customer, and later deal and lead | settings | SP2/R2.6 | SP2/R2.6, R2.7; SP3/R3.8 | new | Reuses the **already-shared** `ACCOUNT_SERVICE_URL: http://account:8000` (`compose.prod.yml:103`; `auth` and `admin-gateway` read the same name), so no compose line is needed. Do not copy notification's `account_internal_url` name: no compose line sets `ACCOUNT_INTERNAL_URL` (inference: notification's conversation-assignee check therefore calls `localhost:8111` on the box) |
| F5 | `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES["crm"]` and `CORE_DLQ_QUEUES` | constants in `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/ops_monitor/config.py:62-109` | SP2/R2.4, SP3/R3.6, SP3/R3.8, SP4/R4.3, R4.4, R4.5, R4.6, R4.7 | ops-monitor | changed | `crm`: `("customer.events",)` gains `"deal.events"` in R2.4 and `"lead.events"` in **R3.8** (EV45, F9: unconditional); three **new profile keys** follow the folds of F15-F17: `support` = `("support.events",)` (R4.4), `marketing` = `("marketing.events",)` (R4.6) and `storefront` = `("storefront.events",)` (R4.7); `"scheduler.events"` moves from `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES["finance"]` to `CORE_DLQ_QUEUES` in R3.6; `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES["inventory"] = ("operations.events",)` is new in R4.3 (EV55); and `"approval.events"` moves from `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES["hrms"]` to `CORE_DLQ_QUEUES` in R4.5 (F18). Pinned by `libs/platform-core/tests/test_ops_monitor_config.py:76-86` (asserts crm == `customer.events` and "deal has no consumer": must change on purpose) and `tests/infra/test_ops_monitor_dlq_queue_sync.py`, which derives workers from compose `./bin/worker` services in **both** directions, so listing any new queue requires its `*-worker` compose service (F7, F9, F15, F16, F17) |
| F6 | ops-monitor `EDGE_RELAY_SCHEMAS` default | compose line | SP1/R1.2b | ops-monitor | changed | The ops-monitor block's default (`compose.prod.yml:851`) omits `lead` while edge-relay's (`:791`) lists it, so a stalled `lead.outbox` raises no outbox-lag alert; R1.2b adds `lead` (`.env.example:1167` stays "leave unset"; `docs/deploy/ENV_SOURCE_MATRIX.md:837` already acknowledges the gap). Test: `tests/infra/test_ops_monitor_compose_env.py` |
| F7 | `deal-worker` compose service and budget entry (and, in R4.3, `operations-worker`) | compose and `infrastructure/edge/memory-budget.json` | SP2/R2.4 (deal), SP4/R4.3 (operations) | SP2/R2.4, SP4/R4.3 | new | `command: ./bin/worker`, profile `split-workers`, the rollback shape of `customer-worker` (`compose.prod.yml:~1449-1458`); `containers["deal-worker"] = {"kind": "background", "profile": "split-workers", ...}`. Priced **0** while its profile is off. Without it, `RUN_CONSUMERS_IN_API=false` plus `split-workers` (the documented rollback) silently stops deal's consumer. `deal/bin/worker` already exists (`exec python -m app.consumers`); lead has no `bin/worker` (F9 adds it in R3.8); `deal/bin/cron` and `lead/bin/cron` are copies of `relay` and are on R1.2b's removal list. Operations gets the same rollback container in R4.3 (`operations-worker`, priced 0 while its profile is off; `services/operations/bin/worker` already exists), because the DLQ list and the compose workers must match in both directions (EV55) |
| F8 | `containers.deal.kind`: `"api"` → `"api_with_worker"` (and, in R4.5-B, the kind `api_with_worker_render`) | `memory-budget.json` kind | SP2/R2.4 (SP4/R4.5-B for the render kind) | SP2/R2.4 | changed | 98 → 99 MB, and **every fold costs the same +1 MB**: deal (this row, R2.4), lead (F9, R3.8), support (F15, R4.4), marketing (F16, R4.6) and storefront (F17, R4.7). Deal alone: ten sellable profiles 3747 → 3748 MB (97.0%, under the 3862 MB hard line); ten plus `admin` 3944 → 3945 MB. **If all five fold: 3752 MB for the ten profiles (97.2%) and 3949 MB with `admin` (102.3%)**, which is the final figure, not 3748 and 3945. The box's `.env` holds `EDGE_BUDGET_ACCEPTED_MB=3944` (owner memory note), exactly today's total with `admin`, so the owner must raise it by one per fold, release by release, before each deploys with `admin` on (3945, 3946, 3947, 3948, 3949 in the order R2.4, R3.8, R4.4, R4.6, R4.7); an agent never edits it (L17). `infrastructure/edge/RUNBOOK.md` literals change in the same commit as each fold (`:2523`, `:2525`, `:2980`, `:3011`, `:3029`, `:3042-3049`, `:3063`), each to **that release's own running total**, or `tests/infra/test_planned_headroom.py::test_runbook_ceiling_example_is_the_number_preflight_would_print` fails. A **standalone** deal-worker would cost +90 MB and is not registered (D-13 assumed no increase, which even deal's fold already breaks, L17; insights' +99 MB stays pending D-13). Each release states its memory as a **change against the running total** (so the progression above, 3748 and 3945 at R2.4 and 3749 and 3946 at R3.8, holds in whatever order the releases merge), and the owner raises `EDGE_BUDGET_ACCEPTED_MB` to the total `preflight.sh` prints, never to a literal copied from a document. **R4.5-B adds one more memory item, unmeasured today:** a new budget kind `api_with_worker_render` (`api_with_worker`'s 99 MB plus the measured delta of the PDF library), used by `deal` and `finance` only, so `kinds.api` and `kinds.api_with_worker` stay as they are; it ships with the measurement and the owner's raised ceiling (L17) |
| F9 | `lead` fold | `Settings.run_consumers_in_api: bool = False` and the lifespan hooks (template `customer/app/main.py:22-77`), new `lead/app/consumers.py` and `lead/bin/worker`, `containers.lead.kind` → `api_with_worker`, a `lead-worker` rollback container (profile `split-workers`, `command: ./bin/worker`), `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES["crm"] += "lead.events"` | SP3/R3.8 | SP3/R3.8, SP4/R4.7 | new | **Unconditional**: TEAM scope on leads (A14, E.2 †, C37) needs lead's own `owner_team` (B4), which only lead's own consumer can feed (EV45), and lead has none today (`services/lead/bin` holds `api`, `cron` and `relay`; `services/lead/app/config.py` has no `run_consumers_in_api`; `memory-budget.json` `containers.lead.kind` is `api`). The consumer runs under a system context and binds the tenant GUC per message (`consumers.use_session_scope`), exactly as F1 describes for deal. Same ops-monitor and RUNBOOK edits as F5, F7, F8, and a second +1 MB (L17). R4.7's round-robin availability and owner-deactivation hook join the same queue |
| F10 | `scheduler` to the core profile | compose and `memory-budget.json` `containers.scheduler.profile` `"finance"` → `null` | SP3/R3.6 | SP3/R3.6, SP4/R4.5 | changed | One background container already priced at 90 MB under `finance`; moving it does not add a container but changes which boxes run it. `tests/infra/test_active_profiles.py`, `test_ops_monitor_profile_containers.py`, `test_compose_memory_budget.py`. Decided earlier in ADR-0015 and not done (`docs/adr/0015-scheduler-and-not-before-delivery.md:294-318`); a preflight check that a profile whose service calls `schedule_event` has the scheduler running is part of it (ADR-0015 Q2's `preflight.sh` guard: refuse a deploy in which an enabled profile's service calls `schedule_event` while `scheduler` is not running). The same commit moves the RUNBOOK's cumulative table (core 1772 to 1862, finance's "adds" 189 to 99, and each row between +90, `infrastructure/edge/RUNBOOK.md:2999-3011`) and its "inventory alone plus `admin`" example (1870 and 2067 MB to 1960 and 2157, `:2521`), because the move shifts 90 MB from `finance` to core; `crm` boxes without `finance` rise by the same 90, and every ten-profile figure is unchanged |

The compose line goes in the **caller's own service block** (the existing pattern: notification `compose.prod.yml:1188`, customer `:1436-1437`, lead `:1481-1482`), never in `x-service-env`, which keeps only the two shared names (`:103-104`). The new env-parity test `tests/infra/test_party_link_env_wiring.py` (precedent `tests/infra/test_payment_env_wiring.py`) loads W1-W52 and asserts **every pair**: the caller's `Settings` has the field and the caller's compose block (or `x-service-env`) sets it to `http://<callee>:8000`; it also fails when a service's `Settings` gains a `*_internal_url` that no W row names |
| F12 | `capture_token_secret: SecretStr` (env `CAPTURE_TOKEN_SECRET`), `product_service_url` and `product_timeout_seconds: float = 5.0` on lead | settings, one new box secret | SP3/R3.7b | SP3/R3.7b | new | `capture_token_secret` is the HMAC key of the form token and of the webhook's `X-Opsgrids-Endpoint` header (B36, C41, C42). Precedent end to end: `engage_unsubscribe_secret` (`services/notification/app/config.py:108`, compose `${ENGAGE_UNSUBSCRIBE_SECRET}` at `infrastructure/edge/compose.prod.yml:1194`, `infrastructure/edge/.env.example:755`, `docs/deploy/ENV_SOURCE_MATRIX.md:177`). The default is a `dev-only-` value that turns public capture **off** outside local rather than stopping the service. Setting it is a **box `.env` edit, owner-only** (`openssl rand -hex 32`); rotating it invalidates every issued form token and webhook header, so every embedded form and webhook sender must be re-issued, and `.env.example`, the ENV_SOURCE_MATRIX row and the RUNBOOK change in the same commit. The deliverables, each in the commit that adds the first line using it (the `ENGAGE_UNSUBSCRIBE_SECRET` pattern end to end): a `capture_enabled` property that is true only when the value is at least 32 bytes and does not start `dev-only-` or `ci-only-` (an empty, short or development value is **capture off**: every capture route answers the one 400, `POST /leads/capture-endpoints` answers 422 `capture_not_configured`, and reads report `capture_configured: false`, while the service still starts); the compose line `CAPTURE_TOKEN_SECRET: ${CAPTURE_TOKEN_SECRET}` in lead's own block with **no default** and never in `x-service-env`; the `.env.example` entry; the key in `infrastructure/edge/lib/secret-strength.sh` (the 32-byte rule); the ENV_SOURCE_MATRIX, env-reference and RUNBOOK rows; and `tests/infra/test_capture_secret_wiring.py` plus new cases in `tests/infra/test_preflight_secrets.py`. `preflight.sh` takes its required keys from `.env.example` and refuses a missing, empty, `dev-only-` or `ci-only-` value, so the owner sets the key in the box `.env` **before the R3.7b deploy**; "capture stays off with the dev default" describes local development and CI only. `product_service_url` reuses the already-shared `PRODUCT_SERVICE_URL: http://product:8000` (`compose.prod.yml:104`), so it needs no compose line: it is how lead learns that the organization's `crm` activation is live (L12) |

### F.1 Caller-to-callee wiring matrix (F11)

One row per internal call. **Setting** is on the caller's `Settings`; every URL setting is paired with `<callee>_timeout_seconds: float = 5.0` unless the row says otherwise, except that the customer 360's legs (W10-W14) share customer's one existing `customer360_upstream_timeout_seconds` (2.0 s, `services/customer/app/config.py:32`) rather than a timeout each. **Compose** is the caller's own service block, `http://<callee>:8000`, except the two shared names, which already sit in `x-service-env` (`infrastructure/edge/compose.prod.yml:103-104`). **Profile caveat:** a callee that runs only under its own profile makes a caller on a box without that profile see "unavailable", never "wrong" (`compose.prod.yml:1186-1188`); the callee profiles are `crm` (customer, deal, lead), `finance`, `inventory` (operations), `support`, `pulseboard` (project), `projects`, `marketing`, `hrms` (leave, and approval until F18 moves it to core in R4.5), `storefront` (storefront **and document**) and core (notification, account, product, payment). Public routes called "as the viewer" use the caller's forwarded identity headers on the `/api/v1` mount (C23), not a service token.

## G. Shared UI components, client hooks and generated types

Walls (ADR-0001, `frontend/.dependency-cruiser.cjs:25-65`): an app imports `@opsgrids/ui` and `@opsgrids/client` only
through their package roots; `@opsgrids/ui` may not import `@opsgrids/client` and the reverse; **shared variants go into
`@opsgrids/ui`, never a fork into `product-crm`**. Therefore a component that fetches is split in two: a presentational
component in ui with a structural props interface, and a hook in client; the app composes them. Every new ui export needs a
story (`frontend/opsgrids-ui/src/stories.test.ts`), every pixel change needs reviewed `visual:update` baselines, the
token baseline (`pnpm tokens:baseline`; 101 offenders in 15 files in `product-crm` at HEAD, 97 in 14 once R1.2b has deleted `ComingSoon`) can only shrink, and every list has
loading, empty, error and forbidden states. Any field added to a CRM response must be added to
`frontend/opsgrids-visual/fixtures/api/crm/*.json`, and any new `GET` needs a `MockRoute`
(`frontend/opsgrids-visual/fixtures/api/routes.ts:46-86`) or the CRM page tests fail. The app package is
`@opsgrids/product-crm` (`frontend/product-crm`).

| # | Name | Package and kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Key props and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | `KanbanBoard` | `@opsgrids/ui` component (`components/KanbanBoard.tsx`) | SP3/R3.3 | SP3/R3.4 (deals board), `product-projects`, `product-pulseboard` | new (promoted) | Replaces the three forks (`product-crm/src/ui/KanbanBoard.tsx`, `product-projects/src/ui/KanbanBoard.tsx`, `product-pulseboard/src/ui/KanbanBoard.tsx`). ## H. `tools/` commands

Production data operations are owner-run `tools/` commands (P§3 F11; agents never run them against the box, never SQL-write
production, never run `e2e/edge_*`). The same holds for the deploy that carries a data step, for every `make edge-*` target and
for the SPA publish (§E.4): an agent builds and tests them locally and the owner runs them on the box. **Gating copies the purge tools, not the reconcile tools**: dry run by default,
read-only (`SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY` or an always-rolled-back transaction), and a write only under `--apply` plus
`--confirm-count N`, `--i-took-a-backup` and `--i-know-this-is-the-live-database` (`tools/purge_test_orgs.py:1892-1910`).
`reconcile_membership_links.py` and `reconcile_permissions.py` do the opposite: they **write** when `--dry-run` is
omitted (`tools/reconcile_membership_links.py:453-457`), and only `remote.sh` always dry-runs first (`remote.sh:434-458`).
Common shape: `--org <uuid>` or `--all`, `--pace <ms>` (default 250), one transaction per organization on the NOBYPASSRLS
app role with the tenant GUC bound for that transaction, stop at the first failing organization, idempotent and
re-runnable, output is **counts and ids only**. Every new tool below that writes tenant data in production (H1 to H4 and H8 to H14) has the purge tools' gating without exception: `plan` is the read-only default, and `apply` needs `--apply` and the three gates (`--confirm-count`, `--i-took-a-backup`, `--i-know-this-is-the-live-database`). A tool that touches more than one CRM schema takes `--service
customer|deal|lead` and runs one child process per service, because `import app` is ambiguous across services
(the repo's root rules); a plan file is the transport between children. On the box a tool runs in a throwaway container from the
owning service's definition with `tools/` mounted read-only, as `reconcile_permissions` does
(`infrastructure/edge/remote.sh:450-456`); the wrapper subcommand and the `make edge-*` target are owner-gated like
`make edge-reconcile` (`Makefile:585`).

| # | Name | Kind | Owner | Consumers | Status | Subcommands, flags and rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | `tools/map_crm_owners.py` (wrapper `remote.sh map-crm-owners`, target `make edge-map-crm-owners`) | owner-run CLI | SP2/R2.6 (built and tested); run by the owner during R2.7 | owner; SP2/R2.7 | new | `census` (read-only, pending D-10 and D-3): per organization the roster size, the distinct `owner_id` counts per table (`companies`, `contacts`, `deals`, `activities`, `leads`, `assignment_rules.owner_ids`) and how many are in the roster, roster emails that equal an active member's email, owner values that equal any membership `employee_id`, leads with a live conversion claim, the `lead.outbox` backlog, and with `--aborted-conversions` the count of `lead.audit_logs` rows with action `lead.conversion_aborted`. `plan --out PLAN.json` (read-only): classifies every legacy owner id `link` (value equals a membership's `employee_id`, an audited human assertion), `email` (roster email equals exactly one active member's email; **needs explicit per-organization or per-row acceptance**), `conflict` or `unmapped`; removed, suspended and invited memberships are report-only; **never fuzzy** (pending D-8; ADR-0008 Q1 rejected email matching for the live link). The plan file holds roster emails, is kept off the repo and off shell history. `apply --service S --plan PLAN.json [--org U \| --all] [--pace MS]` (dry run unless `--apply` and its three gates): sets `owners.membership_id` for accepted rows and rewrites only the exact legacy ids the plan names (so a re-run changes nothing); rewrites `companies.owner_id`, `contacts.owner_id` (customer), `deals.owner_id` (deal), `leads.owner_id` and `assignment_rules.owner_ids` element-wise with order and cursor preserved (lead); **skips every lead whose claim lease is live** (`conversion_claim_id` set and `conversion_claimed_at` within `CONVERSION_LEASE_SECONDS`, `lead/app/domain.py:93-101`) and lists them, because rewriting `owner_id` under a live claim forces an `owner_changed` abort; **leaves `deal.activities` untouched** (append-only trigger, B3); sets `updated_at` explicitly so rows are not mass-touched; writes a journal row per change (B2) and one audit row per organization per service (`acting_as='map-crm-owners-cli'`, counters only); stages **no** outbox events. `verify [--org \| --all]`: per table, owner ids that are neither a membership of the organization nor a known legacy id, plus skipped leads; exit non-zero on strays. `revert --service S --run-id U` (gated like `apply`): restores from the journal where the current value still equals the journaled new value. Needs `ACCOUNT_SERVICE_URL` and the service-token secret inside the container (F4). Order: census → owner reads it → plan → owner accepts → `apply` per service → `verify` → only then the SPA that reads `mem` (data before code; the SPA last). **Refinements fixed by SP2/R2.6:** the pure core and its three per-service children run under the host-side wrapper (`remote.sh map-crm-owners`, `make edge-map-crm-owners`), in a throwaway container as the image's own user; its artifacts (`values-*.json`, `snapshot.json`, `manual-pairs.json`, `plan.json`, `run-*.json`) live in a state directory owned by that user, mode 700, so a plan that holds roster emails can be neither read nor edited by the deploy user and is reviewed and amended only through the tool; the plan carries a digest over its immutable fields. Beside `census`, `plan`, `apply`, `verify` and `revert` it has the subcommands `show`, `accept`, `reject`, `link` (a hand-entered mapping is a plan row of evidence `manual`, never a free edit; it also covers a roster email that differs from the account email) and `forget-plan`. `verify` also reports, per table and service, `legacy_rows_remaining`, `unconvertible_leads` and `unnamed` members: a legacy roster id is not a stray (it resolves in the roster) yet matches no OWN scope after the flip, so "zero strays" can hold while the whole book is still legacy-owned, and the owner records those numbers, and what was done with each, before the flip. `apply` and `revert` require an explicit `--service`, and customer's run file gates deal's and lead's runs |
| H2 | `tools/link_finance_customers.py` (wrapper `remote.sh link-finance-customers`) | owner-run CLI | SP4/R4.3 | owner | new | `plan --out F` (read-only): matches each existing `finance.customers` row to a CRM company by **GSTIN first**, then name plus email, and reports `matched`, `ambiguous`, `unmatched`; **never auto-merges on name** (CRM company names are case-sensitive unique and duplicates exist, `docs/adr/0017-crm-lead-service-and-pipeline-scaffold-removal.md:139-149`). `apply --plan F` (gated): writes `finance.customers.crm_company_id` only for human-approved rows and never touches an invoice, which is a frozen snapshot. A company with several GSTIN profiles is normal (B41) |
| H3 | `tools/sweep_aborted_conversions.py` | owner-run CLI | SP2/R2.4 (**only if the D-3 census is non-zero**) | owner | new (pending D-3) | `report` (default, read-only): lists `lead.audit_logs` `lead.conversion_aborted` rows whose company, contact and deal are still live and untouched since creation, after **re-reading the lead's current state** (`converted_*`, `conversion_generation`). `apply` (gated) replays history **through the same handlers** with synthetic envelopes, one child process per service, because the handlers already hold the safety rules and there should be one decision procedure; so it soft-deletes only what the NEW-9 handlers would, and it sweeps only audit rows that recorded a generation bump (pending D-1, D-2). The bus never delivered those historical events (no bound queue), and `tools/outbox.py` cannot replay published rows, so the audit rows are the only evidence |
| H4 | `tools/backfill_party_rollup.py` | owner-run CLI | SP4/R4.3 | owner | new | Fills the facts `customer.party_documents` and `party_payments` (B79, B80), from them `party_rollup` (B15), and `party_timeline` (B16) from the export routes of C63, for events published before the consumer existed; `plan` is the read-only default and `apply` takes the three gates of the H preamble; idempotent through `source_event_id` and the version guard; never promotes a company's lifecycle (B81) |
| H5 | `tools/check_party_orphans.py` | read-only owner runner of a nightly sweep | SP4/R4.1 (design), SP4/R4.7 (built) | ops, owner | new | The nightly orphan check ADR-0000 §10 promises, covering every plain-UUID reference to a CRM company or contact (finance, operations, notification, support, projects, storefront, deal): each owning service exposes its references (C104) and the job reports ids that no longer resolve; it never deletes. The nightly job runs **inside customer** (a sweep in the API process, F20, which already folds a consumer), and this tool, `tools/check_party_orphans.py --org <uuid> [--list]`, is the read-only owner runner of the same sweep; built in R4.7 |
| H6 | `tools/export_permissions.py`, `tools/export_openapi.py`, `tools/reconcile_permissions.py crm` | existing | SP2/R2.5 (the `ORG` option only) | every release that changes a code or a route | exists, changed in R2.5 | `make openapi` regenerates `libs/contracts/{openapi,permissions}/*.json`; `make edge-reconcile PRODUCTS=crm [APPLY=1] [ORG=<uuid>]` is the live-tenant rollout (§E.4). `ORG` is new in R2.5 and passes the tool's existing `--org` (`tools/reconcile_permissions.py:59,328`) in place of the wrapper's hard-wired `--all` (`infrastructure/edge/remote.sh:456`), so the first reconcile of a new code can hit one designated tenant before the fleet. Neither tool is renamed |
| H7 | `tools/demo/seed_crm.py`, `e2e/_seed_crm.py`, `e2e/edge_product_crud.py` | existing seeds and the production smoke | SP2/R2.7 | SP2/R2.7 | changed | Seeds create reps through the activation path so their owner ids are membership ids that a token can match; no fake membership-looking ids (they look like members and no token can match them). `create_deal` with literal stage keys stays valid because the six legacy keys are the default pipeline's keys. `e2e/edge_product_crud.py:337-350` is unchanged through R2.6 and **rewritten in R2.7** to the activation path (C26): it creates its owner by activating a member (`POST /customers/owners` with a `membership_id` read from C27) and uses that membership id as `owner_id` for the company, contact and deal. The owner runs it as the verify step after a deploy; agents never do |
| H8 | `tools/backfill_pii_indexes.py` (wrapper `remote.sh backfill-pii-indexes`) | owner-run CLI | SP3/R3.1 (built for `customer.contacts.email_index`), SP3/R3.7a (adds `--service lead`: `lead.leads.phone_index`, and `email_index` with collisions skipped and counted) | owner | new | `--service customer\|lead` (one child process per service) and `--target email_index\|phone_index`. `plan` (read-only, the default): per organization the count of rows whose index is `NULL` and whose source value is present. `apply` (gated like H1): computes `platform_core.pii.blind_index(...)` with the current key and writes **only the index column**; for the phone it opens `phone_enc` inside the service process (the plaintext is never printed, logged or stored), so the bulk PII read has an operator, a dry run and one audit row per organization naming the field and the count (`acting_as='backfill-pii-indexes-cli'`). Leaves `updated_at` alone, so C53's keyset does not see a mass update; stages no events; idempotent (rows already indexed are skipped); resumable with `--pace`. It is also the rebuild tool after a PII key rotation, which invalidates every stored index (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/pii.py:184-187`). **Order:** after the R3.1 deploy and before the R3.7a deploy (B10, B40). Needs the PII key inside the container, as H1 needs the service-token secret |
| H9 | `tools/sync_engage_contacts.py` (wrapper `remote.sh sync-engage-contacts`) | owner-run CLI | SP4/R4.2 | owner | new | The first link of existing Engage contacts to CRM contacts (B47), run inside notification's container (`--service notification`). `plan` (read-only, the default): per organization the CRM contacts that have an email, the Engage contacts matched by blind-index equality, the rows it would create, and the rows skipped (no email, erased). `apply` (gated like H1): pages C53 and writes `crm_contact_id` and `origin = 'crm'` on each match and creates a row for each unmatched CRM contact with an email; **writes no consent event and never touches `suppressions`**; idempotent through `uq_contact_org_crm_contact`; counts and ids only. After it, the EV46 triggers keep the projection current |
| H10 | `tools/backfill_owner_team.py` (wrapper `remote.sh backfill-owner-team`, target `make edge-backfill-owner-team`) | owner-run CLI | SP3/R3.8 (SP4/R4.7 extends it) | owner | new | Membership events cannot be replayed, so `owner_team` (B4) needs a bootstrap and a repair tool. `--service customer\|deal\|lead` (one child process per service), `--org <uuid> \| --all`, `--pace MS`. `plan` (read-only, the default) prints per organization the memberships account holds (C57), the `owner_team` rows the service holds, the drift between them and the number of rows whose `owner_manager_id` stamp would change. `apply` (the purge tools' three gates) records the time just **before** it reads account as the rows' `source_occurred_at`, pages C57 (`after_id`, `limit` 500; W17 to W19), upserts `owner_team`, then restamps every owned row set-based with `updated_at` untouched, one transaction per organization on the app role with the tenant bound, stopping at the first failing organization; idempotent, counts and ids only. A second `plan` shows zero drift. It is also the repair tool after any consumer outage. R4.7 extends it to write `owner_team.employee_id` (B71) and to count a row whose `employee_id` differs as drift; the owner re-runs `apply` once after the R4.7 deploy |
| H11 | `tools/link_support_tickets.py` | owner-run CLI | SP4/R4.4 | owner | new | Links the tickets that predate B49 to a contact by an unambiguous email, through C52; `plan` by default, a gated `apply` |
| H12 | `tools/backfill_campaign_attribution.py --service marketing` | owner-run CLI | SP4/R4.6-A | owner | new | The attribution backfill: pages C98 and C99 to fill B69, B70 and from them B53 for leads and deals created before marketing's consumer existed (events are not replayable, and the facts can be rebuilt from these exports); `plan` by default, a gated `apply` |
| H13 | `tools/backfill_rep_unavailability.py --service lead` | owner-run CLI | SP4/R4.7 | owner | new | The availability catch-up: pages C100 for approved leave that is already running or booked into B75 (`from` = today), before the HR producers change; `plan` by default, a gated `apply`. On a box without the `hrms` profile the call fails and availability stays empty |
| H14 | `tools/reapply_contact_erasures.py --service notification\|finance\|operations\|support\|deal\|lead\|storefront` | owner-run CLI | SP4 (each consumer's first deploy: R4.2 to R4.7) | owner | new | Re-applies earlier erasures on a consumer's first deploy, because a published `crm.book.contact.erased` cannot be replayed (EV18): it pages C53 with `erased_only=true` and applies the service's local treatment. One child process per `--service`, inside that service's own container (`import app` is ambiguous across services). **Gated like `tools/purge_test_orgs.py`:** `plan` is the read-only default and prints counts per organization; `apply` needs `--apply`, `--confirm-count N`, `--i-took-a-backup` and `--i-know-this-is-the-live-database`; one transaction per organization with the tenant bound; stop at the first failing organization; idempotent; counts only |

## I. Documents

ADR numbers are allocated **in order and never reused** (`docs/decisions.md:8-11`); the next free number today is
**0038**; `0027` (`inventory-scan-and-bulk-data`) and `0040` (`faster-ci`) are already claimed on other refs. **The slug is
binding and the number is the expected one, confirmed against `docs/decisions.md` when the file is written**; each new ADR
is also added to `docs/decisions.md` and links its register rows in the same change.

Builds on ADR-0000, 0007, 0008, 0015, 0017, 0020, 0021, 0022, 0035, 0036. Amends ADR-0017's limitation text (`:161-174,183-188`) |

## J. Amendments: where this registry resolves a conflict

These are the places where the release lists, the programme and the discovery digests disagreed or left a name open and
this registry decided, and (J25 to J33) where the four specs disagreed with one another or with the code. Each is binding until the owning SP amends it here. Items marked **release list** change what a
release lists; the rest only choose a name.

### J.1 Conflicts, decisions and corrections (J1 to J33)

| # | Amendment | Why | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| J1 | **Release list.** `deals.closed_at` is created in **R2.3**, not R3.1 (B18) | The committed deal schemas already *require* `closed_at` (`crm.pipeline.deal.created/v1.schema.json`), so R2.3's producer-side test cannot pass without a stored value, and R2.3 is the first release with a deal migration, which is where D-12's two-heads decision must be taken once. R3.1 consumes the column | R2.3 chain `e94d7a2c5f18` → `a1c9e2f7b3d4` (adopted unchanged) → stages; R3.1 loses the column |
| J2 | Stage classification is `outcome` (`open`, `won`, `lost`), never `kind` (B20, EV6-EV8, C7) | P§4.1; the digests said `kind` | Event fields `stage_outcome`, `from_outcome`, `to_outcome`; `DealOut.stage_outcome`; SPA `StageOutcome` |
| J3 | Link names outside CRM are `crm_company_id`, `crm_contact_id` everywhere (B41-B51), including support tickets | P§4.2 says "never `customer_id`"; the R4.4 text says `contact_id` and `company_id`; one digest said `client_company_id`; another used `contact_id` on `engage.inbox.conversation.touched` | R4.4's "contact_id and company_id" is shorthand for the `crm_*` pair |
| J4 | The rep role is `CRM_REP`, not `CRM_SALES_REP` (§E.2) | `CRM_SALES_REP` is one of the six frozen legacy codes (customer's); P§4.5 and D-20 name `CRM_REP` | New display names differ from the legacy "Sales representative"; legacy rep templates lose `is_default` for new activations only (§E.3) |
| J5 | The approval request type is `crm.pipeline.discount` (C66, EV23-EV24) | One digest said `crm.pipeline.quote_discount`; the approval grammar is `<product>.<module>.<thing>` (`approval/app/services.py:40-75`: `hrms.time.regularization`, `hrms.time.leave`) | `REQUEST_TYPE_CRM_DISCOUNT` |

The idempotency key of `leads:capture` is `external_ref` (B35) | A digest reused `source_ref` for the storefront enquiry id, and P§6's Marketing row introduced `source_ref` for the campaign link although the name already means "the id of the originating record, used as an idempotency key" in approval (`services/approval/app/models.py:263`), attendance (`services/attendance/app/consumers.py:155`) and finance (`services/finance/app/models.py:578-589`) | No overloaded column. **Amendment returned against P§6, Marketing row:** "`source_ref` and UTM on lead and deal" reads "`marketing_campaign_id` and UTM on lead and deal" |

## K. Decision defaults the registry used (programme §8)

D-1 to D-24 are the programme's (P§8). D-25 to D-34 were raised afterwards, by this registry (D-25), by SP2 (D-26) and by SP4 (D-27 to D-34, D-27 also cited by SP3), and are not in P§8 yet; each keeps the id the spec that raised it cites, and this registry invents none. Every one is open until the owner answers, and the defaults below are what the rows assume.

| ID | Default used here | Where it shows |
|---|---|---|
| D-1 | Soft-delete the orphan deal with an audit row naming the consumer; no `stage_changed` | EV9, F2, C55 |
| D-2 | Only records provably created by the aborted attempt (customer's own `company.created` / `contact.created` audit with `acting_as='lead'` and the id equal to the derived uuid5), no other contacts, no live deal, never a reused record; **log-only first** | F2, C55, EV13 |
| D-3 | A sweep is built only if the owner's read-only count is non-zero | H1 `census`, H3 |
| D-4 | Bump on every abort; an event for the deleted-record bump | EV13 (`record_deleted`) |
| D-5 | One pipeline per org, immutable key, editable label, order, tone, probability, outcome, at least one won and one lost, archive never delete, closed deals immutable | B19-B22, C1-C5 |
| D-6 | `crm.pipeline.stage.write`, ORG floor; read stays under `deal.read`; no new admin template | E18, C1-C5 |
| D-7 | `owners.membership_id`; the Team screen lists org members and activates them; free-form rep creation retires after cutover | B1, C25-C27 |
| D-8 | Email match as a reviewed plan, employee-link evidence for employee-valued rows, unmapped rows untouched and listed, never guessed | H1, B3 |
| D-9 | Approve the access widening with a security review of every OWN route before the flip | A11, A13; §E.2 (the reps' OWN rows) |
| D-10 | Owner runs the read-only census | H1 `census` |
| D-11 | Keep three processes; cost the fold in the ADR | I1 (the merge of lead into customer); F9 and EV45 are lead's **own** consumer fold, a different thing. B86, C94 and C104 on lead, EV45 and the lead handlers of R4.7 all assume lead stays its own process: a yes moves them into customer |
| D-12 | Stages land first; `a1c9e2f7b3d4` is adopted unchanged; insights rebases onto `outcome` | B18, EV6-EV8 |
| D-13 | The programme assumed no increase, but even deal's fold is +1 MB and this registry's five folds are +5 MB; the owner raises `EDGE_BUDGET_ACCEPTED_MB` by one per fold or frees memory (D-11). One fold takes ten profiles plus `admin` from 3944 to 3945 MB, above the owner's accepted ceiling, so D-13 first blocks **R2.4** and not R4.6 (P§8); insights (+99 MB), the unmeasured PDF library of R4.5-B and approval's move to core (F18) are priced in L17 | F8, F9, F15-F18, EV55, L17 |
| D-14 | Company is the party, contact the person, email nullable | B6-B11, C51 |
| D-15 | Engage contacts are a projection of CRM for CRM-entitled orgs, standalone kept | B47, C53, C84, G17, H9, EV46 |
| D-16 | Engage owns sending; Marketing owns planning and attribution and reads Engage | B53, B59, C85, EV51 |
| D-17 | Both project products get the same columns; the kickoff target follows the owner's pick (default: PulseBoard's `project` first, because ADR-0000 §11 already says the PulseBoard client id becomes the CRM customer id; `projects` adopts the same route name later) | B50, EV8, EV36, C64, C92 |
| D-18 | The approval engine is the one mechanism; requester abstraction (a membership id beside the employee id) and platform inbox | B66, C19, C66, C78, G14, EV23, EV50, EV52, F18; the ADR `approvals-for-tenants-without-hrms` (I2) gates the approval deploy |
| D-19 | Quotes in deal; `inventory_items` the one item master | B28, B29, B52, C80, C87, C93 |
| D-20 | Four role codes; legacy and the 17 codes frozen | §E.2, §E.3 |
| D-21 | Plans stay commercial-only and flat | no interface: a staff action in the admin console on the per-seat `crm/growth` plan (retire or fix it: ADR-0035's open question, `docs/adr/0035-list-prices-2026-10-06.md:40, 167-168`). The programme makes it block R2.8; nothing in this registry changes |
| D-22 | `/companies` canonical, 360 under `/contacts/{id}/360`, `/activities` aliased | C21, C23, C17, C67, C68 |
| D-23 | Replace, then delete | G15 |
| D-24 | TEAM from `manager_membership_id` through a per-service projection | A8, A9, A14, B4, B5, EV29, EV45, F9 |
| D-25 (raised by this registry, not in P§8) | Managers reveal the sealed phone or email of their whole team's records (TEAM `read_sensitive`): default **no**, held at OWN | §E.2 ‡, L20 |
| D-26 (raised by SP2, not in P§8) | Seal `contacts.email` at rest (SP2 R2.9 Part C): default **no**; Part C is built only on a yes and only when its gates hold. The plaintext readers C45, C47 and C53 are consciously left on the plaintext column; on a yes each must be sealed-aware first, which reopens R4.1 and R4.2 | B56, B10, L16 |
| D-27 (raised by SP4, cited by SP3, not in P§8) | Erase retention exceptions: what each store keeps after `crm.book.contact.erased` (finance documents, ticket and message bodies, consent evidence, audit rows, deal text), **and what the erase of a lead keeps**. Default: identifiers are overwritten everywhere a store keys a row by the contact id, and legal documents (invoices), consent-ledger rows, suppressions, free-text bodies and append-only deal text are retained and listed in the ADR's coverage table. A lead is erased by an administrator, one lead at a time, in place (C108): name, company, email, phone, notes, the visitor's messages, GSTIN and duplicate pointers are cleared and its audit copies rewritten, while source, status, owner, attribution, the consent evidence (it holds no identifier) and the ids of what the lead converted into stay. The owner and legal confirm each exception | I2, B12, B86, C29, C108, EV18, L19, L23 |
| D-28 (raised by SP4, not in P§8) | Accounting for an online refund of a captured invoice or order payment: **record only**. Finance stores the refund fact on `online_captures`, writes an audit row and logs it; it creates no credit note and no negative allocation, and a person issues the credit note | B82, C103, C105 |
| D-29 (raised by SP4, not in P§8) | The fulfilment billing settings surface: R4.3 includes the minimal slice R4.3-S (`GET` and `PUT` of the organization-default row, one new permission, one card on the existing payment-settings screen), used by the organization's own administrator. The alternative is to ship the chain dark and set rows by owner SQL | C102, E27, F13, F19 |
| D-30 (raised by SP4, not in P§8) | A customer-facing pay page ("payment link") for invoices: **defer**. No public payment surface exists, and R4.5-C ships staff-assisted collection | C103, G25, EV38; replaces the payment links of C20 |
| D-31 (raised by SP4, not in P§8) | Marketing opt-in captured on a public form (the storefront buyer form, and by the same rule a lead capture form): **single opt-in recorded with evidence**, every marketing message carrying the unsubscribe link; a confirmed opt-in (a confirmation email whose link writes the `opted_in` event) is deferred | B39, B77, C58 |
| D-32 (raised by SP4, not in P§8) | Insights v2, company-keyed cross-product facts: **design only**, built when a dashboard needs a company-ranked board that the party projection of R4.3 cannot serve | I12, B72 to B74 |
| D-33 (raised by SP4, not in P§8) | One stated rule for STOP and unsubscribe: **both opt out of marketing only**, which is what the code does; a transactional opt-out is a separate, explicit act. R4.1 corrects the two stale texts, with no behaviour change | I2, I11 |
| D-34 (raised by SP4, not in P§8) | Lifecycle automation: whether CRM may promote a company from `prospect` or `lead` to `customer` on a commercial event. Default: a per-organization setting, **off** (`first_order` promotes on the first accepted order only, the one trigger P§6 names; `first_commercial_event` also promotes on the first won deal and the first paid invoice and is the owner's explicit extension) | EV2, B81, B83, C106 |

## L. Open interface questions (a name cannot settle these)

| # | Question | Registered default or the cost of leaving it open |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | **Who owns a party created by `parties:ensure`?** `companies.owner_id` and `contacts.owner_id` are `NOT NULL` (`customer/app/models.py:118,216`) and after R2.7 must be an activated rep | Default (pending SP4/R4.1): `owner_membership_id` from the caller when given and activatable, else the organization's single active `is_owner` membership (C57 carries `is_owner`), activating its roster row if absent with an audit row; with no unique owner the route answers 422 `owner_required`. Without a rule R4.3's order-to-contact creation cannot ship |
| L2 | **Closed (SP1).** R1.2b stops computing `leads.phone_index` and R3.7a needs it for phone-based duplicate detection (B40) | The column is kept and mapped `deferred=True`; R3.7a resumes writing it at write time and its backfill is the owner-run tool H8. The contractive step (remove the attribute and drop the column, in one change) ships only if R3.7 does not resume the index. The row stays so the handle is stable |
| L3 | Notification's `account_internal_url` (`notification/app/config.py:91`) has no compose line (inference) | Not this programme's to fix; R2.6 uses the shared `ACCOUNT_SERVICE_URL` (F4) so customer does not repeat it. Worth one line in the R2.6 review |
| L4 | The glossary says a company goes `prospect → customer → dormant/churned`, but `CompanyStatus` has `lead` and no `dormant` (B17) | Values are frozen (they are in `crm.book.company.created/v1` and tenant rules); R1.1's glossary documents the real set and R3.x relabels in the UI |
| L5 | **Closed.** Does `lead` get a consumer (EV45)? | Yes, **unconditionally from R3.8** (F9): TEAM on leads (A14, B4, E.2 †, C37) needs lead's own `owner_team`, which only lead's own consumer can feed. `lead` becomes `api_with_worker`, `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES["crm"]` gains `lead.events`, and R4.7's round-robin availability and owner-deactivation hook join the same queue. The row stays so the handle is stable; the memory cost is L17 |
| L6 | **Confirmed (SP2, DD-8).** TEAM including own rows (J10) | SP2 implements A8 and A9 exactly as registered, `or_` included; the `EMPLOYEE_LINK` TEAM path is byte-for-byte unchanged. Nothing reaches TEAM until R3.8 wires `team_col` (A14), and the semantic is still pending D-24. Had SP2 refused it, SP3/R3.8 would have had to stamp `owner_manager_id` differently and the manager role would stay at OWN writes |
| L7 | Legacy `deal.activities.owner_id` after the flip (B3) | Untouched (append-only). A custom role that reads activities at OWN loses its historical rows; `CRM_REP`, `CRM_MANAGER` and `CRM_VIEWER` read at ORG, so the templates are unaffected. The owner confirms (pending D-8) |
| L8 | Whether Engage's scheduled sends already fail the ownership check on the box | Two separate acts. **A local test run (an agent):** an S-sized end-to-end run of a scheduled campaign against the real scheduler consumer (`scheduler/tests/test_consumer.py:158-170`) shows what the rule does to `engage.campaign.campaign.send_requested`. **On the box (the owner, a production read):** whether `scheduler.schedule_requested.rejected` has ever been logged with producer `notification`, which is the real evidence. R3.6 does not wait for either: A17 keeps the notification mapping out of this change, and the answer only decides whether the separate Engage fix is wanted |
| L9 | First-touch versus last-touch attribution (R4.6) | A product decision; the schema carries one `marketing_campaign_id` and one set of UTM columns per lead and per deal either way |
| L10 | Insights adoption | The unmerged `deal_facts` stage CHECK and literal `closed_won`/`closed_lost` predicates must move to `outcome` before custom stages are enabled in any org with insights (otherwise the fold dead-letters); SP4/R4.6-B moves them (B72); D-13's +99 MB also needs an owner answer, and insights ships only after it |
| L11 | Personal data in B7 and B8 for sole proprietors (PAN, address) and in `lead.leads.consent_evidence` | **PAN is settled here, not left to the ADR: it is not stored** (B7), so nothing is sealed or revealed. The address is still decided in the consent and erasure ADR (I2); the registered default keeps tax ids and addresses out of events and out of summaries beyond business facts. **If the owner later wants a standalone PAN** (a buyer with no GSTIN), this shape becomes binding: `value_enc BYTEA`, `value_masked VARCHAR` and `value_index VARCHAR(64)` (`platform_core.pii.seal` and `blind_index`, exactly as `contacts.phone`: `services/customer/app/models.py:176-206`; `pan` is already a masking kind, `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/pii.py:281`); a new dangerous code `crm.book.company.read_sensitive` (default OWN, max ORG, `amr={"otp", "stepup"}`, no ORG floor, an audit row per reveal through `record_sensitive_read`) as a new E-row; a reveal route `POST /companies/{company_id}/tax-ids/{tax_id}:reveal` mirroring `phone:reveal` (`services/customer/app/api/routes.py:423-424`, shared reveal budget) as a new C-row; and a rule that the plaintext never appears in an audit `after`, an event, a log or a summary |
| L12 | How `POST /leads/public/capture` learns that an organization's `crm` activation lapsed, with no principal and the gateway's entitlement gate skipped | **There is no working precedent to copy.** The storefront's entitlement-lapse consumer is unowned and unbuilt (`services/storefront/app/domain.py:715` says "still unowned", `services/storefront/app/consumers.py` has no handler, `suspended` is a reserved state: `services/storefront/app/models.py:73-80`). Registered default (pending `crm-public-lead-capture`, I2): lead reads the organization's entitlements from product-service's **existing** internal route (`GET /internal/v1/organizations/{id}/entitlements`, `services/product/app/api/internal.py:57-79`) at capture time, through the shared `PRODUCT_SERVICE_URL` (F12, W36) with a short in-process cache (60 s), and answers like an unknown endpoint when `crm` is not listed. It needs no consumer, so R3.7 does not wait for the lead fold of R3.8; the alternative, an activation table fed from `lead.events`, would make R3.7 carry that fold. Adopting that route adds callers (W36) and nothing else: it has **no caller allow-list** and answers for any organization on the service token alone (`services/product/app/api/internal.py:1-20`), and hardening it would touch auth's sign-in path, so that is flagged here for the owner and is not part of this programme |
| L13 | Permission for CRM-originated sends (C60): `engage.inbox.message.reply` versus a new CRM code | A cross-product code cannot live in a CRM role template (`cross_product` is refused by the exporter), so the rep needs an Engage role; SP4/R4.2 confirms |
| L14 | **Closed (SP2/R2.4, DD-49): no.** The e2e harness boots neither lead nor the CRM consumers and relays | The harness is not extended. NEW-9 is proven by schema-pinned producer and consumer tests that validate the same committed schema on both sides (the answer to docs/testing.md's warning about cross-service contracts), not by a multi-process broker journey for one near-zero-rate event. The row stays so the handle is stable |
| L15 | Multiple won or lost stages (B20) | Allowed (at least one of each); win-rate and `closed_at` count by outcome, so the rule is unambiguous |
| L16 | The final fate of `customer.owners` (R2.9) | This registry assumes the table stays: it is the roster that `owners:validate` and rep activation read, and no release in the programme drops it; R2.9 may retire only the one-click claim and the employee-id fallbacks |
| L17 | **Memory: an owner decision that amends D-13.** Each consumer fold is +1 MB (`kinds.api_with_worker` 99 against `kinds.api` 98): deal (R2.4), lead (R3.8), support (R4.4), marketing (R4.6), storefront (R4.7). All five take the ten profiles to 3752 MB (97.2% of the 3862 MB reference) and ten plus `admin` to 3949 MB (102.3%), against `EDGE_BUDGET_ACCEPTED_MB=3944` on the box; P§8 D-13 assumed no increase, which even deal's fold already breaks | Registered default: all five fold as registered (F8, F9, F15-F17) and the owner raises `EDGE_BUDGET_ACCEPTED_MB` by one per fold, release by release, or frees memory (D-11). **Alternatives** (SP4 took the three folds as registered and keeps these as the owner's options; each trades memory for cross-service calls): storefront derives the enquiry status by a read of lead and deal when it displays it instead of consuming EV12 (no EV49, no F17); support is reached by a customer-called internal route on erase instead of consuming EV18 (no EV48, no F15), which makes the erase depend on that call succeeding; marketing reads lead and deal counts instead of folding attribution (no EV51, no F16). Lead's fold has no alternative: TEAM scope needs it. **SP4 adds three more items to the same question:** the insights container (+99 MB, R4.6, which ships only after D-13 is answered); the PDF library that R4.5-B makes resident in deal and finance (not measured, inference +10 to +20 MB each, priced through the budget kind `api_with_worker_render` once measured, and R4.5-B waits for the owner to raise the ceiling to the figure the PR prints); and approval's move to core (F18, +99 MB on every box with `crm` and no `hrms`). With insights the ten profiles reach 3851 MB after all five folds, and 3871 to 3891 MB in the PDF worst case, which passes the box's 3862 MB MemTotal at +12 MB, so only a raised `EDGE_BUDGET_ACCEPTED_MB` covers it (the preflight cap is 125% of MemTotal, 4827 MB). Operations' queue entry (EV55) adds no budget line unless its measurement shows a delta above 1 MB |
| L18 | Should a rep **read** only their own records? (J19) | Registered default: no, reps read at ORG and write at OWN, which is today's behaviour (P§3 F2); P§1 criterion 1 is reworded. Narrowing reads to OWN would hide teammates' records from every rep's lists, boards and 360, a larger change than D-9 approves |
| L19 | **Notes attachments and erasure.** An attachment (B57) can hold personal data that an append-only, PII-free activity never did, so an erase must reach it | Default (pending `crm-consent-and-erasure`, I2): deal binds `crm.book.contact.erased` on `deal.events` **unconditionally** from R4.5 (it already folds, EV44, and the handler already clears the quote copies), so the attachment purge joins an existing handler: it has document delete the private objects of the erased contact's activities, the attachment row staying as a fact with its object gone; the ADR also decides whether `original_name` is stored at all. **Until the ADR chooses, R4.5 ships the PDF rows (B28, B55, C18, C74) but not B57, C81, C82 and C83** |
| L20 | **D-25 (raised by this registry): may managers reveal the sealed phone or email of their whole team's records** (TEAM `read_sensitive`)? | Default **no**: `CRM_MANAGER` holds `crm.book.contact.read_sensitive` and `crm.lead.lead.read_sensitive` at OWN, the rep-equivalent (§E.2 ‡). P§8 D-9 approves only the reps' own rows and D-20 fixes names, not grants. If the owner says yes, the cells become TEAM (they already ride the R3.8 widening) and the security review of D-9 covers them |
| L21 | Who may **hand a quote off** to finance, operations or projects (C20)? | Default: no new code; the ORG floor on `crm.pipeline.quote.write` for `order` and `invoice`, plus the callee's entitlement re-check. A dedicated `crm.pipeline.quote.handoff` (a new dangerous E-row, default OWN, max ORG) would let a tenant grant it deliberately to managers or reps; SP4/R4.5 confirms with the owner |
| L22 | Should `PATCH` of a closed deal's value, currency, probability and close date be refused (D-5's "closed deals immutable")? | Default: **no server change in this programme.** D-5's rule is implemented as the no-move rule that SP2 owns (a closed deal moves nowhere: 409), and SP3/R3.5's deal page is read-only for a closed deal; a stricter reading changes the behaviour of live bulk-update and import |
| L23 | **What does the CRM owe a person whose details an anonymous form, a signed webhook or a WhatsApp message put in a lead, and how is junk disposed of?** (pending D-27) | Default: an administrator's per-lead erase on E22 (C108), no bulk erase, and junk disqualified in bulk through ADR-0022's engine. Alternatives: a dedicated `crm.lead.lead.erase` code (needs SP2's catalogue and a reconcile, like E24), or a bulk erase. SP3/R3.7a ships the erase before R3.7b can store a stranger's details, and R3.7b does not ship before the owner has answered D-27 for leads |

## M. Re-verifying this registry at write time

Every row marked `exists` is a claim about HEAD. Before a spec or plan cites a row, re-run the check; a mismatch is a registry
amendment, not a local rename.

| Check | Command or place |
|---|---|
| Alembic heads | per service, `revision`/`down_revision` pairs in `services/{customer,deal,lead}/migrations/versions` (customer `e230c025337e`, deal `e94d7a2c5f18`, lead `91dd3ca663fd`); `tests/infra/test_migration_revisions.py` |
| Scope API | `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/auth.py` symbols `ScopeResolver`, `register_scope_resolver`, `ScopeContext.apply`, `.assert_can_touch`, `require`; line numbers move when `fix/ci-failures` (`40e5c881`) merges |
| Permission set | `python tools/export_permissions.py --check` and `libs/contracts/permissions/crm.json` (17 codes, six role templates, `sources: customer, deal, lead` today) |
| Event contracts | `ls libs/contracts/events` (77 directories at HEAD, 11 of them `crm.*`; no `crm.book.contact.*`, no `crm.pipeline.activity.logged`, no `core.membership.*`, no `core.approval.*`, no `finance.billing.customer.*`, no `operations.orders.order.*` but `invoice_requested`) and `make contracts-check`. **After R1.2b** it shows 81 directories, 15 of them `crm.*`, and the "no `crm.book.contact.*`, no `crm.pipeline.activity.logged`" clause no longer holds; each later release re-counts from there |
| Event envelope | `build_envelope` (`libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/events.py:173-192`) puts `organization_id` on the wire (`:179`); ADR-0000 §7's `org_id` is the pre-implementation name, and consumers read `envelope["organization_id"]` |
| Audit append-only grant | `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/db.py:140-146` (`REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON audit_logs FROM <app role>`): why B12 and B86 are `SECURITY DEFINER` functions |
| HR exit | `services/account/app/consumers.py:192-194` binds product-activation events only, and `core.membership.revoked` is staged only in `revoke_member` (`services/account/app/services.py:920-970`): an HR exit does not revoke a membership (EV25) |
| Watched queues | `libs/platform-core/src/platform_core/ops_monitor/config.py:63-109` (`CORE_DLQ_QUEUES`, `PROFILE_API_CONTAINERS`, `PROFILE_DLQ_QUEUES`) against compose's `./bin/worker` services; `operations.events` is live (`services/operations/app/consumers.py:81`) and unlisted until R4.3 (EV55) |
| Token baseline | `frontend/opsgrids-config/token-baseline.json`: `product-crm` has 101 offenders in 15 files at HEAD, 97 in 14 after R1.2b |
| Next ADR number | `docs/decisions.md` header and `git log --all --diff-filter=A -- 'docs/adr/00[34]*'` |
| Unmerged insights stack | `git ls-tree` of `sprint/insights-q2` for `services/deal/migrations/versions/a1c9e2f7b3d4_add_closed_at.py`; its producer changes are commit `a4b248f1` |
| Memory arithmetic | `infrastructure/edge/lib/memory_budget_totals.py` against `memory-budget.json` (ten profiles 3747 MB, ten plus `admin` 3944 MB, reference 3862 MB; five folds make 3752 and 3949, F8). `api_with_worker` today: account, product, subscription, payment, notification, approval, attendance, leave, customer, finance, console; deal, lead, support, marketing and storefront are still `api`. These figures do not include approval's move to core (F18, +99 MB on a box with `crm` and no `hrms`), insights (+99 MB) or the unmeasured PDF library (L17) |
| Wiring | `infrastructure/edge/compose.prod.yml` environment lines against W1-W52 (§F.1) and `tests/infra/test_party_link_env_wiring.py`; the callee's profile in `memory-budget.json` |
| Consumer scaffolds | `services/{support,storefront,marketing,project,projects,deal}/app/consumers.py` hold an `EventConsumer` and no handler (`grep -c "consumer.on"`), and `services/lead` has no `consumers.py` at all |
| Gateway | `services/app-gateway/routes.json`, `infrastructure/edge/routes.prod.json`, `services/app-gateway/app/main.py` constants `STOREFRONT_PUBLIC_PATHS`, `WEBHOOK_PUBLIC_PATHS` |
| Frontend walls | `frontend/.dependency-cruiser.cjs` rules `ui-imports-no-client`, `client-imports-no-ui`, `ui-only-via-exports` |

| R2.7 | A7 (the three CRM docstrings), A11 (opt-in set), A13, B3 (cutover), C49, C76, G16 (`mem`, `getMySubjectRef()` and the member dialog), H1 (run by the owner), H7 (including the rewrite of `e2e/edge_product_crud.py:337-350`, C26) |
